In this case, 2MLR degenerates to a linear regression model, and EM update rules also guarantee exact recovery of mixing weights in the noiseless setting.

When the ground truth mixing weights
\pi^{\ast}=(\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})
are balanced,
we have
p(\varepsilon,\pi^{\ast})\to\varepsilon\left\|\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}+\min(\pi^{\ast}(1),\pi^{\ast}(2))\asymp\varepsilon
,
hence the final accuracy of mixing weights is on the order of
\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\log\text{\scriptsize$\tfrac{1}{\delta}$}/n})
,
which is independent of the dimension
d
of the regression parameters.
This result strengthens Theorem 5.1 in the conference version of our paper
[
1
]
,
as it demonstrates the exact recovery of regression parameters and characterizes the dependence of the final accuracy of mixing weights on the ground truth mixing weights
\pi^{\ast}

through the newly established bound on the statistical error of mixing weights in Proposition
18
.

\theta^{\ast}
and

\phi^{0}=1.4
(equivalently,
\varphi^{0}=(\pi-1.4)/2
) in (a) and

in (b).

.

In this section of numerical experiments, we confirm the theoretical results established in the previous sections.
A total of 5,000 independently and identically distributed (i.i.d.)

d
-dimensional covariates, denoted by
\{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, are sampled from a normal distribution
\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
.
The ground truth parameters
\theta^{\ast}
are selected at random from a
d
-dimensional unit sphere.
Then, the ground truth mixture weights
\pi^{\ast}
for these two components are specified manually or randomly.
We use
\pi^{\ast}
to generate latent variable samples
\{z_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
from a categorical distribution
\mathcal{CAT}(\pi^{\ast})
.
Gaussian noise
\varepsilon_{i}
is then added to the linear regression
(-1)^{z_{i}+1}\langle\theta^{\ast},x_{i}\rangle
indicated by these latent variables,
resulting in output response samples
\{y_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
.
In all experiments, the full dataset is used for EM updates at each iteration.
Every point in the plots of Fig.
3
represents an average over 50 runs with different initialization values for EM updates.
The code for empirical experiments is available at
https://github.com/dassein/cycloid_em_tit
.

Cycloid Trajectory of Regression Parameters.

At the population level, we demonstrate that the output of the
t
-th iteration lies on the cycloid in the space
\text{span}\{\theta^{t-1},\theta^{\ast}\}
in the noiseless setting.
For the corresponding experiments, we set the signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) to
10^{8}
and examine different dimensions
d
(2, 3, and 50).
As shown in Fig.
2
, all iterations remain close to the theoretical cycloid.
Therefore, the empirical results confirm our theoretical findings in Proposition
11
.

Super-linear Convergence of Sub-Optimality Angle.

We demonstrate the quadratic convergence of
\phi^{t}/\pi
in Fig.
3(a)
in high SNR regime, when
\phi^{t}
is large enough.
The experiments are conducted in the dimension (
d
=50), considering different high SNR values (
10^{4},10^{5},10^{6}
).
The initial values of the first component of mixing weights and the regression parameters are drawn uniformly from an interval
[0,1]
and a unit sphere, respectively.
Each point corresponding to one of 4 EM iterations in Fig.
3(a)
represents an average over 50 independent runs with different initializations.
The slope of the plot shows the convergence rate exponent, and the slopes observed at various SNR levels consistently remain close to 2 when
\phi^{t}
is large enough.
That observation is consistent with our theoretical result establishing a quadratic convergence rate in Proposition
13
,
and aligns with our analysis in the remark of Proposition
19
.

Accuracy of EM Estimate for Mixing Weights and Sub-optimality Angle.

In the noiseless setting, we show that the accuracy of EM estimate for mixing weights
\|\pi^{t}-\bar{\pi}^{*}\|_{1}
is proportional to the sub-optimality angle

\phi^{t-1}
at population level in Proposition
14
.
We illustrate the linear correlation between the accuracy of EM estimate for mixing weights and the sub-optimality angle in Fig.
3(b)
.
For the experimental setup, we set the dimension at
d=50
, and examine different choices of ground truth mixing weights
\pi^{\ast}=(0.6,0.4),(0.8,0.2)
, and
(1,0)
.
It is observed that the accuracy of EM estimate for mixing weights at
t
-th iteration is exactly characterized by the sub-optimality angle
\phi^{t-1}
from the preceding iteration.
Therefore, our empirical results confirm Proposition
14
, which is consistent with our analysis in the remark of Proposition
19
.

Finite-Sample Level Convergence with Different Mixing Weights.

In the noiseless setting, Corollary
8
and Proposition
10
establish that the EM update for regression parameters
\theta^{t}
is unaffected by the ground truth mixing weights
\pi^{\ast}
.
The first subplot of Fig.
3(c)
shows that, at a high SNR (
10^{6}
), the accuracy of normalized regression parameters(measured in
\ell_{2}
norm) remains nearly unchanged across different choices of ground truth mixing weights
\pi^{\ast}=(0.5,0.5),(0.7,0.3),(0.8,0.2)
, and
(1-10^{-6},10^{-6})
, thereby confirming our theoretical analysis
in Proposition
14
.

Theorem
22
further proves that the final accuracy of EM estimate for mixing weights (in
\ell_{1}
norm) depends on both the accuracy of normalized regression parametersand the ground truth mixing weights.
In particular, when the accuracy of normalized regression parametersis small, the closer the ground truth mixing weights are to
(1,0)
or
(0,1)
, the smaller the final accuracy of EM estimate for mixing weights.
To verify this result, we investigate statistical accuracy of EM estimate for regression parameters
\theta
(measured in
\ell_{2}
norm) and mixing weights
\pi
(measured in
\ell_{1}
norm) under different ground truth mixing weights
\pi^{\ast}=(0.5,0.5),(0.7,0.3),(0.8,0.2)
, and
(1-10^{-6},10^{-6})
.
The second subplot of Fig.
3(c)
depicts the dependence of the statistical accuracy of EM estimate for mixing weights on
\pi^{\ast}
, providing further support for Theorem
22
.
In our experiments,
\theta^{\ast}
and
\theta^{0}
are drawn from the
50
-dimensional unit sphere with initial sub-optimality angle
\varphi^{0}=0.3
.

VI
Conclusion

We derive explicit expressions for the EM updates in the two-component Mixed Linear Regression (2MLR) model
with unknown mixing weights and regression parameters across all SNR regimes.
We then characterize the properties of EM updates based on the explicit expressions, establishing their structural behavior and boundedness,
and showing that in the noiseless setting, they follow a cycloid trajectory derived via a recurrence relation for the sub-optimality angle.
In finite high-SNR regimes, we further bound the deviation of the EM updates from this cycloid trajectory.
At the population level, the trajectory-based analysis reveals the order of convergence: linear convergence when the EM estimate is nearly orthogonal to the ground truth regression parameters,
and quadratic convergence when the angle between the estimate and the ground truth is small.
Furthermore, our work provides a novel trajectory-based framework that establishes non-asymptotic guarantees by tightening bounds of the statistical errors between the finite-sample and population EM updates,
revealing the connection between EM’s statistical accuracy and the sub-optimality angle,
and establishing convergence guarantees with arbitrary initialization at the finite-sample level.

Appendix A
Explicit EM Update Expressions and Properties of EM Update Rules

A-A

Connection between EM Update Rules and Gradient Descent of Negative Log-Likelihood

Theorem A.1

(Proposition
1
: Population and Finite-Sample Negative Log-Likelihood)
.

Let
f(\theta,\pi):=-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}[\ln p(s\mid\theta,\pi)]
be the negative log-likelihood function at the population level,
and
f_{n}(\theta,\pi):=-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ln p(s_{i}\mid\theta,\pi)
be the negative log-likelihood function at the finite-sample level for the dataset
\mathcal{S}=\{s_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}=\{(x_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
of
n
i.i.d. samples.
Then,
f(\theta,\pi)
and
f_{n}(\theta,\pi)
can be expressed as:

\displaystyle f(\theta,\pi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\langle\theta,\mathbb{E}[xx^{\top}]\cdot\theta\rangle-\mathbb{E}\left[\ln\frac{\cosh\left(y\langle x,\theta\rangle/\sigma^{2}+\nu\right)}{\cosh\nu}\right]+\mathtt{C}

\displaystyle f_{n}(\theta,\pi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\left\langle\theta,\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}x_{i}^{\top}\cdot\theta\right\rangle-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ln\frac{\cosh\left(y_{i}\langle x_{i},\theta\rangle/\sigma^{2}+\nu\right)}{\cosh\nu}+\mathtt{C}_{n}

where
\mathbb{E}[\cdot]=\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}[\cdot]
is the expectation over the ground truth distribution
p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})
, and
\mathtt{C}
and
\mathtt{C}_{n}
are constants that are independent of
\theta
and
\pi
.

Proof.

By the detailed derivation of negative log-likelihood in Appendix
D
(which is adapted from pages 20-25, Appendix B of
[
1
]
),
we have shown the above expressions for
f(\theta,\pi)
and
f_{n}(\theta,\pi)
with these constants
\mathtt{C}
and
\mathtt{C}_{n}
.

\displaystyle\mathtt{C}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\mathbb{E}[y^{2}]-\mathbb{E}[\ln p(x)]-\frac{1}{2}\ln\left(2\pi\sigma^{2}\right)\overset{x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}{=}\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}(\|\theta^{\ast}\|^{2}+\sigma^{2})-\frac{d}{2}(1+\ln(2\pi))-\frac{1}{2}\ln\left(2\pi\sigma^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\mathtt{C}_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}y_{i}^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ln p(x_{i})-\frac{1}{2}\ln\left(2\pi\sigma^{2}\right)

∎

Theorem A.2

(Lemma
2
: EM Update Rules and Gradients)
.

Let
U(\theta,\nu):=\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\ln\cosh\left(y\langle x,\theta\rangle/\sigma^{2}+\nu\right)
at population level and
U_{n}(\theta,\nu):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ln\cosh\left(y_{i}\langle x_{i},\theta\rangle/\sigma^{2}+\nu\right)
at finite-sample level,
then the population EM update rules
M(\theta,\nu),N(\theta,\nu)
and finite-sample EM update rules
M_{n}(\theta,\nu),N_{n}(\theta,\nu)
for regression parameters
\theta
and imbalance of mixing weights
\tanh\nu
are:

\displaystyle M(\theta,\nu)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[xx^{\top}]^{-1}\sigma^{2}\nabla_{\theta}U(\theta,\nu),\quad

\displaystyle N(\theta,\nu)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\nu}U(\theta,\nu),

\displaystyle M_{n}(\theta,\nu)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}x_{i}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\sigma^{2}\nabla_{\theta}U_{n}(\theta,\nu),\quad

\displaystyle N_{n}(\theta,\nu)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\nu}U_{n}(\theta,\nu).

Proof.

By the derivation of EM update rules in Appendix
D
(which is adapted from pages 20-25, Appendix B of
[
1
]
),
and the Leibniz rule such that
\nabla_{\theta}\mathbb{E}[\ln\cosh(\cdot)]=\mathbb{E}[\nabla_{\theta}\ln\cosh(\cdot)],\nabla_{\nu}\mathbb{E}[\ln\cosh(\cdot)]=\mathbb{E}[\nabla_{\nu}\ln\cosh(\cdot)]
and
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\ln\cosh(t)=\tanh(t)
, we have the population EM update rules as follows, and it is worth noting that
\mathbb{E}[xx^{\top}]=I_{d}
for
x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
:

\displaystyle M(\theta,\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[xx^{\top}]^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)yx=\mathbb{E}[xx^{\top}]^{-1}\nabla_{\theta}U(\theta,\nu)=\mathbb{E}[xx^{\top}]^{-1}\sigma^{2}\nabla_{\theta}U(\theta,\nu)

\displaystyle N(\theta,\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)=\nabla_{\nu}U(\theta,\nu)

Similarly, we can establish the results for the finite-sample EM update rules by substituting
f_{n},U_{n}
for
f,U
and
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n},s_{i}:=(x_{i},y_{i})
for
\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})},s:=(x,y)
into the above expressions.
∎

Theorem A.3

(Proposition
3
: Connection between EM and Gradient Descent)
.

The population/finite-sample EM update rules give:

\displaystyle M(\theta,\nu)

\displaystyle=\theta-\mathbb{E}[xx^{\top}]^{-1}\sigma^{2}\nabla_{\theta}f(\theta,\pi),\quad

\displaystyle N(\theta,\nu)

\displaystyle=\tanh\nu-\nabla_{\nu}f(\theta,\pi),

\displaystyle M_{n}(\theta,\nu)

\displaystyle=\theta-\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}x_{i}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\sigma^{2}\nabla_{\theta}f_{n}(\theta,\pi),\quad

\displaystyle N_{n}(\theta,\nu)

\displaystyle=\tanh\nu-\nabla_{\nu}f_{n}(\theta,\pi).

Proof.

Noticing that
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\ln\cosh(t)=\tanh(t)
and the direct computation of the gradients of the negative log-likelihood function
f(\theta,\pi),f_{n}(\theta,\pi)
with respect to
\theta
and
\nu
and comparing with the EM update rules
M(\theta,\nu),N(\theta,\nu)
and
M_{n}(\theta,\nu),N_{n}(\theta,\nu)
gives the results.
It is worth noting that
\mathbb{E}[xx^{\top}]=I_{d}
for the random variable
x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
.
∎

A-B

Explict EM Update Expressions

Theorem A.4

(Theorem
4
: Explict EM Update Expressions)
.

Let
A_{\eta}=k\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}},k=\frac{\|\theta\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|},\eta=\frac{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}{\sigma}
,
and
\varphi=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho|\in(0,\pi/2]
,
\rho=\frac{\langle\theta,\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta\|\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
,
and
\varphi_{\eta}=\arcsin(\sin\varphi/\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}})
, namely
\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}=\sin\varphi/\sin\varphi_{\eta}
,
and Gaussian random variables
g_{\eta},g^{\prime}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
with
\mathbb{E}[g_{\eta}g^{\prime}]=\sin\varphi_{\eta}
, then population EM update rules for regression parameters
\theta
and imbalance of mixing weights
\tanh\nu
are:

\displaystyle M(\theta,\nu)/\|\theta^{\ast}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{e}_{1}\frac{\sin\varphi}{\sin\varphi_{\eta}}\mathbb{E}[\tanh\left(A_{\eta}g_{\eta}g^{\prime}+\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)(-1)^{z+1}\nu\right)g_{\eta}g^{\prime}]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\vec{e}_{2}\frac{\cos\varphi}{\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}}\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)\mathbb{E}[\tanh\left(A_{\eta}g_{\eta}g^{\prime}+\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)(-1)^{z+1}\nu\right)g_{\eta}(g_{\eta}-\sin\varphi_{\eta}\cdot g^{\prime})]

\displaystyle N(\theta,\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left((-1)^{z+1}A_{\eta}g_{\eta}g^{\prime}+\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)\nu\right)\right].

Proof.

Let’s start from the EM update rules,

M(\theta,\nu)=\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)yx\right],N(\theta,\nu)=\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\right]
,
also let
k:=\frac{\|\theta\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
,
\eta:=\frac{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}{\sigma}
,
Z_{\varepsilon}=(-1)^{z+1}\varepsilon/\sigma\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, and denote cosine of angle

\rho:=\frac{\langle\theta,\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
, a pair of orthonormal vectors
\vec{e}_{1}:=\theta/\|\theta\|,\vec{e}_{2}=\frac{\theta^{\ast}-\langle\theta^{\ast},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\vec{e}_{1}}{\|\theta^{\ast}-\langle\theta^{\ast},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\vec{e}_{1}\|}
.
By introducing
Z=\langle x,\vec{e}_{1}\rangle,Z_{\ast}=\langle x,\vec{e}_{2}\rangle
, and
x^{\perp}=x-Z\vec{e}_{1}-Z_{\ast}\vec{e}_{2}
, then:

\displaystyle y/\|\theta^{\ast}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{z+1}[\rho Z+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}Z_{\ast}+\eta^{-1}\cdot Z_{\varepsilon}]

\displaystyle y\langle x,\theta\rangle/\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\|\theta^{\ast}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2})\times(y\langle x,\theta\rangle/\|\theta^{\ast}\|^{2})=\eta^{2}\times(-1)^{z+1}[\rho Z+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}Z_{\ast}+\eta^{-1}\cdot Z_{\varepsilon}]\cdot kZ

\displaystyle yx/\|\theta^{\ast}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{z+1}[\rho Z+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}Z_{\ast}+\eta^{-1}\cdot Z_{\varepsilon}]\cdot[Z\vec{e}_{1}+Z_{\ast}\vec{e}_{2}+x^{\perp}]

Therefore, by using the independence of
Z,Z_{\ast},Z_{\varepsilon}
and

x^{\perp}
, then

\displaystyle M(\theta,\nu)/\|\theta^{\ast}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{e}_{1}\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left(k\eta^{2}\left[\rho Z+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}Z_{\ast}+\eta^{-1}\cdot Z_{\varepsilon}\right]Z+(-1)^{z+1}\nu\right)\left[\rho Z+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}Z_{\ast}+\eta^{-1}\cdot Z_{\varepsilon}\right]Z\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\vec{e}_{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left(k\eta^{2}\left[\rho Z+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}Z_{\ast}+\eta^{-1}\cdot Z_{\varepsilon}\right]Z+(-1)^{z+1}\nu\right)\left[\rho Z+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}Z_{\ast}+\eta^{-1}\cdot Z_{\varepsilon}\right]Z_{\ast}\right]

\displaystyle N(\theta,\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left((-1)^{z+1}k\eta^{2}\left[\rho Z+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}Z_{\ast}+\eta^{-1}\cdot Z_{\varepsilon}\right]Z+\nu\right)\right]

By introducing
Z^{\prime}:=(\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}Z_{\ast}+\eta^{-1}\cdot Z_{\varepsilon})/\sqrt{(1-\rho^{2})+\eta^{-2}}
, then

\left(Z_{\ast}-(\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}/\sqrt{(1-\rho^{2})+\eta^{-2}})Z^{\prime}\right)\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}Z^{\prime},Z
:

\displaystyle M(\theta,\nu)/\|\theta^{\ast}\|=\vec{e}_{1}\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left(k\eta^{2}\left[\rho Z+\sqrt{(1-\rho^{2})+\eta^{-2}}Z^{\prime}\right]Z+(-1)^{z+1}\nu\right)\left[\rho Z+\sqrt{(1-\rho^{2})+\eta^{-2}}Z^{\prime}\right]Z\right]

\displaystyle+\vec{e}_{2}\frac{\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}}{\sqrt{(1-\rho^{2})+\eta^{-2}}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left(k\eta^{2}\left[\rho Z+\sqrt{(1-\rho^{2})+\eta^{-2}}Z^{\prime}\right]Z+(-1)^{z+1}\nu\right)\left[\rho Z+\sqrt{(1-\rho^{2})+\eta^{-2}}Z^{\prime}\right]Z^{\prime}\right]

\displaystyle N(\theta,\nu)=\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left((-1)^{z+1}k\eta^{2}\left[\rho Z+\sqrt{(1-\rho^{2})+\eta^{-2}}Z^{\prime}\right]Z+\nu\right)\right]

If
\rho\neq 0
, then
\varphi=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho|\in(0,\pi/2]
, therefore
\rho=\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)\sin\varphi,\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}=\cos\varphi
, we define
A_{\eta}=k\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}
and
\varphi_{\eta}:=\arcsin(\sin\varphi/\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}})
, namely
\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}=\sin\varphi/\sin\varphi_{\eta}
.
By introducing
g^{\prime}\equiv Z^{\prime\prime}:=\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)

and
g:=(\rho Z+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}Z^{\prime})\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1),g_{\eta}:=(\rho Z+\sqrt{(1-\rho^{2})+\eta^{-2}}Z^{\prime})/\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}
with
\mathbb{E}[gg^{\prime}]=\sin\varphi,\mathbb{E}[g_{\eta}g^{\prime}]=\sin\varphi_{\eta}
:

\displaystyle M(\theta,\nu)/\|\theta^{\ast}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{e}_{1}\frac{\sin\varphi}{\sin\varphi_{\eta}}\mathbb{E}[\tanh\left(A_{\eta}g_{\eta}g^{\prime}+\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)(-1)^{z+1}\nu\right)g_{\eta}g^{\prime}]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\vec{e}_{2}\frac{\cos\varphi}{\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}}\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)\mathbb{E}[\tanh\left(A_{\eta}g_{\eta}g^{\prime}+\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)(-1)^{z+1}\nu\right)g_{\eta}(g_{\eta}-\sin\varphi_{\eta}\cdot g^{\prime})]

\displaystyle N(\theta,\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left((-1)^{z+1}A_{\eta}g_{\eta}g^{\prime}+\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)\nu\right)\right]

If
\rho=0
, then
\varphi=0,\varphi_{\eta}=0,\lim_{\varphi\to 0}\sin\varphi_{\eta}/\sin\varphi=1/\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}},\cos\varphi=\cos\varphi_{\eta}=1,A_{\eta}=k\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}
and
g=g^{\prime}=g_{\eta}
with
\mathbb{E}[gg^{\prime}]=\mathbb{E}[g_{\eta}g^{\prime}]=0
.
Therefore, the product
(-1)^{z+1}g_{\eta}g^{\prime}
of two independent standard normal random variables
g_{\eta},g^{\prime}
has a density involving Bessel function
X=(-1)^{z+1}g_{\eta}g^{\prime}\sim f_{X}(x)=K_{0}(|x|)/\pi
(see page 50, Section 4.4 Bessel Function Distributions, Chapter 12 Continuous Distributions (General) of
[
49
]
),

\displaystyle M(\theta,\nu)/\|\theta^{\ast}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{e}_{1}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}\mathbb{E}[\tanh\left(A_{\eta}X+\nu\right)X]+\vec{e}_{2}\mathbb{E}[\tanh\left(A_{\eta}g_{\eta}g^{\prime}+(-1)^{z+1}\nu\right)g_{\eta}^{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{e}_{1}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}\mathbb{E}[\tanh\left(A_{\eta}X+\nu\right)X]+\vec{e}_{2}\mathbb{E}[(-1)^{z+1}]\mathbb{E}[\tanh\left(A_{\eta}g_{\eta}g^{\prime}+\nu\right)g_{\eta}^{2}]

\displaystyle N(\theta,\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left(A_{\eta}X+\nu\right)\right]

The above expressions when
\rho=0
also align with the expressions when
\rho\neq 0
with the convention of
\textnormal{sgn}(0)=1
and
(\sin\varphi_{\eta}/\sin\varphi)_{\rho=0}=\lim_{\varphi\to 0}\sin\varphi_{\eta}/\sin\varphi=1/\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}
.
∎

A-C

Bound of the EM Update Rule in General SNR Regime

Theorem A.5

(Proposition
5
: Boundedness of EM Update Rule)
.

The EM update rule for
\theta
is bounded by the following bound, which depends on SNR
\eta=\|\theta^{\ast}\|/\sigma
:

\left\|M(\theta,\nu)\right\|\leq\frac{\arctan\eta}{\pi/2}\|\theta^{\ast}\|+\frac{2}{\pi}\sigma.

Proof.

Since we can express
g^{\prime}=\sin\varphi_{\eta}g_{\eta}+\cos\varphi_{\eta}h_{\eta}
for some
h_{\eta}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
with
h_{\eta}\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}g_{\eta}
,
noting that
\mathbb{E}[|g_{\eta}h_{\eta}|]=\mathbb{E}[|g_{\eta}|]\cdot\mathbb{E}[|h_{\eta}|]=\frac{2}{\pi}
and
\mathbb{E}[|g_{\eta}g^{\prime}|]=\frac{2}{\pi}[\varphi_{\eta}\sin\varphi_{\eta}+\cos\varphi_{\eta}]
, then for
M(\theta,\nu)\in\text{span}\{\vec{e}_{1},\vec{e}_{2}\}
:

\displaystyle\left|\langle M(\theta,\nu)/\|\theta^{\ast}\|,\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\sin\varphi}{\sin\varphi_{\eta}}\mathbb{E}[|g_{\eta}g^{\prime}|]=\frac{2}{\pi}\sin\varphi\left[\varphi_{\eta}+\frac{\cos\varphi_{\eta}}{\sin\varphi_{\eta}}\right]

\displaystyle\left|\langle M(\theta,\nu)/\|\theta^{\ast}\|,\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\cos\varphi}{\cos\varphi_{\eta}}\mathbb{E}[|g_{\eta}h_{\eta}|]=\frac{2}{\pi}\frac{\cos\varphi}{\cos\varphi_{\eta}}

\displaystyle\left\|M(\theta,\nu)\right\|/\|\theta^{\ast}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\left|\langle M(\theta,\nu)/\|\theta^{\ast}\|,\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\right|^{2}+\left|\langle M(\theta,\nu)/\|\theta^{\ast}\|,\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\right|^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\pi}\sqrt{H(\sin\varphi,\eta^{-2})}\leq\frac{2}{\pi}\sqrt{H(1,\eta^{-2})}=\frac{2}{\pi}\left(\arctan\eta+\eta^{-1}\right)

where the last inequality is due to the fact (Lemma
E.6
) that
H(s,\delta)
is non-decreasing with respect to
s=\sin\varphi\in[0,1]
given
\delta:=\eta^{-2}\geq 0
, and
H(s,\delta)
is defined as:

H(s,\delta)=\left(s\arcsin\frac{s}{\sqrt{1+\delta}}+\sqrt{1-s^{2}+\delta}\right)^{2}+\frac{(1+\delta)(1-s^{2})}{1-s^{2}+\delta}

Therefore, we establish the bound for
\left\|M(\theta,\nu)\right\|
, which depends on SNR
\eta=\|\theta^{\ast}\|/\sigma
.

\left\|M(\theta,\nu)\right\|\leq\frac{\arctan\eta}{\pi/2}\|\theta^{\ast}\|+\frac{2}{\pi}\sigma

∎

A-D

Analysis for the Fixed Points of EM Updates in General SNR Regime

Theorem A.6

(Proposition
6
: Distinct Fixed Points of EM Update Rules)
.

The EM update rules
M(\theta,\nu),N(\theta,\nu)
for regression parameters
\theta
and the imbalance of mixing weights
\tanh\nu
have the following three distinct fixed points
(\theta^{\ast},\nu^{\ast}),(-\theta^{\ast},-\nu^{\ast}),(\vec{0},0)
:

\begin{pmatrix}M(\theta^{\ast},\nu^{\ast})\\
N(\theta^{\ast},\nu^{\ast})\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\theta^{\ast}\\
\tanh\nu^{\ast}\end{pmatrix},\quad\begin{pmatrix}M(-\theta^{\ast},-\nu^{\ast})\\
N(-\theta^{\ast},-\nu^{\ast})\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}-\theta^{\ast}\\
\tanh(-\nu^{\ast})\end{pmatrix},\quad\begin{pmatrix}M(\vec{0},0)\\
N(\vec{0},0)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\vec{0}\\
\tanh(0)\end{pmatrix}.

Proof.

For the point
(\theta,\nu)=(\vec{0},0)
, by the equations (
6
), (
7
) of the EM update rules, we have:

\displaystyle M(\vec{0},0)=\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\vec{0}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+0\right)yx\right]=\tanh(0)\cdot\mathbb{E}[yx]=\vec{0},\quad N(\vec{0},0)=\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\vec{0}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+0\right)\right]=\tanh(0)

For the point
(\theta,\nu)=(\theta^{\ast},\nu^{\ast})
, we have
k=\|\theta\|/\|\theta^{\ast}\|=1,\varphi=\pi/2,\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)=1,\vec{e}_{1}=\theta/\|\theta\|=\theta^{\ast}/\|\theta^{\ast}\|
,
then
\cos\varphi=0,\sin\varphi=1,\sin\varphi_{\eta}=\sin\varphi/\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}=1/\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}},\cos\varphi_{\eta}=\eta^{-1}/\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}},A_{\eta}=k\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}=\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}=\sin\varphi_{\eta}/\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}
,
by the expressions of the EM update rules with
g_{\eta},g^{\prime}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
and
\mathbb{E}[g_{\eta}g^{\prime}]=\sin\varphi_{\eta}
:

\displaystyle M(\theta^{\ast},\nu^{\ast})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\theta^{\ast}\frac{\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}}{\sin\varphi_{\eta}}\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left(\sin\varphi_{\eta}\frac{g_{\eta}g^{\prime}}{\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}}+(-1)^{z+1}\nu^{\ast}\right)\frac{g_{\eta}g^{\prime}}{\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}}\right]

\displaystyle N(\theta^{\ast},\nu^{\ast})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left((-1)^{z+1}\sin\varphi_{\eta}\frac{g_{\eta}g^{\prime}}{\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}}+\nu^{\ast}\right)\right]

Note that the distribution of
g_{\eta}g^{\prime}/\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}
has the density involving Bessel function
\cos\varphi_{\eta}\exp(\sin\varphi_{\eta}\cdot x)K_{0}(|x|)/\pi
(see page 50, Section 4.4 Bessel Function Distributions, Chapter 12 Continuous Distributions (General) of
[
49
]
),
therefore by introducing the random variable
X
with a simple probability density function
X\sim f_{X}(x)=K_{0}(|x|)/\pi
, we can write the expectations as:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left(\sin\varphi_{\eta}\frac{g_{\eta}g^{\prime}}{\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}}+(-1)^{z+1}\nu^{\ast}\right)\frac{g_{\eta}g^{\prime}}{\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}}\right]=\cos\varphi_{\eta}\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathbb{E}_{z}\left[\exp(\sin\varphi_{\eta}X)\tanh(\sin\varphi_{\eta}X+(-1)^{z+1}\nu^{\ast})X\right]

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left((-1)^{z+1}\sin\varphi_{\eta}\frac{g_{\eta}g^{\prime}}{\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}}+\nu^{\ast}\right)\right]=\cos\varphi_{\eta}\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathbb{E}_{z}\left[\exp(\sin\varphi_{\eta}X)\tanh\left((-1)^{z+1}\sin\varphi_{\eta}X+\nu^{\ast}\right)\right]

Note that
\mathbb{P}[z=1]=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\tanh\nu^{\ast}=\frac{\exp(\nu^{\ast})}{2\cosh\nu^{\ast}},\mathbb{P}[z=-1]=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\tanh\nu^{\ast}=\frac{\exp(-\nu^{\ast})}{2\cosh\nu^{\ast}}
,
X
is a symmetric random variable,
and
(\exp(t)+\exp(-t))\tanh(t)=2\sinh(t),2\sinh(a+b)=\exp(b)\exp(a)-\exp(-b)\exp(-a),2\cosh(b)=\exp(b)+\exp(-b)
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathbb{E}_{z}\left[\exp(\sin\varphi_{\eta}X)\tanh(\sin\varphi_{\eta}X+(-1)^{z+1}\nu^{\ast})X\right]=\frac{1}{\cosh\nu^{\ast}}\mathbb{E}_{X}[\sinh(\sin\varphi_{\eta}X+\nu^{\ast})X]=\mathbb{E}_{X}\left[\exp(\sin\varphi_{\eta}X)X\right]

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathbb{E}_{z}\left[\exp(\sin\varphi_{\eta}X)\tanh\left((-1)^{z+1}\sin\varphi_{\eta}X+\nu^{\ast}\right)\right]=\frac{1}{\cosh\nu^{\ast}}\mathbb{E}_{X}\left[\sinh(\sin\varphi_{\eta}X+\nu^{\ast})\right]=\tanh\nu^{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{X}\left[\exp(\sin\varphi_{\eta}X)\right]

By letting
\nu\to 0
in the third formula in table 6.611
with modified Bessel function
K_{\nu}
, Section 6.61 Combinations of Bessel functions and exponentials, Page 703
of
[
55
]
, applying Leibniz integral rule or the dominated convergence theorem to exchange the order of taking limit
and taking expectations (see Theorem 1.5.8, page 24 of
[
56
]
) with
|\alpha|<1
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X}\left[\exp(\alpha X)\right]=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}}\implies\mathbb{E}_{X}\left[\exp(\alpha X)X\right]=\mathbb{E}_{X}\left[\partial_{\alpha}\exp(\alpha X)\right]=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\alpha}\mathbb{E}_{X}\left[\exp(\alpha X)\right]=\frac{\alpha}{(1-\alpha^{2})^{3/2}}

By substituting
\alpha\leftarrow\sin\varphi_{\eta}
in the above expressions, we have:

\mathbb{E}_{X}\left[\exp(\sin\varphi_{\eta}X)X\right]=\frac{\sin\varphi_{\eta}}{\cos^{3}\varphi_{\eta}},\quad\mathbb{E}_{X}\left[\exp(\sin\varphi_{\eta}X)\right]=\frac{1}{\cos\varphi_{\eta}}

Putting the above expressions together, we have shown that:

\displaystyle M(\theta^{\ast},\nu^{\ast})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\theta^{\ast}\frac{\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}}{\sin\varphi_{\eta}}\cos\varphi_{\eta}\mathbb{E}_{X}\left[\exp(\sin\varphi_{\eta}X)X\right]=\theta^{\ast}\frac{\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}}{\sin\varphi_{\eta}}\cos\varphi_{\eta}\frac{\sin\varphi_{\eta}}{\cos^{3}\varphi_{\eta}}=\theta^{\ast}

\displaystyle N(\theta^{\ast},\nu^{\ast})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cos\varphi_{\eta}\tanh\nu^{\ast}\mathbb{E}_{X}\left[\exp(\sin\varphi_{\eta}X)\right]=\cos\varphi_{\eta}\tanh\nu^{\ast}\frac{1}{\cos\varphi_{\eta}}=\tanh\nu^{\ast}

For the point
(\theta,\nu)=(-\theta^{\ast},-\nu^{\ast})
, we have
k=\|\theta\|/\|\theta^{\ast}\|=1,\varphi=\pi/2,\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)=-1,\vec{e}_{1}=\theta/\|\theta\|=-\theta^{\ast}/\|\theta^{\ast}\|
,
then
\cos\varphi=0,\sin\varphi=1,\sin\varphi_{\eta}=\sin\varphi/\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}=-1/\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}},\cos\varphi_{\eta}=\eta^{-1}/\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}},A_{\eta}=k\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}=\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}=\sin\varphi_{\eta}/\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}
,
by the expressions of the EM update rules with
g_{\eta},g^{\prime}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
and
\mathbb{E}[g_{\eta}g^{\prime}]=\sin\varphi_{\eta}
:

\displaystyle M(-\theta^{\ast},-\nu^{\ast})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\theta^{\ast}\frac{\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}}{\sin\varphi_{\eta}}\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left(\sin\varphi_{\eta}\frac{g_{\eta}g^{\prime}}{\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}}+(-1)^{z+1}\nu^{\ast}\right)\frac{g_{\eta}g^{\prime}}{\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}}\right]=-M(\theta^{\ast},\nu^{\ast})=-\theta^{\ast}

\displaystyle N(-\theta^{\ast},-\nu^{\ast})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left((-1)^{z+1}\sin\varphi_{\eta}\frac{g_{\eta}g^{\prime}}{\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}}+\nu^{\ast}\right)\right]=-N(\theta^{\ast},\nu^{\ast})=\tanh(-\nu^{\ast})

Therefore, we have verified these three distinct fixed points
(\theta^{\ast},\nu^{\ast}),(-\theta^{\ast},-\nu^{\ast}),(\vec{0},0)
of the EM update rules.
∎

Theorem A.7

(Proposition
7
: Contraction Property of EM Update Rule around the Fixed Point)
.

Suppose that the unit direction vector
\vec{e}^{\perp}

is orthogonal to the ground truth
\theta^{\ast}
of regression parameters, namely
\langle\theta^{\ast},\vec{e}^{\perp}\rangle=0,\|\vec{e}^{\perp}\|=1
,
there exists
k^{\ast}(\eta)>0
which is determined by
\eta=\|\theta^{\ast}\|/\sigma
such that the
(k^{\ast}(\eta)\|\theta^{\ast}\|\vec{e}^{\perp},0)
is a fixed point of the EM update rule
M(\theta,\nu),N(\theta,\nu)
for regression parameters
\theta
and the imbalance of mixing weights
\tanh\nu
given any SNR
\eta=\|\theta^{\ast}\|/\sigma>0
:

\begin{pmatrix}M(k^{\ast}(\eta)\|\theta^{\ast}\|\vec{e}^{\perp},0)\\
N(k^{\ast}(\eta)\|\theta^{\ast}\|\vec{e}^{\perp},0)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}k^{\ast}(\eta)\|\theta^{\ast}\|\vec{e}^{\perp}\\
\tanh(0)\end{pmatrix}.

For any
k>0
,
N(k\|\theta^{\ast}\|\vec{e}^{\perp},0)=\tanh(0)
and
M(k\|\theta^{\ast}\|\vec{e}^{\perp},0),\vec{e}^{\perp}
have the same direction,

\begin{cases}0<\|M(k\|\theta^{\ast}\|\vec{e}^{\perp},0)\|/\|\theta^{\ast}\|-k<k^{\ast}(\eta)-k\quad\text{if }k<k^{\ast}(\eta),\\
0=\|M(k\|\theta^{\ast}\|\vec{e}^{\perp},0)\|/\|\theta^{\ast}\|-k=k^{\ast}(\eta)-k\quad\text{if }k=k^{\ast}(\eta),\\
0>\|M(k\|\theta^{\ast}\|\vec{e}^{\perp},0)\|/\|\theta^{\ast}\|-k>k^{\ast}(\eta)-k\quad\text{if }k>k^{\ast}(\eta).\end{cases}

Moreover, the bounds of
k^{\ast}(\eta)
are:

\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}<k^{\ast}(\eta)<\min\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}},1\right),\forall\,\eta>0.

with
\lim_{\eta\to 0_{+}}k^{\ast}(\eta)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}
and
\lim_{\eta\to\infty}k^{\ast}(\eta)=\frac{2}{\pi}
.

Proof.

Suppose the regression parameters
\theta=k\|\theta^{\ast}\|\vec{e}^{\perp}
for some
k>0
and the imbalance of mixing weights
\tanh\nu=0
,
then by proof of for the expressions of the EM update rules, we have the following expressions with
A_{\eta}=k\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}
:

\displaystyle M(k\|\theta^{\ast}\|\vec{e}^{\perp},0)/\|\theta^{\ast}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{e}_{1}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}\mathbb{E}[\tanh\left(A_{\eta}X+0\right)X]+\vec{e}_{2}\mathbb{E}[(-1)^{z+1}]\mathbb{E}[\tanh\left(A_{\eta}g_{\eta}g^{\prime}+0\right)g_{\eta}^{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{e}^{\perp}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}\mathbb{E}[\tanh\left(A_{\eta}X\right)X]

\displaystyle N(k\|\theta^{\ast}\|\vec{e}^{\perp},0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left(A_{\eta}X+0\right)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\tanh\left(A_{\eta}X\right)\right]=0

due to the fact that
\vec{e}_{1}=\theta/\|\theta\|=\vec{e}^{\perp}
and
X=(-1)^{z+1}g_{\eta}g^{\prime}\sim f_{X}(x)=K_{0}(|x|)/\pi
is a symmetric random variable, and
g_{\eta},g^{\prime}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
with
\mathbb{E}[g_{\eta}g^{\prime}]=0
,

\mathbb{E}[\tanh(A_{\eta}g_{\eta}g^{\prime})g_{\eta}^{2}]=\mathbb{E}_{g_{\eta}}\mathbb{E}_{g^{\prime}}[\tanh(A_{\eta}g_{\eta}g^{\prime})g_{\eta}^{2}]=\mathbb{E}_{g_{\eta}}[0]=0

Moreover, the EM update
M(k\|\theta^{\ast}\|\vec{e}^{\perp},0)
and
\vec{e}^{\perp}
have the same direction, since

M(k\|\theta^{\ast}\|\vec{e}^{\perp},0)=\vec{e}^{\perp}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}\mathbb{E}[\tanh\left(A_{\eta}X\right)X]\in\text{span}\{\vec{e}^{\perp}\},\left\langle\frac{M(k\|\theta^{\ast}\|\vec{e}^{\perp},0)}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|},\vec{e}^{\perp}\right\rangle=\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}\mathbb{E}[\tanh\left(A_{\eta}X\right)X]>0

For the ease of analysis, we introduce the following function
G_{\eta}(k)
with
k>0
:

G_{\eta}(k)=\frac{\|M(k\|\theta^{\ast}\|\vec{e}^{\perp},0)\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}-k=\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}\mathbb{E}[\tanh\left(A_{\eta}X\right)X]-k=\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}\mathbb{E}[\tanh(k\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}X)X]-k

We obtain the derivative and second derivative of
G_{\eta}(k)
as follows by applying Leibniz integral rule or the dominated convergence theorem to exchange the order of taking derivative and taking expectations (see Theorem 1.5.8, page 24 of
[
56
]
):

\displaystyle\frac{\partial G_{\eta}(k)}{\partial k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{k}\tanh(k\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}X)X]-1=\eta^{2}(1+\eta^{-2})\mathbb{E}\left[[1-\tanh^{2}(k\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}X)]X^{2}\right]-1

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}G_{\eta}(k)}{\partial k^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta^{2}(1+\eta^{-2})\mathbb{E}\partial_{k}\left[\frac{X^{2}}{\cosh^{2}(k\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}X)}\right]=-2\eta^{4}(1+\eta^{-2})^{3/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\tanh(k\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}X)X^{3}}{\cosh^{2}(k\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}X)}\right]<0

The initial/final values of
G_{\eta}(k)
and its derivative are obtained by applying the dominated convergence theorem to exchange the order of taking derivative and taking expectations (see Theorem 1.5.8, page 24 of
[
56
]
) and using the fact
\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]=1
:

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to 0_{+}}G_{\eta}(k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}\mathbb{E}[\lim_{k\to 0_{+}}\tanh(k\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}X)X]-0=\mathbb{E}[0]-0=0

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to 0_{+}}\frac{\partial G_{\eta}(k)}{\partial k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta^{2}(1+\eta^{-2})\mathbb{E}\left[[1-\lim_{k\to 0_{+}}\tanh^{2}(k\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}X)]X^{2}\right]-1=\eta^{2}(1+\eta^{-2})-1=\eta^{2}>0

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}G_{\eta}(k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}\mathbb{E}[\lim_{k\to\infty}\tanh(k\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}X)X]-\lim_{k\to\infty}k=\mathbb{E}[|X|]-\infty=-\infty

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{\partial G_{\eta}(k)}{\partial k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta^{2}(1+\eta^{-2})\mathbb{E}\left[[1-\lim_{k\to\infty}\tanh^{2}(k\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}X)]X^{2}\right]-1=\eta^{2}(1+\eta^{-2})\mathbb{E}[0]-1=-1

The signs of some special values of
G_{\eta}(k)
are determined by Lemma
E.4

with
A:=\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}}=\frac{\sqrt{4A^{2}+1}-1}{2A}

and
A^{\prime}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}}=\frac{\sqrt{12A^{\prime 2}+1}-1}{2\sqrt{3}A^{\prime}}

and noting the fact that
\tanh(x)<1,\forall x>0
and
\mathbb{E}[|X|]=\frac{2}{\pi}
:

\displaystyle G_{\eta}\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}\right)<\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}\mathbb{E}[|X|]-\frac{2}{\pi}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}=0

\displaystyle G_{\eta}(1)=\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}\left(\mathbb{E}[\tanh(AX)X]-\frac{\sqrt{4A^{2}+1}-1}{2A}\right)<0

\displaystyle G_{\eta}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\right)=\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}\left(\mathbb{E}[\tanh(A^{\prime}X)X]-\frac{\sqrt{12A^{\prime 2}+1}-1}{6A^{\prime}}\right)>0

Existence and uniqueness of
k^{\ast}(\eta)
: Therefore, there exists
k^{\ast}(\eta)\in(\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}},\min(\frac{2}{\pi}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}},1))
such that
G_{\eta}(k^{\ast}(\eta))=0
by the continuity of
G_{\eta}(k)
and the intermediate value theorem
(see 4.23 Theorem on page 93 of
[
57
]
). Moreover, the root
k^{\ast}(\eta)
of
G_{\eta}(k)=0
is unique, otherwise it would contradict to the fact of
G_{\eta}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}})>0
and
\frac{\partial^{2}G_{\eta}(k)}{\partial k^{2}}<0
.
If there exists another root
\tilde{k}^{\ast}(\eta)
such that
G_{\eta}(\tilde{k}^{\ast}(\eta))=0
(we may assume
k^{\ast}(\eta)<\tilde{k}^{\ast}(\eta)
),
then there exists
\bar{k}\in(k^{\ast}(\eta),\tilde{k}^{\ast}(\eta))
such that
\frac{\partial G_{\eta}(k)}{\partial k}\mid_{k=\bar{k}}=0=\frac{G_{\eta}(\tilde{k}^{\ast}(\eta))-G_{\eta}(k^{\ast}(\eta))}{\tilde{k}^{\ast}(\eta)-k^{\ast}(\eta)}

by using the mean value theorem (see 5.10 Theorem on page 108 of
[
57
]
).
Then by
\frac{\partial^{2}G_{\eta}(\bar{k})}{\partial k^{2}}<0
, we have
\frac{\partial G_{\eta}(k)}{\partial k}>\frac{\partial G_{\eta}(k)}{\partial k}\mid_{k=\bar{k}}=0
for
k\in(\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}},\bar{k})
, since
k^{\ast}(\eta)\in(\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}},\bar{k})
,
we have
G_{\eta}(k^{\ast}(\eta))>G_{\eta}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}})>0
, which contradicts to the fact of
G_{\eta}(k^{\ast}(\eta))=0
.

Contraction mapping with fixed point
k^{\ast}(\eta)
: Again, by applying the mean value theorem to
G_{\eta}(k)
on
(0,k^{\ast}(\eta))
and noting the fact that
\lim_{k\to 0_{+}}G_{\eta}(k)=G_{\eta}(k^{\ast}(\eta))=0
,
there exists
\bar{k}\in(0,k^{\ast}(\eta))
such that
G_{\eta}(\bar{k})=0
and
\frac{\partial G_{\eta}(k)}{\partial k}\mid_{k=\bar{k}}=0
.
Noting
\frac{\partial^{2}G_{\eta}(k)}{\partial k^{2}}<0
, we have
\frac{\partial G_{\eta}(k)}{\partial k}>\frac{\partial G_{\eta}(k)}{\partial k}\mid_{k=\bar{k}}=0
for
k\in(0,\bar{k})
,
and
\frac{\partial G_{\eta}(k)}{\partial k}<\frac{\partial G_{\eta}(k)}{\partial k}\mid_{k=\bar{k}}=0
for
k\in(\bar{k},\infty)
, and therfore

\displaystyle G_{\eta}(k)>\lim_{k\to 0_{+}}G_{\eta}(k)=0\quad\forall k\in(0,\bar{k}];\quad G_{\eta}(k)>G_{\eta}(k^{\ast}(\eta))=0\quad\forall k\in(\bar{k},k^{\ast}(\eta))

\displaystyle\implies G_{\eta}(k)>0\quad\forall k\in(0,k^{\ast}(\eta));\quad G_{\eta}(k)<G_{\eta}(k^{\ast}(\eta))=0\quad\forall k\in(k^{\ast}(\eta),\infty)

Recalling the definition of
G_{\eta}(k):=\frac{\|M(k\|\theta^{\ast}\|\vec{e}^{\perp},0)\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}-k
for
k>0
,
noting
G_{\eta}(k)+k=\|M(k\|\theta^{\ast}\|\vec{e}^{\perp},0)\|/\|\theta^{\ast}\|=\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}\mathbb{E}[\tanh\left(k\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}X\right)X]
is strictly increasing with respect to
k
, we have:

\displaystyle 0<\frac{\|M(k\|\theta^{\ast}\|\vec{e}^{\perp},0)\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}-k=G_{\eta}(k)=(G_{\eta}(k)+k)-k<(G_{\eta}(k^{\ast}(\eta))+k^{\ast}(\eta))-k=k^{\ast}(\eta)-k\quad\text{if }k<k^{\ast}(\eta)

\displaystyle 0=\frac{\|M(k\|\theta^{\ast}\|\vec{e}^{\perp},0)\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}-k=G_{\eta}(k)=(G_{\eta}(k)+k)-k=(G_{\eta}(k^{\ast}(\eta))+k^{\ast}(\eta))-k=k^{\ast}(\eta)-k\quad\text{if }k=k^{\ast}(\eta)

\displaystyle 0>\frac{\|M(k\|\theta^{\ast}\|\vec{e}^{\perp},0)\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}-k=G_{\eta}(k)=(G_{\eta}(k)+k)-k>(G_{\eta}(k^{\ast}(\eta))+k^{\ast}(\eta))-k=k^{\ast}(\eta)-k\quad\text{if }k>k^{\ast}(\eta)

Limit behavior of
k^{\ast}(\eta)
: To analyze the limit bahavior of
k^{\ast}(\eta)
as
\eta\to 0_{+}
and
\eta\to\infty
,
we notice that
A:=\eta^{2}\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}=\eta\sqrt{1+\eta^{2}}\to 0_{+}
as
\eta\to 0_{+}
and
A\to\infty
as
\eta\to\infty
,
and there is a one-to-one increasing mapping
\eta(A)
from
A
to
\eta
.
Consequently, we write
k^{\ast}(\eta(A))
as
k^{\ast}(A)
for simplicity.
From the definition of
k^{\ast}(\eta)
such that
G_{\eta}(k^{\ast}(\eta))=0
,
we show that
k=k^{\ast}(A)
is the unique root of
F_{1}(A,k)=0
for any
A>0
, and is also the unique root of
F_{2}(A,k)=0
for any
A>0
,
where
F_{1}(A,k),F_{2}(A,k)
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle F_{1}(A,k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{E}[\tanh(kAX)X]-k\frac{\sqrt{4A^{2}+1}-1}{2A}\right)/A^{3}

\displaystyle F_{2}(A,k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tanh(kAX)X]-k\frac{\sqrt{4A^{2}+1}-1}{2A}

These
F_{1}(A,k),F_{2}(A,k)
are continuous in
A
and
k
for any
A>0,k>0
, their limits as
A\to 0_{+}
and
A\to\infty
are as follows:

\displaystyle F_{1,0_{+}}(k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{A\to 0_{+}}F_{1}(A,k)=\lim_{A\to 0_{+}}3k^{3}-k+\mathcal{O}(A^{2})=k(3k^{2}-1)

\displaystyle F_{2,\infty}(k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{A\to\infty}F_{2}(A,k)=\mathbb{E}[|X|]-k=\frac{2}{\pi}-k

due to that
\tanh(t)=t-\frac{t^{3}}{3}+\mathcal{O}(t^{5}),\frac{\sqrt{4A^{2}+1}-1}{2A}=A-A^{3}+\mathcal{O}(A^{5})
and
\mathbb{E}[X^{2n}]=[(2n-1)!!]^{2},\mathbb{E}[|X|]=\frac{2}{\pi}
by Lemma
E.3
.
We denote the unique positive root of
F_{1,0_{+}}(k)=0
as
k^{\ast}_{0_{+}}
and the unique root of
F_{2,\infty}(k)=0
as
k^{\ast}_{\infty}
.

k^{\ast}_{0_{+}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}},\quad k^{\ast}_{\infty}=\frac{2}{\pi}

We show that the limits of
k^{\ast}(A)
as
A\to 0_{+}
and
A\to\infty
are
k^{\ast}_{0_{+}}
and
k^{\ast}_{\infty}
, respectively,

\lim_{\eta\to 0_{+}}k^{\ast}(\eta)=\lim_{A\to 0_{+}}k^{\ast}(A)=k^{\ast}_{0_{+}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}},\quad\lim_{\eta\to\infty}k^{\ast}(\eta)=\lim_{A\to\infty}k^{\ast}(A)=k^{\ast}_{\infty}=\frac{2}{\pi}

by checking the following conditions of
F_{1}(A,k),F_{2}(A,k)
and
F_{1,0_{+}}(k),F_{2,\infty}(k)
and applying Lemma
E.5
for the rigourous analysis
and susbstiting
A_{0}\leftarrow 0_{+},\infty
,
F\leftarrow F_{1},F_{2}
,
k_{A_{0}}^{\ast}\leftarrow k^{\ast}_{0_{+}},k^{\ast}_{\infty}
in this lemma, respectively.

Conditions of
F_{1}(A,k),F_{2}(A,k)
and
F_{1,0_{+}}(k),F_{2,\infty}(k)
: (1)
F_{1}(A,k),F_{2}(A,k)
are continuous in
k
for any
A>0
, since

\frac{\partial F_{1}(A,k)}{\partial k}
and
\frac{\partial F_{2}(A,k)}{\partial k}
exists for any
k,A>0
.
The root
k=k^{\ast}(A)
of
F_{1}(A,k)=0,F_{2}(A,k)=0
is unique for any
A>0
.

(2)
F_{1}(A,k),F_{2}(A,k)
converge to
F_{1,0_{+}}(k),F_{2,\infty}(k)
on compact interval
I:=[\frac{1}{2},1]
as
A\to 0_{+}
and
A\to\infty
, respectively.

\sup_{k\in I}|F_{1}(A,k)-F_{1,0_{+}}(k)|\leq\sup_{k\in I}\max(|30k^{5}A^{2}|,|2kA^{2}|)=30A^{2}<\varepsilon

for
0<A<\sqrt{\varepsilon/30},\forall\varepsilon>0
, by using
t-\frac{1}{3}t^{3}<\tanh(t)<t-\frac{t^{3}}{3}+\frac{2}{15}t^{5},\forall t>0
,
A-A^{3}<\frac{\sqrt{4A^{2}+1}-1}{2A}<A-A^{3}+2A^{5}
and
\mathbb{E}[X^{2n}]=[(2n-1)!!]^{2}
in Lemma
E.3
.
Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{k\in I}|F_{2}(A,k)-F_{2,\infty}(k)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{k\in I}|2\mathbb{E}[|X|\exp(-2kA|X|)]|+\left|\frac{k}{2}A^{-1}\right|<\frac{4}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int^{\infty}_{0}\sqrt{x}\exp(-Ax)\mathrm{d}x+\frac{1}{2}A^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}A^{-\frac{3}{2}}+\frac{1}{2}A^{-1}\leq\frac{5}{2}A^{-1}<\varepsilon

for
A>\max(\frac{1}{2},\frac{5}{2\varepsilon}),\forall\varepsilon>0
, by using
1-2\exp(-2t)<\tanh(t)<1-\exp(-2t),\forall t>0
,
1-\frac{1}{2}A^{-1}<\frac{\sqrt{4A^{2}+1}-1}{2A}<1-\frac{1}{2}A^{-1}+\frac{1}{8}A^{-2}\leq 1-\frac{1}{4}A^{-1},\forall A\geq\frac{1}{2}

and
K_{0}(x)<K_{\frac{1}{2}}(x)=\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{\exp(-x)}{\sqrt{x}},\forall x>0
by the monotonicity property of
K_{\nu}
with respect to
\nu\geq 0
(see Section 10.37 Inequalities; Monotonicity; Section 10.39 Relation to Other
Functions, Chapter 10 Bessel Function of
[
58
]
).

(3) The roots
k^{\ast}_{0_{+}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}},k^{\ast}_{\infty}=\frac{2}{\pi}
of
F_{1,0_{+}}(k)=0,F_{2,\infty}(k)=0
are the interior points of the compact interval
I=[\frac{1}{2},1]
, and

\frac{\mathrm{d}F_{1,0_{+}}(k)}{\mathrm{d}k}\mid_{k=k^{\ast}_{0_{+}}}=2\neq 0,\frac{\mathrm{d}F_{2,\infty}(k)}{\mathrm{d}k}\mid_{k=k^{\ast}_{\infty}}=-1\neq 0
,
and
\frac{\mathrm{d}F_{1,0_{+}}(k)}{\mathrm{d}k}=9k^{2}-1,\frac{\mathrm{d}F_{2,\infty}(k)}{\mathrm{d}k}=-1
are continuous in
k
on
I
.
∎

A-E

EM Update Rule in the Noiseless Setting

Theorem A.8

(Corollary
8
: EM Updates in Noiseless Setting, Corollary 3.3 in
[
1
]
)
.

In the noiseless setting, namely SNR
\eta:=\|\theta^{\ast}\|/\sigma\to\infty
, the population EM update rules
M(\theta,\nu),N(\theta,\nu)
for regression parameters
\theta
and imbalance of mixing weights
\tanh\nu
are:

\displaystyle\frac{M(\theta,\nu)}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\frac{2}{\pi}\left[\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\varphi\frac{\theta^{\ast}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}+\cos\varphi\frac{\theta}{\|\theta\|}\right]

\displaystyle N(\theta,\nu)=\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\frac{2}{\pi}\varphi\cdot\tanh\nu^{\ast}

where
\varphi:=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho|
,
\rho:=\frac{\langle\theta,\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta\|\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
.

Proof.

We provide a completely new proof based on our previous newly established results rather than the old approach in
[
1
]
.
Taking the limit
\eta\rightarrow+\infty
, then
\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}\rightarrow 1,A_{\eta}\to+\infty,g_{\eta}\to g
and
\varphi_{\eta}\to\varphi,\cos\varphi_{\eta}\rightarrow\cos\varphi,\sin\varphi_{\eta}\rightarrow\sin\varphi
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\rightarrow\infty}M(\theta,\nu)/\|\theta^{\ast}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{e}_{1}\mathbb{E}[|gg^{\prime}|]+\vec{e}_{2}\frac{1}{\cos\varphi}\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)\mathbb{E}[g(g-\sin\varphi\cdot g^{\prime})\operatorname{sgn}(gg^{\prime})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{e}_{1}\mathbb{E}[|gg^{\prime}|]+\vec{e}_{2}\frac{\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)}{\cos\varphi}\left(\mathbb{E}[g^{2}\operatorname{sgn}(gg^{\prime})]-\sin\varphi\mathbb{E}[|gg^{\prime}|]\right)

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\rightarrow\infty}N(\theta,\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)\mathbb{E}[(-1)^{z+1}]\cdot\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{sgn}(gg^{\prime})]=\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)\tanh\nu^{\ast}\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{sgn}(gg^{\prime})]

By applying Lemma
E.2
, we have
\mathbb{E}[\textnormal{sgn}(gg^{\prime})]=\frac{2}{\pi}\varphi,\mathbb{E}[g^{2}\textnormal{sgn}(gg^{\prime})]=\frac{2}{\pi}[\varphi+\sin\varphi\cos\varphi]
, and
\mathbb{E}[|gg^{\prime}|]=\frac{2}{\pi}[\varphi\sin\varphi+\cos\varphi]
:

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\rightarrow\infty}M(\theta,\nu)/\|\theta^{\ast}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\pi}[\varphi\sin\varphi+\cos\varphi]\vec{e}_{1}+\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)\frac{2}{\pi}\varphi\cos\varphi\vec{e}_{2}

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\rightarrow\infty}N(\theta,\nu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{sgn}(\rho)\frac{2}{\pi}\varphi\cdot\tanh\nu^{\ast}

Noting that
\vec{e}_{1}=\theta/\|\theta\|,\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\sin\varphi\vec{e}_{1}+\cos\varphi\vec{e}_{2}=\theta^{\ast}/\|\theta^{\ast}\|
, we have:

\lim_{\eta\rightarrow\infty}\frac{M(\theta,\nu)}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\frac{2}{\pi}\left[\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\varphi\frac{\theta^{\ast}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}+\cos\varphi\frac{\theta}{\|\theta\|}\right]

∎

A-F

Deviation from Cycloid Limit of EM Updates in High SNR Regime

Theorem A.9

(Proposition
9
: Deviation from Cycloid Limit of EM Updates in High SNR Regime)
.

In the finite high SNR regime given by
\eta\gtrsim\frac{1}{\min(1,\sqrt{k})\cos\varphi}\vee 1
, if the mixing weights are known balanced
\pi=\pi^{\ast}=(1/2,1/2)
,
then the difference between the EM update rule and its limit is bounded by:

\left\|\frac{M(\theta)}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}-\lim_{\eta\rightarrow\infty}\frac{M(\theta)}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\right\|=\mathcal{O}\left(\eta^{-2}\vee\cos^{2}\varphi\frac{\log\Lambda}{\Lambda^{4}}\right)

where
\Lambda:=\eta\sqrt{k}\cos\varphi
,
k:=\frac{\|\theta\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
and
\varphi:=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho|
,
\rho:=\frac{\langle\theta,\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta\|\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
.

Proof.

When the mixing weights are known balanced
\pi=\pi^{\ast}=(1/2,1/2)
, we must have
\nu=\nu^{\ast}=0
.
Consequently, the EM update rules for the imbalance of mixing weights directly gives

N(\theta,\nu)_{\nu=0,\nu^{\ast}=0}=0

by using the symmetry of the data distributions with two balanced components.
Furthermore, the EM update rule for regresssion parameteres becomes

\displaystyle\frac{M(\theta)}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\frac{M(\theta,\nu)_{\nu=0,\nu^{\ast}=0}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\vec{e}_{1}\frac{\sin\varphi}{\sin\varphi_{\eta}}\mathbb{E}[\tanh\left(A_{\eta}g_{\eta}g^{\prime}\right)g_{\eta}g^{\prime}]+\vec{e}_{2}\frac{\cos\varphi}{\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}}\mathbb{E}[\tanh\left(A_{\eta}g_{\eta}g^{\prime}\right)g_{\eta}(g_{\eta}-\sin\varphi_{\eta}\cdot g^{\prime})]

We further bound the difference between the EM update rule and its limit (the cycloid trajectory) when SNR goes to infinity.

\displaystyle\frac{M(\theta)}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}-\lim_{\eta\rightarrow\infty}\frac{M(\theta)}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\vec{e}_{1}\left[\frac{\sin\varphi}{\sin\varphi_{\eta}}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}[\tanh(A_{\eta}g_{\eta}g^{\prime})g_{\eta}g^{\prime}-|g_{\eta}g^{\prime}|]}_{T_{1,1}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\sin\varphi}{\sin\varphi_{\eta}}|g_{\eta}g^{\prime}|-|gg^{\prime}|\right]}_{T_{1,2}}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\vec{e}_{2}\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\left[\frac{\cos\varphi}{\cos\varphi_{\eta}}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}[\tanh(A_{\eta}g_{\eta}g^{\prime})g_{\eta}(g_{\eta}-\sin\varphi_{\eta}\cdot g^{\prime})-\textnormal{sgn}(g_{\eta}g^{\prime})g_{\eta}(g_{\eta}-\sin\varphi_{\eta}\cdot g^{\prime})]/\cos\varphi_{\eta}}_{T_{2,1}}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\vec{e}_{2}\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\cos\varphi}{\cos\varphi_{\eta}}\textnormal{sgn}(g_{\eta}g^{\prime})g_{\eta}(g_{\eta}-\sin\varphi_{\eta}\cdot g^{\prime})/\cos\varphi_{\eta}-\textnormal{sgn}(gg^{\prime})g(g-\sin\varphi\cdot g^{\prime})/\cos\varphi\right]}_{T_{2,2}}

Evaluation of the terms
T_{1,2},T_{2,2}
:

By applying the Lemma
E.2
for
g_{\eta},g,g^{\prime}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
with
\mathbb{E}[g_{\eta}g^{\prime}]=\sin\varphi_{\eta},\mathbb{E}[gg^{\prime}]=\sin\varphi
,
we have
E[|g_{\eta}g^{\prime}|]=\frac{2}{\pi}[\varphi_{\eta}\sin\varphi_{\eta}+\cos\varphi_{\eta}],\mathbb{E}[|gg^{\prime}|]=\frac{2}{\pi}[\varphi\sin\varphi+\cos\varphi]
:

T_{1,2}=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\sin\varphi}{\sin\varphi_{\eta}}|g_{\eta}g^{\prime}|-|gg^{\prime}|\right]=\frac{\sin\varphi}{\sin\varphi_{\eta}}\frac{2}{\pi}[\varphi_{\eta}\sin\varphi_{\eta}+\cos\varphi_{\eta}]-\frac{2}{\pi}[\varphi\sin\varphi+\cos\varphi]=\frac{2}{\pi}\left[(\varphi_{\eta}-\varphi)\sin\varphi-\frac{\sin(\varphi_{\eta}-\varphi)}{\sin\varphi_{\eta}}\right]

Again, by applying the Lemma
E.2
for
g_{\eta},g,g^{\prime}
, we have
\mathbb{E}[\textnormal{sgn}(g_{\eta}g^{\prime})g_{\eta}(g_{\eta}-\sin\varphi_{\eta}\cdot g^{\prime})/\cos\varphi_{\eta}]=\frac{2}{\pi}\varphi_{\eta}\cos\varphi_{\eta}
and
\mathbb{E}[\textnormal{sgn}(gg^{\prime})g(g-\sin\varphi\cdot g^{\prime})/\cos\varphi]=\frac{2}{\pi}\varphi\cos\varphi
:

T_{2,2}=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\cos\varphi}{\cos\varphi_{\eta}}\textnormal{sgn}(g_{\eta}g^{\prime})g_{\eta}(g_{\eta}-\sin\varphi_{\eta}\cdot g^{\prime})/\cos\varphi_{\eta}-\textnormal{sgn}(gg^{\prime})g(g-\sin\varphi\cdot g^{\prime})/\cos\varphi\right]=\frac{2}{\pi}(\varphi_{\eta}-\varphi)\cos\varphi

We bound the following quantities to establish the asymptotic notations of
T_{1,1},T_{2,1}
in terms of SNR
\eta
and
\varphi
, :

0\leq 1-\frac{\sin\varphi_{\eta}}{\sin\varphi}=1-(1+\eta^{-2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{\eta^{-2}}{2},\quad 0\leq\frac{\sin\varphi}{\sin\varphi_{\eta}}-1=\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}-1\leq\frac{\eta^{-2}}{2}

0\leq\varphi-\varphi_{\eta}\leq\pi\sin\frac{\varphi-\varphi_{\eta}}{2}=\pi\frac{(1-\frac{\sin\varphi_{\eta}}{\sin\varphi})\sin\varphi}{2\cos\frac{\varphi+\varphi_{\eta}}{2}}\leq\frac{\pi(\frac{\eta^{-2}}{2})\sin\varphi}{2\cos\varphi}=\frac{\pi\sin\varphi}{4\cos\varphi}\eta^{-2}

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\sin(\varphi-\varphi_{\eta})}{(\varphi-\varphi_{\eta})}\cdot\frac{1}{\sin\varphi_{\eta}\sin\varphi}-1\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sin^{2}\varphi}\left(\frac{\sin\varphi}{\sin\varphi_{\eta}}-1\right)\frac{\sin(\varphi_{\eta}-\varphi)}{(\varphi_{\eta}-\varphi)}+\frac{1}{\sin^{2}\varphi}\left|\frac{\sin(\varphi_{\eta}-\varphi)}{(\varphi_{\eta}-\varphi)}-1\right|+\left(\frac{1}{\sin^{2}\varphi}-1\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sin^{2}\varphi}\left(\frac{\eta^{-2}}{2}+\frac{1}{6}\times\left[\frac{\pi\sin\varphi}{4\cos\varphi}\eta^{-2}\right]^{2}+\cos^{2}\varphi\right)=\frac{\cos^{2}\varphi}{\sin^{2}\varphi}+\frac{\eta^{-2}}{\sin^{2}\varphi}\mathcal{O}\left(1\vee\frac{\sin^{2}\varphi}{\cos^{2}\varphi}\eta^{-2}\right)

Therefore, when
\eta\gtrsim\frac{1}{\cos\varphi}
, we have
\mathcal{O}\left(1\vee\eta^{-2}\frac{\sin^{2}\varphi}{\cos^{2}\varphi}\right)=\mathcal{O}(1)
:

\displaystyle|T_{1,2}|=\frac{2}{\pi}\left|\frac{\sin(\varphi-\varphi_{\eta})}{(\varphi-\varphi_{\eta})}\frac{1}{\sin\varphi_{\eta}\sin\varphi}-1\right|(\varphi-\varphi_{\eta})\sin\varphi\leq\frac{\cos\varphi\eta^{-2}}{2}+\frac{\eta^{-4}}{2\cos\varphi}\mathcal{O}\left(1\vee\frac{\eta^{-2}\sin^{2}\varphi}{\cos^{2}\varphi}\right)=\frac{\cos\varphi\eta^{-2}}{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\eta^{-4}}{\cos\varphi}\right)

|T_{2,2}|=\frac{2}{\pi}(\varphi-\varphi_{\eta})\cos\varphi\leq\frac{\sin\varphi\eta^{-2}}{2}

Asymptotic notations of the terms
T_{1,1},T_{2,1}
:

Since we can express
g_{\eta},g^{\prime}
in terms of independent random varaibles
R,U

with
R\sim r\exp(-\frac{r^{2}}{2})\mathbb{I}_{r\geq 0}
(standard Rayleigh distribution with
\mathbb{E}[R^{2}]=2
)
and
U\sim\mathrm{Unif}[0,4\pi)
,

g_{\eta}=R\cos((U-\varphi_{\eta})/2),\quad g^{\prime}=R\sin((U+\varphi_{\eta})/2)

Noting that
\varrho:=R^{2}/2\sim\mathrm{Exp}(1)
follows the standard exponential distribution with
\mathbb{E}[\varrho]=1
,
applying
gg^{\prime}=\varrho[\sin U+\sin\varphi_{\eta}]
and

\textnormal{sgn}(g_{\eta}g^{\prime})g_{\eta}(g_{\eta}-\sin\varphi_{\eta}\cdot g^{\prime})/\cos\varphi_{\eta}=\varrho\textnormal{sgn}(\sin(U+\varphi_{\eta}))|\cos U+\cos\varphi_{\eta}|
, then:

\displaystyle T_{1,1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\tanh(A_{\eta}|g_{\eta}g^{\prime}|)-1)|g_{\eta}g^{\prime}|]=-2\mathbb{E}_{U}\mathbb{E}_{\varrho}\left[\frac{|\sin U+\sin\varphi_{\eta}|\varrho}{\exp(2A_{\eta}|\sin U+\sin\varphi_{\eta}\varrho|)+1}\right]

\displaystyle T_{2,1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\tanh(A_{\eta}|g_{\eta}g^{\prime}|)-1)\textnormal{sgn}(g_{\eta}g^{\prime})g_{\eta}(g_{\eta}-\sin\varphi_{\eta}\cdot g^{\prime})]/\cos\varphi_{\eta}=-2\mathbb{E}_{U}\mathbb{E}_{\varrho}\left[\frac{\textnormal{sgn}(\sin(U+\varphi_{\eta}))|\cos U+\cos\varphi_{\eta}|\varrho}{\exp(2A_{\eta}|\sin U+\sin\varphi_{\eta}|\varrho)+1}\right]

Since
\exp(t)\leq\exp(t)+1\leq 2\exp(t)
for all
t\geq 0
, and

\mathbb{E}_{\varrho}\left[\rho\exp(-2A_{\eta}|\sin U+\sin\varphi_{\eta}|\varrho)\right]=\frac{1}{(1+2A_{\eta}|\sin U+\sin\varphi_{\eta}|)^{2}}

we have shown that
T_{1},T_{2}
can be approximated by the following expectations upto some constant factors:

T_{1,1}\asymp\mathbb{E}_{U}\left[\frac{|\sin U+\sin\varphi_{\eta}|}{(1+2A_{\eta}|\sin U+\sin\varphi_{\eta}|)^{2}}\right],\quad T_{2,1}\asymp\mathbb{E}_{U}\left[\frac{\textnormal{sgn}(\sin(U+\varphi_{\eta}))|\cos U+\cos\varphi_{\eta}|}{(1+2A_{\eta}|\sin U+\sin\varphi_{\eta}|)^{2}}\right]

When
\Lambda^{\prime}:=\sqrt{A_{\eta}}\cos\varphi_{\eta}=\eta(1+\eta^{-2})^{\frac{1}{4}}\sqrt{k}\cos\varphi_{\eta}\gtrsim 1
, applying Lemma
E.1
for asymptotic notations of above expectations:

\displaystyle T_{1,1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{A_{\eta}^{2}\cos\varphi_{\eta}}\log\left(A_{\eta}\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}\right)\right)=\cos^{3}\varphi_{\eta}\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log\Lambda^{\prime}}{[\Lambda^{\prime}]^{4}}\right)

\displaystyle T_{2,1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\tan\varphi_{\eta}}{A_{\eta}^{2}\cos\varphi_{\eta}}\log\left(A_{\eta}\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}\right)\right)=\sin\varphi_{\eta}\cos^{2}\varphi_{\eta}\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log\Lambda^{\prime}}{[\Lambda^{\prime}]^{4}}\right)

where the condition
\Lambda^{\prime}\gtrsim 1
is satisfied and therefore
\Lambda^{\prime}\asymp\sqrt{k}(\eta\cos\varphi\vee 1)
when SNR
\eta\gtrsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}\cos\varphi}\vee 1
is large enough.

Bounds for the difference between the EM update rule and its limit:

When SNR
\eta\gtrsim\frac{1}{\min(1,\sqrt{k})\cos\varphi}\vee 1
is large enough, and we set
\Lambda:=\sqrt{k}\eta\cos\varphi\asymp\sqrt{k}(\eta\cos\varphi\vee 1)\asymp\Lambda^{\prime}
,
then
\sin\varphi/\sin\varphi_{\eta}=\sqrt{1+\eta^{-2}}=\Theta(1),\cos\varphi/\cos\varphi_{\eta}=\Theta(1)
:

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{M(\theta)}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}-\lim_{\eta\rightarrow\infty}\frac{M(\theta)}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\right\|=\left\|\left(\vec{e}_{1}\frac{\sin\varphi}{\sin\varphi_{\eta}}T_{1,1}+\vec{e}_{2}\frac{\cos\varphi}{\cos\varphi_{\eta}}T_{2,1}\right)+(\vec{e}_{1}T_{1,2}+\vec{e}_{2}\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)T_{2,2})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\vec{e}_{1}\cos\varphi_{\eta}+\vec{e}_{2}\sin\varphi_{\eta}\right\|\cos^{2}\varphi\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log\Lambda}{\Lambda^{4}}\right)+\left\|\vec{e}_{1}\cos\varphi+\vec{e}_{2}\sin\varphi\right\|\frac{\eta^{-2}}{2}+\left\|\vec{e}_{1}\right\|\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\eta^{-4}}{\cos\varphi}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\eta^{-2}}{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\eta^{-4}}{\cos\varphi}\vee\cos^{2}\varphi\frac{\log\Lambda}{\Lambda^{4}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\eta^{-2}\vee\cos^{2}\varphi\frac{\log\Lambda}{\Lambda^{4}}\right)

∎

Appendix B
Population Level Analysis in Noiseless Regime

B-A

Recurrence Relation and Cycloid Trajectory of EM Iterations in Noiseless Setting

Theorem B.1

(Proposition
10
: Recurrence Relation of Sub-optimality Angle, Proposition 4.3 in
[
1
]
)
.

In the noiseless setting, namely SNR
\eta:=\|\theta^{\ast}\|/\sigma\to\infty
,
if the sub-optimality angle
\varphi^{t}\neq\frac{\pi}{2}
,
then the recurrence relation of the sub-optimality angle
\varphi^{t}
for population EM updates is:

\tan\varphi^{t+1}=\tanh\varphi^{t}+\varphi^{t}(\tan^{2}\varphi^{t}+1)

where
\varphi^{t}:=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho^{t}|
,
\rho^{t}:=\frac{\langle\theta^{t},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta^{t}\|\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
.

Proof.

The proof based on Corollary 3.3 of EM Updates in Noiseless Setting of
[
1
]
can be found on page 31, Appendix D of
[
1
]
.
Let’s start from the EM update rule for
\theta^{t}
in previous Corollary, since
\rho^{0},\rho^{t-1}
have the same sign
(validated by checking the sign of
\langle\theta^{t},\theta^{\ast}\rangle
)

\frac{\theta^{t}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)^{-1}\left[\varphi^{t-1}\frac{\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})\theta^{\ast}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}+\cos\varphi^{t-1}\frac{\theta^{t-1}}{\|\theta^{t-1}\|}\right]

With
\left\langle\frac{\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})\theta^{\ast}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|},\frac{\theta^{t-1}}{\|\theta^{t-1}\|}\right\rangle=|\rho^{t-1}|=\sin\varphi^{t-1}

\sin\varphi^{t}\frac{\|\theta^{t}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=|\rho^{t}|\frac{\|\theta^{t}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\left\langle\frac{\theta^{t}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|},\frac{\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})\theta^{\ast}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\right\rangle=\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)^{-1}[\varphi^{t-1}+\cos\varphi^{t-1}\sin\varphi^{t-1}]

\frac{\|\theta^{t}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)^{-1}\sqrt{[\varphi^{t-1}]^{2}+\cos^{2}\varphi^{t-1}+2\varphi^{t-1}\cos\varphi^{t-1}\sin\varphi^{t-1}}

Therefore

\sin\varphi^{t}=\frac{\varphi^{t-1}+\cos\varphi^{t-1}\sin\varphi^{t-1}}{\sqrt{[\varphi^{t-1}]^{2}+\cos^{2}\varphi^{t-1}+2\varphi^{t-1}\cos\varphi^{t-1}\sin\varphi^{t-1}}}

Hence

\cos\varphi^{t}=\sqrt{1-\sin^{2}\varphi^{t}}=\frac{\cos^{2}\varphi^{t-1}}{\sqrt{[\varphi^{t-1}]^{2}+\cos^{2}\varphi^{t-1}+2\varphi^{t-1}\cos\varphi^{t-1}\sin\varphi^{t-1}}}

Thus, we obtain the recurrence relation for
\varphi^{t}

\tan\varphi^{t}=\frac{\sin\varphi^{t}}{\cos\varphi^{t}}=\frac{\varphi^{t-1}}{\cos^{2}\varphi^{t-1}}+\frac{\sin\varphi^{t-1}}{\cos\varphi^{t-1}}=\tan\varphi^{t-1}+\varphi^{t-1}[\tan^{2}\varphi^{t-1}+1]

∎

Theorem B.2

(Proposition
11
: Parametric Equation for Cycloid Trajectory of EM Updates, Proposition 4.4 in
[
1
]
)
.

In the noiseless setting, namely SNR
\eta:=\|\theta^{\ast}\|/\sigma\to\infty
,
the coordinates
\mathtt{x}^{t},\mathtt{y}^{t}
of normalized vector
\frac{\theta^{t}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\mathtt{x}^{t}\hat{e}_{1}+\mathtt{y}^{t}\hat{e}_{2}^{t}=\mathtt{x}^{t}\hat{e}_{1}+\mathtt{y}^{t}\hat{e}_{2}^{0},\forall t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}

for population EM updates can be parameterized by the sub-optimality angle
\phi^{t-1}
as follows:

\displaystyle 1-\textnormal{sgn}(\rho^{0})\mathtt{x}^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}[\phi^{t-1}-\sin\phi^{t-1}]

\displaystyle\mathtt{y}^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}[1-\cos\phi^{t-1}]

where
\varphi^{t-1}:=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho^{t-1}|
,
\rho^{t-1}:=\frac{\langle\theta^{t-1},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta^{t-1}\|\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
.
Hence, the trajecotry of EM iterations
\theta^{t}
is on the cycloid with a parameter
\frac{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}{\pi}
, on the plane
\text{span}\{\theta^{0},\theta^{\ast}\}
.

Proof.

The proof based on Corollary 3.3 of EM Updates in Noiseless Setting and Proposition 4.3 of Recurrence Relation of
[
1
]
can be found on page 32, Appendix D of
[
1
]
.
Since
\tan\varphi^{t}=\tan\varphi^{t-1}+\varphi^{t-1}(\tan^{2}\varphi^{t-1}+1)
in the proven recurrence relation, it shows that
\tan\varphi^{t}\geq\tan\varphi^{t-1}\geq 0
, therefore
0\leq\varphi^{0}\leq\varphi^{1}\leq\cdots\leq\varphi^{t-1}\leq\varphi^{t}<\frac{\pi}{2}
.

Let
\hat{e}_{1}:=\frac{\theta^{\ast}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
, and

\hat{e}^{t}_{2}:=\hat{e}_{2}\mid_{\theta=\theta^{t}}=\frac{\theta-\hat{e}_{1}\hat{e}_{1}^{\top}\theta}{\|\theta-\hat{e}_{1}\hat{e}_{1}^{\top}\theta\|}\mid_{\theta=\theta^{t}}=\frac{\frac{\theta^{t}}{\|\theta^{t}\|}-[\rho^{t}]\frac{\theta^{\ast}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}}{\sqrt{1-[\rho^{t}]^{2}}}
and
\langle\hat{e}_{1},\hat{e}^{t}_{2}\rangle=0,\|\hat{e}_{1}\|=\|\hat{e}^{t}_{2}\|=1

\displaystyle\frac{\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|\|\theta^{t-1}\|}\langle\theta^{t-1}-\hat{e}_{1}\hat{e}_{1}^{\top}\theta^{t-1},\theta^{t}-\hat{e}_{1}\hat{e}_{1}^{\top}\theta^{t}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|\|\theta^{t-1}\|}\{\langle\theta^{t-1},\theta^{t}\rangle-\langle\theta^{t-1},\hat{e}_{1}\rangle\langle\hat{e}_{1},\theta^{t}\rangle\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\varphi^{t-1}\sin\varphi^{t-1}+\cos\varphi^{t-1}]-\sin\varphi^{t-1}[\varphi^{t-1}+\cos\varphi^{t-1}\sin\varphi^{t-1}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cos^{3}\varphi^{t-1}>0

Hence, we conclude that
\langle\hat{e}^{t-1}_{2},\hat{e}^{t}_{2}\rangle>0
, With
\hat{e}^{t-1}_{2},\hat{e}^{t}_{2}\perp\hat{e}_{1}
and
\hat{e}^{t-1}_{2},\hat{e}^{t}_{2}\in\operatorname{span}\{\theta^{t},\theta^{t-1},\theta^{\ast}\}\subset\operatorname{span}\{\theta^{0},\theta^{\ast}\}
,
\|\hat{e}^{t-1}_{2}\|=\|\hat{e}^{t}_{2}\|=1
, we validate
\hat{e}^{0}_{2}=\cdots=\hat{e}^{t-1}_{2}=\hat{e}^{t}_{2}
.

By the definition of
\hat{e}^{t}_{2}
, we obtain
\theta^{t}=\|\theta^{t}\|\{\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})\sin\varphi^{t}\hat{e}_{1}+\cos\varphi^{t}\hat{e}^{t}_{2}\}=\|\theta^{t}\|\{\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})\sin\varphi^{t}\hat{e}_{1}+\cos\varphi^{t}\hat{e}^{0}_{2}\}

Since
\theta^{t}\in\operatorname{span}\{\theta^{t-1},\theta^{\ast}\}
, then

\theta^{t}\in\operatorname{span}\{\theta^{0},\theta^{\ast}\}
, we can express

\frac{\theta^{t}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\mathtt{x}^{t}\hat{e}_{1}+\mathtt{y}^{t}\hat{e}^{t}_{2}=\mathtt{x}^{t}\hat{e}_{1}+\mathtt{y}^{t}\hat{e}^{0}_{2}
.

Comparing the expressions for
\theta^{t}
, we derive the following result.

\frac{\theta^{t}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\mathtt{x}^{t}\hat{e}_{1}+\mathtt{y}^{t}\hat{e}^{0}_{2}=\left\{\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})\sin\varphi^{t}\cdot\frac{\|\theta^{t}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\right\}\hat{e}_{1}+\left\{\cos\varphi^{t}\cdot\frac{\|\theta^{t}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\right\}\hat{e}^{0}_{2}

With the recurrence relation
\sin\varphi^{t}\cdot\frac{\|\theta^{t}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)^{-1}[\varphi^{t-1}+\cos\varphi^{t-1}\sin\varphi^{t-1}]
,
\cos\varphi^{t}\cdot\frac{\|\theta^{t}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)^{-1}\cos^{2}\varphi^{t-1}
, which we showed in the proof of Recurrence Relation, we derive the implicit equation of
\mathtt{x}^{t},\mathtt{y}^{t}(t\geq 1)
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathtt{x}^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{\theta^{t}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|},\hat{e}_{1}\right\rangle=\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)^{-1}\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})[\varphi^{t-1}+\cos\varphi^{t-1}\sin\varphi^{t-1}]

\displaystyle\mathtt{y}^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\frac{\theta^{t}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|},\hat{e}^{0}_{2}\right\rangle=\left\langle\frac{\theta^{t}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|},\hat{e}^{t}_{2}\right\rangle=\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\right)^{-1}\cos^{2}\varphi^{t-1}

Let’s cancel out the parameter
\varphi^{t-1}
in the parameterized curve

\varphi^{t-1}\mapsto(\mathtt{x}^{t},\mathtt{y}^{t})

\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})\frac{\pi}{2}\mathtt{x}^{t}=\sqrt{\left(\frac{\pi}{2}\mathtt{y}^{t}\right)\left(1-\frac{\pi}{2}\mathtt{y}^{t}\right)}+\arccos\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2}\mathtt{y}^{t}}

Let
\phi:=2\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\varphi\right)\in(0,\pi]
, then we rewrite the implicit equations of
\mathtt{x}^{t},\mathtt{y}^{t}(t\geq 1)
as follows, which is a cycloid curve
[
59
]
:

\displaystyle 1-\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{0})\mathtt{x}^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi^{-1}[\phi-\sin\phi]_{\phi=\phi^{t-1}}

\displaystyle\mathtt{y}^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi^{-1}[1-\cos\phi]_{\phi=\phi^{t-1}}

∎

B-B

Linear Growth and Quadratic Convergence of Sub-optimality Angles in Noiseless Setting

Theorem B.3

(Proposition
12
: Linear Growth of Sub-optimality Angle)
.

In the noiseless setting, namely SNR
\eta:=\|\theta^{\ast}\|/\sigma\to\infty
,

\tan\varphi^{t}
of the sub-optimality angle
\varphi^{t}
grows at least linearly:

\tan\varphi^{t+1}\geq 2\cdot\tan\varphi^{t}

where
\varphi^{t}:=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho^{t}|
,
\rho^{t}:=\frac{\langle\theta^{t},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta^{t}\|\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
.

Proof.

We start from the recurrence relation of
\varphi^{t}
for the population EM update rule:

\tan\varphi^{t+1}=\tan\varphi^{t}+\varphi^{t}(\tan^{2}\varphi^{t}+1)

The inequality
\tan\varphi^{t+1}\geq 2\cdot\tan\varphi^{t}
holds when
\varphi^{t}=0
.
Let’s consider the case when
\varphi^{t}>0
.
By the trigonometry identity
\sin(2\varphi^{t})=2\tan\varphi^{t}/(1+\tan^{2}\varphi^{t})
,
and the inequality
2\varphi^{t}\geq\sin(2\varphi^{t})
, we have:

\tan\varphi^{t+1}=(1+\frac{2\varphi^{t}}{\sin(2\varphi^{t})})\tan\varphi^{t}\geq 2\cdot\tan\varphi^{t}

∎

Theorem B.4

(Proposition
13
: Quadratic Convergence of Sub-optimality Angle)
.

In the noiseless setting, namely SNR
\eta:=\|\theta^{\ast}\|/\sigma\to\infty
,
the sub-optimality angle
\phi^{t}
converges quadratically to zero when
\phi^{t}\leq 1.4
is small enough:

\frac{\phi^{t+1}}{\pi}\leq\left[\frac{\phi^{t}}{\pi}\right]^{2}

where
\phi^{t}:=2\arccos|\rho^{t}|
,
\rho^{t}:=\frac{\langle\theta^{t},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta^{t}\|\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
.

Proof.

If
\phi^{t}=0
, then
\phi^{t+1}=0
by the EM updates in noiseless setting.
Otherwise, we have
\phi^{t+1}>0
when
\phi^{t}>0
from the EM updates in noiseless setting.
We start from the recursive relation of
\phi^{t}
for the population EM update rule:

\tan\varphi^{t+1}=\tanh\varphi^{t}+\varphi^{t}(\tan^{2}\varphi^{t}+1)

By the definition
\phi^{t}=2(\frac{\pi}{2}-\varphi^{t})
and apply the inequality
x\leq\tan x,\forall x\in[0,\frac{\pi}{2})
, we have:

\frac{1}{\tan\frac{\phi^{t+1}}{2}}=\frac{1}{\tan\frac{\phi^{t}}{2}}+\frac{\pi-\phi^{t}}{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\sin^{2}\frac{\phi^{t}}{2}}\implies\frac{\phi^{t+1}}{\pi}\leq\frac{2}{\pi}\tan\frac{\phi^{t+1}}{2}=\left[\frac{\phi^{t}}{\pi}\right]^{2}\cdot\frac{2\pi(1-\cos\phi^{t})/[\phi^{t}]^{2}}{\sin\phi^{t}+(\pi-\phi^{t})}

By the inequality based on Taylor’s theorem
\cos x\geq 1-\frac{x^{2}}{2}+\frac{x^{4}}{24}-\frac{x^{6}}{720},\sin x\geq x-\frac{x^{3}}{6},\forall x\geq 0
, we have:

\frac{2\pi(1-\cos\phi^{t})}{[\phi^{t}]^{2}}\leq\pi-\frac{\pi}{12}[\phi^{t}]^{2}+\frac{\pi}{360}[\phi^{t}]^{4}\leq\pi-\frac{1}{6}[\phi^{t}]^{3}\leq\sin\phi^{t}+(\pi-\phi^{t})

where the second inequality is due to
30-\frac{60}{\pi}\phi^{t}-[\phi^{t}]^{2}\geq 0
when
0\leq\phi^{t}\leq 1.4
.
Therefore, when
0\leq\phi^{0}\leq 1.4
, we have
0\leq\phi^{t+1}\leq\phi^{t}\leq\cdots\leq\phi^{0}\leq 1.4
and

\frac{\phi^{t+1}}{\pi}\leq\left[\frac{\phi^{t}}{\pi}\right]^{2}

∎

B-C

Accuracy of Regression Parameters and Mixing Weights in Noiseless Setting

Theorem B.5

(Proposition
14
: Accuracy of EM Updates and Sub-optimality Angle)
.

In the noiseless setting, namely SNR
\eta:=\|\theta^{\ast}\|/\sigma\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\frac{\|\theta^{t}-\textnormal{sgn}(\rho^{0})\theta^{\ast}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\left[\phi^{t-1}\right]^{2}}{2\pi}

\displaystyle\|\pi^{t}-\bar{\pi}^{\ast}\|_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\phi^{t-1}}{\pi}\cdot\left\|\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}

where
\phi^{t-1}:=2(\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho^{t-1}|)
,
\rho^{t-1}:=\frac{\langle\theta^{t-1},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta^{t-1}\|\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
,
and
\bar{\pi}^{\ast}:=\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}+\textnormal{sgn}(\rho^{0})(\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}),\mathds{1}:=(1,1)
.

Proof.

Let’s prove the first inequality by starting from the equations of Cycloid Trajectory parameterized by the angle
\phi^{t-1}
.

\displaystyle\frac{\|\theta^{t}-\textnormal{sgn}(\rho^{0})\theta^{\ast}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}\left\|[\phi^{t-1}-\sin\phi^{t-1}]\hat{e}_{1}+[1-\cos\phi^{t-1}]\hat{e}_{2}^{0}\right\|=\frac{1}{\pi}\left\|\int_{0}^{\phi^{t-1}}([\phi-\cos\phi]\hat{e}_{1}+[\sin\phi]\hat{e}_{2}^{0})\mathrm{d}\phi\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\phi^{t-1}}\left\|[\phi-\cos\phi]\hat{e}_{1}+[\sin\phi]\hat{e}_{2}^{0}\right\|\mathrm{d}\phi=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\phi^{t-1}}2\sin\frac{\phi}{2}\mathrm{d}\phi=\frac{4}{\pi}(1-\cos\frac{\phi^{t-1}}{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{[\phi^{t-1}]^{2}}{2\pi}

In the above derivation, the first inequality is due to the fact that
\|\int_{0}^{\phi^{t}}\vec{v}(\phi)\mathrm{d}\phi\|\leq\int_{0}^{\phi^{t}}\|\vec{v}(\phi)\|\mathrm{d}\phi
for vector
\vec{v}(\phi)
and
\phi^{t-1}\geq 0
,
and the second inequality follows from the inequality
1-\cos x\leq\frac{x^{2}}{2}
.

The proof for the accuracy in mixing weights (the indentity in the second line) is adapted from page 34, Appendix D of
[
1
]
.
Using EM Updates in Noiseless Setting, and note that
\mathrm{sgn}(\rho^{t-1})=\mathrm{sgn}(\rho^{0})
, we obtain that equation.

\tanh(\nu^{t})=\mathrm{sgn}(\rho^{0})\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\varphi^{t-1}\right)\cdot\tanh(\nu^{\ast})

Since
\pi^{t}(1)=\frac{1+\tanh(\nu^{t})}{2},\pi^{t}(2)=\frac{1-\tanh(\nu^{t})}{2}
and

\bar{\pi}^{\ast}(1)=\frac{1+\mathrm{sgn}(\rho^{0})\tanh(\nu^{\ast})}{2},\bar{\pi}^{\ast}(2)=\frac{1-\mathrm{sgn}(\rho^{0})\tanh(\nu^{\ast})}{2}
.

\|\pi^{t}-\bar{\pi}^{\ast}\|_{1}=|\pi^{t}(1)-\bar{\pi}^{\ast}(1)|+|\pi^{t}(2)-\bar{\pi}^{\ast}(2)|=|\tanh(\nu^{t})-\mathrm{sgn}(\rho^{0})\tanh(\nu^{\ast})|=\left|1-\frac{2}{\pi}\varphi^{t-1}\right|\cdot\left\|\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}=\frac{\phi^{t-1}}{\pi}\cdot\left\|\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}

In the above equation, we use such an identity
\tanh(\nu^{\ast})=\left\|\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}

and the fact that
\phi^{t-1}=\pi-2\varphi^{t-1}
.
∎

B-D

Convergence Guarantee of EM Updates at the Population Level in Noiseless Setting

Theorem B.6

(Theorem
15
: Population Level Convergence, Theorem 4.1 in
[
1
]
)
.

In the noiseless setting, namely SNR
\eta:=\|\theta^{\ast}\|/\sigma\to\infty
, if the initial sup-optimality angle cosine
\rho^{0}:=\frac{\langle\theta^{0},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta^{0}\|\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\neq 0
,
then with the number of total iterations at most
T=\mathcal{O}(\log\frac{1}{|\rho^{0}|}\vee\log\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon})
, the error of EM update at the population level is bounded by:

\frac{\|\theta^{T+1}-\textnormal{sgn}(\rho^{0})\theta^{\ast}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}<\varepsilon
and
\|\pi^{T+1}-\bar{\pi}^{\ast}\|_{1}=\left\|\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\varepsilon})
.
where
\bar{\pi}^{\ast}:=\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}+\textnormal{sgn}(\rho^{0})(\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}),\mathds{1}:=(1,1)
.

Proof.

We provide a new straightforward proof for the convergence gaurantee of EM updates at the population level in the noiseless setting,
by invoking the results for accuracy in regeressioon parameters and mixing weights,
and applying the linear growth and quadratic convergence of the sub-optimality angles.
The other old lengthy proof of this Theorem 4.1 of Population Level Convergence in
[
1
]
can be found on pages 34-35, Appendix D of
[
1
]
.

Since when the sub-optimality angle
\phi^{t}>1.4
is large, the other sub-optimality angle
\varphi^{t}=\frac{\pi}{2}-\frac{\phi^{t}}{2}\leq\frac{\pi}{2}-0.7\approx 0.8708<1
is also small enough.
Suppose the initial sub-optimality angle
\varphi^{0}<1
,
then after at most
T_{1}:=\lceil(\ln\tan 1+\ln\frac{1}{|\rho^{0}|})/\ln 2\rceil_{+}=\Theta(\log\frac{1}{|\rho^{0}|})
EM iterations, the sub-optimality angle
\phi^{t}\leq 1.4,\forall t\geq T_{1}
will be sufficiently small enough.

\displaystyle\tan\varphi^{t}\geq\tan\varphi^{T_{1}}\geq 2^{T_{1}}\tan\varphi^{0}\geq\tan 1\cdot\frac{1}{|\rho^{0}|}\cdot\tan\varphi^{0}=\tan 1\cdot\frac{1}{\sin\varphi^{0}}\cdot\frac{\sin\varphi^{0}}{\cos\varphi^{0}}\geq\tan 1\quad\forall t\geq T_{1}

\displaystyle\implies\varphi^{t}\geq 1\implies\phi^{t}=2\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\varphi^{t}\right)<1.4\quad\forall t\geq T_{1}

Otherwise, if the initial sub-optimality angle
\varphi^{0}\geq 1
, then
T_{1}=0
and we still have
\phi^{t}<1.4,\forall t\geq T_{1}
.
Then by the quadratic convergence of the sub-optimality angle
\phi^{t}
when
\phi^{t}<1.4
is small enough,
then after at most
T_{2}:=\lceil(\ln\ln\frac{\pi}{2\varepsilon}-\ln 2-\ln\ln\frac{\pi}{1.4})/\ln 2\rceil_{+}=\Theta(\log\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon})
, we have the following inequality:

\displaystyle\phi^{T_{1}+T_{2}}\leq\pi\left(\frac{\phi^{T_{1}}}{\pi}\right)^{2^{T_{2}}}<\pi\left(\frac{1.4}{\pi}\right)^{2^{T_{2}}}\leq\pi\left(\frac{1.4}{\pi}\right)^{\frac{\frac{1}{2}\ln\frac{\pi}{2\varepsilon}}{\ln\frac{\pi}{1.4}}}=\pi\left(\frac{\pi}{2\varepsilon}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}=\sqrt{2\pi\varepsilon}

Therefore, after at most
T:=T_{1}+T_{2}=\Theta(\log\frac{1}{|\rho^{0}|}\vee\log\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon})
EM iterations, we have
\phi^{T}<\sqrt{2\pi\varepsilon}
for all
t\geq T
.
By using the results for accuracy in regeressioon parameters and mixing weights, we have the following bounds:

\frac{\|\theta^{T+1}-\textnormal{sgn}(\rho^{0})\theta^{\ast}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\leq\frac{[\phi^{T}]^{2}}{2\pi}<\varepsilon,\quad\|\pi^{T+1}-\bar{\pi}^{\ast}\|_{1}=\frac{\phi^{T}}{\pi}\cdot\left\|\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}<\sqrt{\frac{2\varepsilon}{\pi}}\cdot\left\|\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}=\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\varepsilon})\cdot\left\|\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}

where the last inequality of both bounds follows from
\phi^{T}<\sqrt{2\pi\varepsilon}
.
∎

Appendix C
Finite-Sample Level Analysis in Noiseless Setting

C-A

Statistical Error, Statistical Accuracy of EM Updates in Noiseless Setting

Theorem C.1

(Proposition
16
: Projected Error of Easy EM Update for Regression Parameters, Proposition 5.2 in
[
1
]
)
.

In the noiseless setting, the projection on
\text{span}\{\theta,\theta^{\ast}\}
for the statistical error of
\theta
satisfies

\frac{\|P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}[M_{n}^{\operatorname{easy}}(\theta,\nu)-M(\theta,\nu)]\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\vee\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right),

with probability at least
1-\delta
, where
M_{n}(\theta,\nu),M(\theta,\nu)
are the EM update rules for
\theta
at the Finite-sample level and the
population level respectively, and the orthogonal projection matrix
P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}
satisfies

\text{span}(P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}})=\text{span}\{\theta,\theta^{\ast}\}

.

Proof.

We provide a new straightfowrd proof here.
In the noiseless setting, as SNR
\eta=\frac{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}{\sigma}\to\infty
, we have
y_{i}\to(-1)^{z_{i}+1}\langle x_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle,\tanh(y_{i}\langle x_{i},\theta\rangle/\sigma^{2}+\nu)\to(-1)^{z_{i}+1}\textnormal{sgn}(\langle x_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle\langle x_{i},\theta\rangle)

for the
i
-th data point
z_{i},s_{i}=(x_{i},y_{i})
.

M^{\operatorname{easy}}_{n}(\theta,\nu)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\textnormal{sgn}\left(\langle x_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle\langle x_{i},\theta\rangle\right)\langle x_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle x_{i}

Without loss of generality, we assume
\dim\text{span}\{\theta,\theta^{\ast}\}=2
and therefore
\dim\text{span}\{\theta,\theta^{\ast}\}^{\perp}=d-2
.

We introduce
\rho:=\frac{\langle\theta,\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
,
and define
\hat{e}_{1}:=\frac{\theta^{\ast}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
,
\vec{e}_{1}:=\frac{\theta}{\|\theta\|}
,
and
\hat{e}_{2}:=\frac{\theta-\langle\theta,\hat{e}_{1}\rangle\hat{e}_{1}}{\|\theta-\langle\theta,\hat{e}_{1}\rangle\hat{e}_{1}\|}
,

\vec{e}_{2}:=\frac{\theta^{\ast}-\langle\theta^{\ast},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\vec{e}_{1}}{\|\theta^{\ast}-\langle\theta^{\ast},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\vec{e}_{1}\|}
.
Also, let
P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}=\vec{e}_{1}\vec{e}_{1}^{\top}+\vec{e}_{2}\vec{e}_{2}^{\top}
,
which can be expressed with orthonormal basis matrices
U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}=(\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\vec{e}_{1},\vec{e}_{2})

such that
P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}=U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\top}
.
Also, we have
\varphi=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho|
, therefore
\rho=\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\sin\varphi
.
By defining
g_{i}:=\langle x_{i},\hat{e}_{1}\rangle,g^{\prime}_{i}:=\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\langle x_{i},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle
,
then
g_{i},g^{\prime}_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
with
\mathbb{E}[g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i}]=\sin\varphi
, and
h^{\prime}_{i}=\langle x_{i},\vec{e}_{2}\rangle\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
satisfies
g_{i}=\sin\varphi\cdot g^{\prime}_{i}+\cos\varphi\cdot h^{\prime}_{i},h^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}g^{\prime}_{i}
.

\displaystyle U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\top}\frac{M_{n}^{\operatorname{easy}}(\theta,\nu)}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i}|,\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\textnormal{sgn}(g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i})g_{i}h^{\prime}_{i}\right)^{\top}

\displaystyle\frac{\|P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}[M_{n}^{\operatorname{easy}}(\theta,\nu)-M(\theta,\nu)]\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\left\|U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\top}\frac{[M_{n}^{\operatorname{easy}}(\theta,\nu)-M(\theta,\nu)]}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\right\|=\left\|U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\top}\frac{M_{n}^{\operatorname{easy}}(\theta,\nu)}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}-\mathbb{E}\left[U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\top}\frac{M^{\operatorname{easy}}(\theta,\nu)}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\left[\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\right)|g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i}|\right]^{2}+\left[\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\right)\textnormal{sgn}(g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i})g_{i}h^{\prime}_{i}\right]^{2}}

where
