|g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i}|,\textnormal{sgn}(g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i})g_{i}h^{\prime}_{i}
are i.i.d. Sub-Exponential random variables by Proposition 2.7.1 part (b) on page 33 of
[
52
]
and noting that for
\forall q\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,

\mathbb{E}\left[(|g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i}|-\mathbb{E}[|g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i}|])^{q}\right]^{1/q}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i}|^{q}\right]^{1/q}+\mathbb{E}[|g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i}|]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{g_{i}^{2q}+(g^{\prime}_{i})^{2q}}{2}\right]^{1/q}+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{g_{i}^{2}+(g^{\prime}_{i})^{2}}{2}\right]\leq 2\mathrm{e}q+1\leq 3\mathrm{e}q,

\mathbb{E}\left[(\textnormal{sgn}(g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i})g_{i}h^{\prime}_{i}-\mathbb{E}[\textnormal{sgn}(g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i})g_{i}h^{\prime}_{i}])^{q}\right]^{1/q}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|g_{i}h^{\prime}_{i}|^{q}\right]^{1/q}+|\mathbb{E}[\textnormal{sgn}(g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i})g_{i}h^{\prime}_{i}]|\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|g_{i}h^{\prime}_{i}|^{q}\right]^{1/q}+\mathbb{E}[|g_{i}h^{\prime}_{i}|]\leq 2\mathrm{e}q+1\leq 3\mathrm{e}q,

where the first inequality follows from Minkowski’s inequality, and the second last inequality is due to the facts for moment of Gaussian random variables
g_{i},g^{\prime}_{i},h^{\prime}_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.
Consequently, by Bernstein’s inequality (see Corollary 2.8.3 on page 38 of
[
52
]
) for Sub-Exponential random variables
|g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i}|,\textnormal{sgn}(g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i})g_{i}h^{\prime}_{i}
,
with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\frac{\left\|P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}[M_{n}^{\text{easy}}(\theta,\nu)-M(\theta,\nu)]\right\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\leq\left|\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\right)|g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i}|\right|+\left|\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\right)\textnormal{sgn}(g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i})g_{i}h^{\prime}_{i}\right|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\vee\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}

∎

Theorem C.2

(Proposition
17
: Statistical Error of EM Update for Regression Parameters, Proposition 5.3 in
[
1
]
)
.

In the noiseless setting, the statistical error of
\theta
for finite-sample EM
updates with
n\gtrsim d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}
samples satisfies

\frac{\|M_{n}^{\operatorname{easy}}(\theta,\nu)-M(\theta,\nu)\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right),\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{\|M_{n}(\theta,\nu)-M(\theta,\nu)\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right),

with probability at least
1-\delta
,
M_{n}(\theta,\nu),M(\theta,\nu)
denote the EM update rules for
\theta
at the Finite-sample level and the
Population level.

Proof.

Let the ensemble matrix of data samples be
\mathsf{\Sigma}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}x_{i}^{\top}
, and
note that
M(\theta,\nu)=\mathbb{E}\left[M_{n}^{\operatorname{easy}}(\theta,\nu)\right]
,
by letting
\vec{v}=\left(M_{n}^{\operatorname{easy}}(\theta,\nu)-M(\theta,\nu)\right)/\|\theta^{\ast}\|=\left(M_{n}^{\operatorname{easy}}(\theta,\nu)-\mathbb{E}[M^{\operatorname{easy}}(\theta,\nu)]\right)/\|\theta^{\ast}\|
, we have

\frac{M_{n}(\theta,\nu)-M(\theta,\nu)}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\mathsf{\Sigma}^{-1}\vec{v}-\mathsf{\Sigma}^{-1}(\mathsf{\Sigma}-I_{d})\frac{M(\theta,\nu)}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}

Without loss of generality, we assume
\dim\text{span}\{\theta,\theta^{\ast}\}=2
and therefore
\dim\text{span}\{\theta,\theta^{\ast}\}^{\perp}=d-2
.
Let
P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp}=\sum_{j=1}^{d-2}\vec{e}_{j}^{\perp}(\vec{e}_{j}^{\perp})^{\top}

and
U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp}=\left(\vec{e}_{1}^{\perp},\ldots,\vec{e}_{d-2}^{\perp}\right)
with
\text{span}(U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp})=\text{span}\{\theta,\theta^{\ast}\}^{\perp}
.
For the length of vector
\vec{v}
, noting that orthogonal projection matrice
P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp}

can be expressed with orthonormal basis matrix
U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp}

such that
P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp}=U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp}(U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp})^{\top}
, we have

\|\vec{v}\|^{2}=\langle(P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}+P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp})\vec{v},(P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}+P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp})\vec{v}\rangle=\langle\vec{v},(P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}+P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp})\vec{v}\rangle=\langle\vec{v},P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}\vec{v}\rangle+\langle\vec{v},P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp}\vec{v}\rangle=\|P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}\vec{v}\|^{2}+\|(U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp})^{\top}\vec{v}\|^{2}

where the first term is bounded by
\|P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}\vec{v}\|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\vee\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}
,
and we rewrite the second term by introducing independent Gaussian variables

g_{i}:=\langle x_{i},\theta^{\ast}/\|\theta^{\ast}\|\rangle\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1),\vec{g}_{i}:=\textnormal{sgn}(\langle x_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle\langle x_{i},\theta\rangle)(U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp})^{\top}x_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d-2})
with
g_{i}\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\vec{g}_{i}
.

\|(U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp})^{\top}\vec{v}\|^{2}=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\vec{g}_{i}\right\|^{2}=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}^{2}\right)\cdot\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{g_{i}}{\sqrt{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}(g_{i^{\prime}})^{2}}}\vec{g}_{i}\right\|^{2}=\frac{1}{n^{2}}Z_{1}\cdot Z_{2}

where
Z_{1}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}^{2}\sim\chi^{2}(n)
and
the weighted sum
\sum_{i=1}^{n}(g_{i}/\sqrt{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}(g_{i^{\prime}})^{2}})\vec{g}_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d-2})
by the rotational invariance of Gaussian distribution,
therefore
Z_{2}=\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}(g_{i}/\sqrt{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}(g_{i^{\prime}})^{2}})\vec{g}_{i}\|^{2}\sim\chi^{2}(d-2)
with
Z_{1}\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}Z_{2}
.
By the concentration inequality for Chi-square distribution (see Lemma 1, page 1325 in
[
53
]
), given
n\geq d
, with probability at least
1-\delta/2
,

\|\vec{v}\|\leq\|P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}\vec{v}\|+\frac{1}{n}\sqrt{Z_{1}\cdot Z_{2}}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\vee\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}+\frac{1}{n}\sqrt{\left(n\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\left(d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\vee\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}

From the assumption
n\gtrsim d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}
, by Example 6.2 (Operator norm bounds for the standard Gaussian ensemble) and Example 6.3 (Gaussian covariance estimation) on page 162 of
[
60
]
, with probability at least
1-\delta/2
, we have

\gamma_{\min}(\mathsf{\Sigma})\asymp 1,\quad\left\|\mathsf{\Sigma}-I_{d}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}

where
\gamma_{\min}
represents the minimum eigenvalue, and
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{2}
is the
\ell_{2}
-operator norm for matrix.
Noting that
\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\gtrsim\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}
when
n\gtrsim d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}
and
\|M(\theta,\nu)\|/\|\theta^{\ast}\|\leq 1
in the noiseless setting,
and putting the above results together, we have

\frac{\|M_{n}(\theta,\nu)-M(\theta,\nu)\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\leq\frac{\|\vec{v}\|}{\gamma_{\min}(\mathsf{\Sigma})}+\frac{\left\|\mathsf{\Sigma}-I_{d}\right\|_{2}}{\gamma_{\min}(\mathsf{\Sigma})}\frac{\|M(\theta,\nu)\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}

∎

Theorem C.3

(Proposition
18
: Statistical Error of EM Update for Mixing Weights)
.

In the noiseless setting, the statistical error of mixing weights for finite-sample EM updates
satisfies

\left|N_{n}(\theta,\nu)-N(\theta,\nu)\right|=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}/n}{\log\left(1+\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}/n}{p}\right)}\wedge\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right)

with probability at least
1-\delta
, where
N_{n}(\theta,\nu),N(\theta,\nu)
denote the EM update rules for imbalance
\tanh\nu
of the mixing weights at the Finite-sample level and the Population level,
and
p:=\|\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\|_{1}\frac{\phi}{2\pi}+\min(\pi^{\ast}(1),\pi^{\ast}(2))
,
\phi=2\arccos|\rho|,\rho=\frac{\langle\theta,\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
.

Proof.

In the noiseless setting, as SNR
\eta\rightarrow\infty
, we have
y_{i}\rightarrow(-1)^{z_{i}+1}\langle x_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle
and therefore
\tanh(y_{i}\langle x_{i},\theta\rangle/\sigma^{2}+\nu)\to(-1)^{z_{i}+1}\textnormal{sgn}(\langle x_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle\langle x_{i},\theta\rangle)

for the
i
-th data point
z_{i},s_{i}=(x_{i},y_{i})
.

N_{n}(\theta,\nu)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(-1)^{z_{i}+1}\textnormal{sgn}(\langle x_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle\langle x_{i},\theta\rangle)

Let
\rho=\frac{\langle\theta,\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
,
and
\varphi=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos|\rho|,\phi=2\arccos|\rho|
, therefore
\rho=\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\sin\varphi
.
By defining
g_{i}=\langle x_{i},\theta^{\ast}/\|\theta^{\ast}\|\rangle,g^{\prime}_{i}=\langle x_{i},\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\theta/\|\theta\|\rangle
,
and introducing
V_{i}=(1+\textnormal{sgn}(\nu^{\ast})(-1)^{z_{i}+1}\textnormal{sgn}(g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i}))/2
, noting
N(\theta,\nu)=\mathbb{E}[N_{n}(\theta,\nu)]
, we have

\left|N_{n}(\theta,\nu)-N(\theta,\nu)\right|=2\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(V_{i}-\mathbb{E}[V_{i}])\right|

with the i.i.d. random variables
\{V_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\text{Bern}(q)
following Bernoulli distribution with parameter
q\geq\frac{1}{2}
such that

q=\mathbb{E}[V_{i}]=\frac{1}{2}\left(1+\textnormal{sgn}(\nu^{\ast})\mathbb{E}[(-1)^{z_{i}+1}]\mathbb{E}[\textnormal{sgn}(g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i})]\right)=\frac{1}{2}+\tanh|\nu^{\ast}|\cdot\frac{\varphi}{\pi}

by using the facts that
\mathbb{E}[(-1)^{z_{i}+1}]=\tanh\nu^{\ast}
and
\mathbb{E}[\textnormal{sgn}(g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i})]=\frac{2}{\pi}\varphi
(see Lemma
E.2
).
Then, we can express the
1-q
with
\phi=\pi-2\varphi
and
\|\pi-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\|_{1}=\tanh|\nu^{\ast}|,\min(\pi^{\ast}(1),\pi^{\ast}(2))=\frac{1-\tanh|\nu^{\ast}|}{2}

1-q=\frac{\pi-\tanh|\nu^{\ast}|\cdot 2\varphi}{2\pi}=\left\|\pi-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}\cdot\frac{\phi}{2\pi}+\min(\pi^{\ast}(1),\pi^{\ast}(2))

By Lemma
E.7
of concentration inequality for Bernoulli distribution, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\left|N_{n}(\theta,\nu)-N(\theta,\nu)\right|=2\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(V_{i}-\mathbb{E}[V_{i}])\right|\lesssim\min\left(\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}/n}{\log\left(1+\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}/n}{1-q}\right)},\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right)

We complete the proof by introducing
p:=1-q=\|\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\|_{1}\frac{\phi}{2\pi}+\min(\pi^{\ast}(1),\pi^{\ast}(2))\asymp\tanh|\nu^{\ast}|\phi+(1-\tanh|\nu^{\ast}|)
.
∎

Lemma C.4

(Projected Statistical Accuracy of Easy EM Update for Regression Parameters)
.

In the noiseless setting, the projection on
\text{span}\{\theta,\theta^{\ast}\}
for the statistical accuracy of regression parameters
\theta
satisfies

\frac{\left\|P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}\left[\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)M^{\operatorname{easy}}_{n}(\theta,\nu)-M^{\operatorname{easy}}_{n}(\theta^{\ast},\nu^{\ast})\right]\right\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\mathcal{O}\left(\phi^{2}\vee\phi\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right)

with probability at least
1-\delta
, where
M^{\operatorname{easy}}_{n}(\theta,\nu)
denotes the Easy EM update rule for regression parameters at the Finite-sample level,
orthogonal projection matrix
P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}
satisfies
\text{span}(P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}})=\text{span}\{\theta,\theta^{\ast}\}
,
and
\phi=2\arccos|\rho|,\rho=\frac{\langle\theta,\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume
\dim\text{span}\{\theta,\theta^{\ast}\}=2
and therefore
\dim\text{span}\{\theta,\theta^{\ast}\}^{\perp}=d-2
.
We introduce
\rho:=\frac{\langle\theta,\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
,
and define
\hat{e}_{1}:=\frac{\theta^{\ast}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}
,
\vec{e}_{1}:=\frac{\theta}{\|\theta\|}
,
and
\hat{e}_{2}:=\frac{\theta-\langle\theta,\hat{e}_{1}\rangle\hat{e}_{1}}{\|\theta-\langle\theta,\hat{e}_{1}\rangle\hat{e}_{1}\|}
,

\vec{e}_{2}:=\frac{\theta^{\ast}-\langle\theta^{\ast},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\vec{e}_{1}}{\|\theta^{\ast}-\langle\theta^{\ast},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle\vec{e}_{1}\|}
.
Also, let
P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}=\hat{e}_{1}\hat{e}_{1}^{\top}+\hat{e}_{2}\hat{e}_{2}^{\top}
,
which can be expressed with orthonormal basis matrices
U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}=(\hat{e}_{1},\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\hat{e}_{2})

such that
P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}=U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\top}
.
Also, we have
\phi=2\arccos|\rho|
, therefore
\rho=\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\cos\frac{\phi}{2}
.
By defining
g_{i}:=\langle x_{i},\hat{e}_{1}\rangle,g^{\prime}_{i}:=\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\langle x_{i},\vec{e}_{1}\rangle
,
then
g_{i},g^{\prime}_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
with
\mathbb{E}[g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i}]=\cos\frac{\phi}{2}
, and
h_{i}=\langle x_{i},\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\hat{e}_{2}\rangle\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
satisfies
g^{\prime}_{i}=\cos\frac{\phi}{2}\cdot g_{i}+\sin\frac{\phi}{2}\cdot h_{i},h_{i}\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}g_{i}
.

\displaystyle-U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\top}\frac{\left[\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)M^{\operatorname{easy}}_{n}(\theta,\nu)-M^{\operatorname{easy}}_{n}(\theta^{\ast},\nu^{\ast})\right]}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[1-\textnormal{sgn}(g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i})]g_{i}^{2},-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[\textnormal{sgn}(g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i})-1]g_{i}h_{i}\right)^{\top}

Applying Proposition
14
of Errors of EM Updates and Sub-optimality Angle, and
\mathbb{E}[M^{\operatorname{easy}}_{n}(\theta,\nu)]=M(\theta,\nu),\mathbb{E}[M^{\operatorname{easy}}_{n}(\theta^{\ast},\nu^{\ast})]=\theta^{\ast}\in\text{span}\{\theta,\theta^{\ast}\}=\text{span}(U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}})
, then expectation
\mathbb{E}[U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\top}\vec{v}]
of
\vec{v}:=[\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)M^{\operatorname{easy}}_{n}(\theta,\nu)-M^{\operatorname{easy}}_{n}(\theta^{\ast},\nu^{\ast})]/\|\theta^{\ast}\|
is bounded:

\|\mathbb{E}[U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\top}\vec{v}]\|=\left\|U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\top}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)M^{\operatorname{easy}}_{n}(\theta,\nu)-M^{\operatorname{easy}}_{n}(\theta^{\ast},\nu^{\ast})\right]}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\right\|=\|\mathbb{E}[\vec{v}]\|=\frac{\|M(\theta,\nu)-\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\theta^{\ast}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\leq\frac{\phi^{2}}{2\pi}

Applying
\sqrt{(s+s^{\prime})^{2}+(t+t^{\prime})^{2}}\leq\sqrt{s^{2}+t^{2}}+\sqrt{2}\max(s^{\prime},t^{\prime})_{+}
for
s+s^{\prime},t+t^{\prime}\geq 0
,
noting
P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}=U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\top}
:

\|P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}\vec{v}\|=\|U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\top}\vec{v}\|\lesssim\phi^{2}+\max\left(\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\right)[1-\textnormal{sgn}(g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i})]g_{i}^{2},\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\right)[\textnormal{sgn}(g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i})-1]g_{i}h_{i}\right)_{+}

By introducing
\{\varrho_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}=\{R_{i}^{2}/2\}_{i=1}^{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\operatorname{Exp}(1)
with
\{R_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}r\exp(r^{2}/2)\mathds{1}_{r\geq 0}
(the standard Rayleigh distribution),

\{V_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\operatorname{Unif}[0,2\pi)
, and
U_{i}:=2V_{i}-\pi\mod(2\pi)\sim\operatorname{Unif}[0,2\pi)
,
we express
g_{i}=R_{i}\cos V_{i},h_{i}=R_{i}\sin V_{i}
and follows:

X_{i}:=[1-\textnormal{sgn}(g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i})]g_{i}^{2}=2\varrho_{i}(1-\cos U_{i})\mathds{1}_{U_{i}\in[0,\phi)}\geq 0,\quad Y_{i}:=[\textnormal{sgn}(g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i})-1]g_{i}h_{i}=2\varrho_{i}\sin U_{i}\mathds{1}_{U_{i}\in[0,\phi)}\geq 0

Noting
\mathbb{E}[\varrho_{i}^{q}]=q!
for
q\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, and
2(1-\cos U_{i})\mathds{1}_{U_{i}\in[0,\phi)}\leq\phi^{2}\mathds{1}_{U_{i}\in[0,\phi)},2\sin U_{i}\leq 2\phi\mathds{1}_{U_{i}\in[0,\phi)}
, then for
q\geq 3
:

\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{2}]\leq 2![\phi^{2}]^{2}\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{U_{i}\in[0,\phi)}]=\frac{\phi^{5}}{\pi},\quad\mathbb{E}[X_{i}^{q}]\leq q![\phi^{2}]^{q}\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{U_{i}\in[0,\phi)}]=\frac{q!\phi^{2q+1}}{2\pi}=\frac{q!}{2}\frac{\phi^{5}}{\pi}[\phi^{2}]^{q-2}

\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}^{2}]\leq 2![2\phi]^{2}\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{U_{i}\in[0,\phi)}]=\frac{4\phi^{3}}{\pi},\quad\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}^{q}]\leq q![2\phi]^{q}\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{U_{i}\in[0,\phi)}]=\frac{q!2^{q}\phi^{q+1}}{2\pi}=\frac{q!}{2}\frac{4\phi^{3}}{\pi}[2\phi]^{q-2}

By Bernstein’s inequality (see Theorem 2.10 on page 37 of
[
61
]
) with probability at least
1-\delta
, noting
\phi^{2}\lesssim\phi
, we have:

\max\left(\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\right)X_{i},\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}-\mathbb{E}\right)Y_{i}\right)_{+}\lesssim\max\left(\phi^{\frac{5}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}+\phi^{2}\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n},\phi^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}+\phi\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right)\asymp\phi^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}+\phi\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}

Therefore, we complete the proof by using
a+\sqrt{ab}+b\asymp a+b\asymp a\vee b
for
a,b\geq 0
.
∎

Theorem C.5

(Proposition
19
: Statistical Accuracy of EM Updates for Regression Parameters and Mixing Weights)
.

In the noiseless setting, the finite-sample EM with
n\gtrsim d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}
samples achieves the statistical accuracy of regression parameters and mixing weights:

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|M_{n}(\theta,\nu)-\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\theta^{\ast}\right\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\phi^{2}\vee\phi^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\vee\phi\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\left|N_{n}(\theta,\nu)-\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\tanh\nu^{\ast}\right|

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\phi\left\|\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}\vee\left[\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}/n}{\log\left(1+\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}/n}{p}\right)}\wedge\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right]\right)

with probability at least
1-\delta
, where
M_{n}(\theta,\nu)
denotes the EM update rule for regression parameters at the Finite-sample level,
and
\phi:=2\arccos|\rho|,\rho:=\frac{\langle\theta,\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}

and
p:=\frac{\phi}{2\pi}\|\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\|_{1}+\min(\pi^{\ast}(1),\pi^{\ast}(2))
.

Proof.

Noting
\mathsf{\Sigma}M_{n}(\theta,\nu)=M_{n}^{\text{easy}}(\theta,\nu)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\textnormal{sgn}(\langle x_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle\langle x_{i},\theta\rangle)\langle x_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle x_{i}
in the noiseless setting,
with the ensemble matrix of data samples
\mathsf{\Sigma}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}x_{i}^{\top}
, and letting
\vec{v}:=[\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)M^{\operatorname{easy}}_{n}(\theta,\nu)-M^{\operatorname{easy}}_{n}(\theta^{\ast},\nu^{\ast})]/\|\theta^{\ast}\|
, then:

\frac{M_{n}(\theta,\nu)-\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\theta^{\ast}}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\mathsf{\Sigma}^{-1}\cdot\vec{v}

Let
P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp}=\sum_{j=1}^{d-2}\hat{e}_{j}^{\perp}(\hat{e}_{j}^{\perp})^{\top}

and
U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp}=\left(\hat{e}_{1}^{\perp},\ldots,\hat{e}_{d-2}^{\perp}\right)
with
\text{span}(U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp})=\text{span}\{\theta,\theta^{\ast}\}^{\perp}
.
For the length of vector
\vec{v}
, noting that orthogonal projection matrice
P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp}

can be expressed with orthonormal basis matrix
U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp}

such that
P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp}=U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp}(U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp})^{\top}
.

\|\vec{v}\|^{2}=\langle(P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}+P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp})\vec{v},(P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}+P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp})\vec{v}\rangle=\langle\vec{v},(P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}+P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp})\vec{v}\rangle=\langle\vec{v},P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}\vec{v}\rangle+\langle\vec{v},P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp}\vec{v}\rangle=\|P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}\vec{v}\|^{2}+\|(U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp})^{\top}\vec{v}\|^{2}

where the first term is bounded by
\|P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}\vec{v}\|\lesssim\phi^{2}\vee\phi\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
,
and we rewrite the second term by introducing Gaussian r.v.

g_{i}:=\langle x_{i},\theta^{\ast}/\|\theta^{\ast}\|\rangle,g^{\prime}_{i}:=\langle x_{i},\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\theta/\|\theta\|\rangle\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1),\vec{g}_{i}:=\textnormal{sgn}(\langle x_{i},\theta^{\ast}\rangle\langle x_{i},\theta\rangle)(U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp})^{\top}x_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d-2})
with
g_{i},g^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\vec{g}_{i}
.

\|(U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp})^{\top}\vec{v}\|^{2}=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}[1-\textnormal{sgn}(g^{\prime}_{i}g_{i})]g_{i}\vec{g}_{i}\right\|^{2}=\frac{2}{n^{2}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}[1-\textnormal{sgn}(g^{\prime}_{i}g_{i})]g_{i}^{2}\right)\cdot\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{[1-\textnormal{sgn}(g^{\prime}_{i}g_{i})]g_{i}}{\sqrt{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}2[1-\textnormal{sgn}(g^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}}g_{i^{\prime}})](g_{i^{\prime}})^{2}}}\vec{g}_{i}\right\|^{2}

where the weighted sum
\sum_{i=1}^{n}([1-\textnormal{sgn}(g^{\prime}_{i}g_{i})]g_{i}/\sqrt{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}2[1-\textnormal{sgn}(g^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}}g_{i^{\prime}})](g_{i^{\prime}})^{2}})\vec{g}_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d-2})
by the rotational invariance of Gaussian distribution,
therefore
\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}([1-\textnormal{sgn}(g^{\prime}_{i}g_{i})]g_{i}/\sqrt{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}2[1-\textnormal{sgn}(g^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}}g_{i^{\prime}})](g_{i^{\prime}})^{2}})\vec{g}_{i}\|^{2}\sim\chi^{2}(d-2)
.
By the concentration inequality for Chi-square distribution (see Lemma 1, page 1325 in
[
53
]
), then:

\frac{1}{n}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{[1-\textnormal{sgn}(g^{\prime}_{i}g_{i})]g_{i}}{\sqrt{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}2[1-\textnormal{sgn}(g^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}}g_{i^{\prime}})](g_{i^{\prime}})^{2}}}\vec{g}_{i}\right\|^{2}\lesssim\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}

Letting
\{\varrho_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}=\{R_{i}^{2}/2\}_{i=1}^{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\operatorname{Exp}(1)
with
\{R_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}r\exp(r^{2}/2)\mathds{1}_{r\geq 0}
(the standard Rayleigh distribution),

\{V_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\operatorname{Unif}[0,2\pi)
, and
U_{i}:=2V_{i}-\pi\mod(2\pi)\sim\operatorname{Unif}[0,2\pi)
,
we express
g_{i}=R_{i}\cos V_{i},h_{i}=R_{i}\sin V_{i},g^{\prime}_{i}=\cos\frac{\phi}{2}\cdot g_{i}+\sin\frac{\phi}{2}\cdot h_{i}
.

X_{i}:=[1-\textnormal{sgn}(g_{i}g^{\prime}_{i})]g_{i}^{2}=2\varrho_{i}(1-\cos U_{i})\mathds{1}_{U_{i}\in[0,\phi)}\geq 0,\quad\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\leq\mathbb{E}[\varrho_{i}]\mathbb{E}[\phi^{2}\mathds{1}_{U_{i}\in[0,\phi)}]=\frac{\phi^{3}}{2\pi}

By the concentration inequality for
X_{i}
obtained in proof of previous Lemma,
(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}-\mathbb{E})X_{i}\lesssim\phi^{\frac{5}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}+\phi^{2}\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}
, then:

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[1-\textnormal{sgn}(g^{\prime}_{i}g_{i})]g_{i}^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\lesssim\phi^{3}+\phi^{\frac{5}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}+\phi^{2}\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\asymp\phi^{2}\left(\phi\vee\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right)

By combining the above inequalities together, we bound
\vec{v}
as follows with
n\gtrsim d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}
and probability at least
1-\delta/4
.

\|\vec{v}\|\leq\|P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}\vec{v}\|+\|(U_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}^{\perp})^{\top}\vec{v}\|\lesssim\phi^{2}\vee\phi\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}+\sqrt{\phi^{2}\left(\phi\vee\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}\right)\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\asymp\phi^{2}\vee\phi^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\vee\phi\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}

From the assumption
n\gtrsim d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}
, by Example 6.2 (Operator norm bounds for the standard Gaussian ensemble) and Example 6.3 (Gaussian covariance estimation) on page 162 of
[
60
]
, with probability at least
1-\delta/4
, we have

\gamma_{\min}(\mathsf{\Sigma})\asymp 1
,
where
\gamma_{\min}
represents the minimum eigenvalue.

\frac{\left\|M_{n}(\theta,\nu)-\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\theta^{\ast}\right\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\leq\frac{\|\vec{v}\|}{\gamma_{\min}(\mathsf{\Sigma})}\asymp\phi^{2}\vee\phi^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\vee\phi\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}

For the mixing weights, by using the Triangle inequality, Proposition for error of mixing weights in population level,
and the Proposition for statistical error of mixing weights in finite-sample level, we have the following with probability at least
1-\delta/2
.

\displaystyle\left|N_{n}(\theta,\nu)-\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\tanh\nu^{\ast}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|N(\theta,\nu)-\textnormal{sgn}(\rho)\tanh\nu^{\ast}\right|+\left|N_{n}(\theta,\nu)-N(\theta,\nu)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\phi\left\|\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}+\min\left(\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}/n}{\log\left(1+\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}/n}{p}\right)},\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right)

where
p:=\|\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\|_{1}\frac{\phi}{2\pi}+\min(\pi^{\ast}(1),\pi^{\ast}(2))\asymp\tanh|\nu^{\ast}|\phi+(1-\tanh|\nu^{\ast}|)
.
∎

C-B

Initialization of Easy EM Updates and Perturbation in Sub-Optimality Angle

Theorem C.6

(Proposition
20
: Initialization with Easy EM, Proposition 5.4 in
[
1
]
)
.

In the noiseless setting, suppose we run the sample-splitting finite-sample
Easy EM with
n^{\prime}\asymp\frac{n}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}
fresh samples for each iteration, then after at most
T_{0}=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)
iterations, it satisfies
\varphi^{T_{0}}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}

with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Proof.

See the detailed proof on pages 41-44, Appendix E of
[
1
]
based on Berry-Esseen bound for the central limit theorem (see Theorem 1.1 of
[
62
]
).
The proposition is restated here for completeness and to keep the paper self-contained.
∎

Lemma C.7

(Perturbation in Sub-Optimality Angle)
.

For
\vartheta:=\sin\varphi\hat{e}_{1}+\cos\varphi\hat{e}_{2}
,
where
\{\hat{e}_{1},\hat{e}_{2}\}
is an orthonormal basis for the subspace

\operatorname{span}\{\hat{e}_{1},\hat{e}_{2}\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
\varphi\in\left(0,\frac{\pi}{2}\right)
; with a pertubation vector
\varrho\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with lengh
\|\varrho\|=r\in(0,\sin\varphi)
; then the
angle
\varphi^{\prime}:=\arcsin\frac{|\langle\vartheta+\varrho,\hat{e}_{1}\rangle|}{\|\vartheta+\varrho\|}
, satisfies
\varphi^{\prime}\geq\varphi-\arcsin r

Proof.

Note that with
\|\varrho\|=r\in(0,\sin\varphi)
,
\langle\vartheta+\varrho,\hat{e}_{1}\rangle=\langle\vartheta,\hat{e}_{1}\rangle+\langle\varrho,\hat{e}_{1}\rangle\geq\sin\varphi-\|\varrho\|=\sin\varphi-r>0
, thus

\sin\varphi^{\prime}=\frac{\langle\vartheta+\varrho,\hat{e}_{1}\rangle}{\|\vartheta+\varrho\|}>0

Express the pertubation vector by
\varrho=-r^{\prime}\cos(\varphi-\Delta)\hat{e}_{1}+r^{\prime}\sin(\varphi-\Delta)\hat{e}_{2}+\sqrt{r^{2}-\left[{r^{\prime}}\right]^{2}}\hat{e}
, where
r^{\prime}\in[0,r],\Delta\in(-\pi,\pi]
and

\hat{e}\in\operatorname{span}\{\hat{e}_{1},\hat{e}_{2}\}^{\perp},\|\hat{e}\|=1

\displaystyle\langle\vartheta+\varrho,\hat{e}_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\vartheta,\hat{e}_{1}\rangle+\langle\varrho,\hat{e}_{1}\rangle=\sin\varphi-r^{\prime}\cos(\varphi-\Delta)

\displaystyle\|\vartheta+\varrho\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|[\sin\varphi-r^{\prime}\cos(\varphi-\Delta)]\hat{e}_{1}+[\cos\varphi+r^{\prime}\sin(\varphi-\Delta)]\hat{e}_{2}+\sqrt{r^{2}-\left[{r^{\prime}}\right]^{2}}\hat{e}\right\|=\sqrt{[1+r^{2}]-2r^{\prime}\sin\Delta}

Hence, let
p:=\frac{r^{\prime}|\sin\Delta|}{r}\in[0,1],-r^{\prime}\cos\Delta\geq-\sqrt{[r^{\prime}]^{2}-[rp]^{2}}\geq-\sqrt{r^{2}-[rp]^{2}}=-r\sqrt{1-p^{2}}

and
p=\frac{r^{\prime}}{r}|\sin\Delta|\leq 1

\displaystyle\sin\varphi^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{[1-r^{\prime}\sin\Delta]\sin\varphi-r^{\prime}\cos\Delta\cos\varphi}{\sqrt{[1+r^{2}]-2r^{\prime}\sin\Delta}}\geq\frac{[1-rp]\sin\varphi-r\sqrt{1-p^{2}}\cos\varphi}{\sqrt{[1+r^{2}]-2rp}}:=\psi(p)

For
\psi(p):=\frac{[1-rp]\sin\varphi-r\sqrt{1-p^{2}}\cos\varphi}{\sqrt{[1+r^{2}]-2rp}}
, by
\cos\left(\varphi+\left[\frac{\pi}{2}-\arcsin p\right]\right)\leq\cos\varphi
,
then
\cos\varphi-r\cos\left(\varphi+\left[\frac{\pi}{2}-\arcsin p\right]\right)>0

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}p}\log\psi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{r\cdot\left\{\cos\varphi-r\cos\left(\varphi+\left[\frac{\pi}{2}-\arcsin p\right]\right)\right\}(p-r)}{\sqrt{1-p^{2}}\left\{[1-rp]\sin\varphi-r\sqrt{1-p^{2}}\cos\varphi\right\}\cdot\{[1+r^{2}]-2rp\}}

therefore,
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}p}\log\psi<0,\forall p\in(0,r);\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}p}\log\psi>0,\forall p\in(r,1)
, hence
\psi(p)\geq\psi(p)\mid_{p=r}
for
p\in[0,1]

\displaystyle\sin\varphi^{\prime}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\psi(p)\geq\psi(p)\mid_{p=r}\sqrt{1-r^{2}}\sin\varphi-r\cos\varphi=\sin(\varphi-\arcsin r)

Note that
r\in(0,\sin\varphi)
, that is
\frac{\pi}{2}>\varphi>\varphi-\arcsin r>0
, and we show that

\varphi^{\prime}\geq\varphi-\arcsin r

∎

C-C

Convergence Guarantees for Finite-Sample EM Updates in Noiseless Setting

Theorem C.8

(Proposition
21
: Convergence of Sub-Optimality Angle)
.

In the noiseless setting, suppose
\varphi^{0}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
, given a positive number
\varepsilon\lesssim\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}
, we run Easy finite-sample EM for
T_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{d}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)
iterations followed by the standard finite-sample EM for at most
T^{\prime}=\mathcal{O}\left([\log\frac{n}{d}\wedge\log\frac{n}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}]\vee\log[\log\frac{n}{\ln\frac{1}{\delta}}/\log\frac{n}{d}]\vee[\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}/\log\frac{n}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}]\right)
iterations
with
n\gtrsim d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}
samples, then it satisfies

\phi^{T}\leq\varepsilon,

with probability at least
1-T\delta
, where
T:=T_{1}+T^{\prime},\varphi^{0}:=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos\left|\frac{\langle\theta^{0},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta^{0}\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\right|
and
\varphi^{T}:=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos\left|\frac{\langle\theta^{T},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta^{T}\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\right|
.

Proof.

We provide a new concise and rigorous proof for the Proposition for convergence of angle, which is reproduced here for completeness.
The other old lengthy proof of this Proposition for convergence of angle can be found on pages 45-50, Appendix E of
[
1
]
.
We divide the proof into four stages:

(1) In the first stage, we show that after running the Easy EM for at most
T_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{d}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)
iterations at the finite-sample level, given the initial sub-optimality angle
\varphi^{0}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
,
the sub-optimality angle satisfies
\varphi^{T_{1}}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
.

(2) In the second stage, we show that after running the standard EM for at most
T_{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{n}{d}\wedge\log\frac{n}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)
iterations at the finite-sample level, given that
\varphi^{T_{1}}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
,
the sub-optimality angle satisfies
\phi^{T_{1}+T_{2}}\lesssim 1
.

(3) In the third stage, we show that after running the standard EM for at most
T_{3}=\mathcal{O}\left(\log[\log\frac{n}{d}\wedge\log\frac{n}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}]\right)
iterations at the finite-sample level, given that
\phi^{T_{1}+T_{2}}\lesssim 1
,
the sub-optimality angle satisfies
\phi^{T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
.

(4) In the fourth stage, we show that after running the standard EM for at most
T_{4}=\mathcal{O}\left(\log[\log\frac{n}{\ln\frac{1}{\delta}}/\log\frac{n}{d}]\vee[\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}/\log\frac{n}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}]\right)
iterations at the finite-sample level, given that
\phi^{T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
,
the sub-optimality angle satisfies
\phi^{T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4}}\leq\varepsilon
.

We denote
\vec{e}_{1}=\theta^{\ast}/\|\theta^{\ast}\|
as the unit vector in the direction of
\theta^{\ast}
,
also define
\varphi^{t}=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos\left|\frac{\langle\theta^{t},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta^{t}\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\right|,\phi^{t}=2\arccos\left|\frac{\langle\theta^{t},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta^{t}\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\right|
as the sub-optimality angles.
Also, we denote
\bar{\theta}^{t}=M(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})
and
\bar{\varphi}^{t}:=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos\left|\frac{\langle\bar{\theta}^{t},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\bar{\theta}^{t}\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\right|,\bar{\phi}^{t}=2\arccos\left|\frac{\langle\bar{\theta}^{t},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\bar{\theta}^{t}\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\right|
as the sub-optimality angles of
\bar{\theta}^{t}
for the analysis of population level.
Further, we denote
c_{\text{proj}},c_{\text{stat}}
as the the universal constants for the projected error and the statistical error
such that
\|P_{\theta,\theta^{\ast}}[M_{n}^{\text{easy}}(\theta,\nu)-M(\theta,\nu)]\|/\|\theta^{\ast}\|\leq c_{\text{proj}}\times\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
and
\|M_{n}^{\text{easy}}(\theta,\nu)-M(\theta,\nu)\|/\|\theta^{\ast}\|,\|M_{n}(\theta,\nu)-M(\theta,\nu)\|/\|\theta^{\ast}\|\leq c_{\text{stat}}\times\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}

with
n\gtrsim d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}
large enough sample size.

Stage 1: run Easy EM from
\varphi^{0}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
to
\varphi^{T_{1}}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}

Let’s select sample number
n\gtrsim d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}
large enough
such that assumption
\varphi^{t}<C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}<\frac{1}{20}
holds for all
t\in[0,T_{1})
,
and the statistical error of
\|\theta^{t}-\bar{\theta}^{t}\|/\|\theta^{\ast}\|\leq c_{\text{stat}}\times\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}<\frac{2}{\pi}\times\frac{1}{20}
is also small enough.
then we can establish the lower bound for the projection of
\bar{\theta}^{t+1}=M(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})
onto
\vec{e}_{1}
, which is denoted by

\displaystyle\frac{\langle\bar{\theta}^{t+1},\hat{e}_{1}\rangle}{\langle\theta^{t},\hat{e}_{1}\rangle}=\frac{\|M(\theta^{t},\nu^{t})\|}{\|\bar{\theta}^{t}+(\theta^{t}-\bar{\theta}^{t})\|}\times\frac{\tan\bar{\varphi}^{t+1}}{\tan\varphi^{t}}\times\frac{\cos\bar{\varphi}^{t+1}}{\cos\varphi^{t}}\geq\frac{2/\pi}{(2/\pi)(\frac{1}{20}+\cos\frac{1}{20})+(2/\pi)\times\frac{1}{20}}\times\frac{1+\sqrt{5}}{2}\times\frac{1}{\tan\frac{1}{20}+1}>\frac{7}{5}

where the first inequality is by the fact the distance from the point on the cycloid trajectory to the origin is increasing as the sub-optimality angle
\varphi^{t}
is increasing,
and its minimum value is
2/\pi
, and the second inequality is due to the Proposition for the linear growth of
\tan\varphi^{t}
,
and the last inequality is from the proof of the Proposition of the recurrence relation.
Without loss of generality, we assume
\langle\theta^{0},\hat{e}_{1}\rangle>0
, then by the assumption
\varphi^{0}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
, we have
\langle\theta^{t},\hat{e}_{1}\rangle>0
for all
t
, then

\displaystyle\sin\varphi^{t+1}=\frac{\langle\bar{\theta}^{t+1},\hat{e}_{1}\rangle-\langle\bar{\theta}^{t+1}-\theta^{t+1},\hat{e}_{1}\rangle}{\|\bar{\theta}^{t+1}+(\theta^{t+1}-\bar{\theta}^{t+1})\|}\geq\frac{\frac{7}{5}\langle\theta^{t},\hat{e}_{1}\rangle/\|\theta^{\ast}\|-c_{\text{proj}}\times\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}}{\|\bar{\theta}^{t+1}\|/\|\theta^{\ast}\|+\frac{2}{\pi}\times\frac{1}{20}}\geq\frac{\frac{7}{5}\times\frac{19}{20}}{\frac{1}{10}+\cos\frac{1}{20}}\sin\varphi^{t}-\frac{c_{\text{proj}}}{\frac{1}{10}+\cos\frac{1}{20}}\times\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}

where the second inequality is due the following fact and noting that
\sin\varphi^{t}=\frac{\langle\theta^{t},\hat{e}_{1}\rangle}{\|\theta^{t}\|}
:

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\pi}\leq\|\bar{\theta}^{t+1}\|/\|\theta^{\ast}\|\leq\frac{2}{\pi}\times\left(\frac{1}{20}+\cos\frac{1}{20}\right),\quad\|\theta^{t}\|/\|\theta^{\ast}\|\geq\|\bar{\theta}^{t}\|/\|\theta^{\ast}\|-\|\theta^{t}-\bar{\theta}^{t}\|/\|\theta^{\ast}\|\geq\frac{2}{\pi}\times\left(1-\frac{1}{20}\right)

Suppose
\varphi^{0}\geq 45c_{\text{proj}}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
by
\varphi^{0}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
,
then
\sin\varphi^{0}-30c_{\text{proj}}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\geq 10c_{\text{proj}}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
, the linear growth is shown.

\displaystyle\sin\varphi^{t+1}\geq(1+\frac{1}{30})\sin\varphi^{t}-c_{\text{proj}}\times\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\implies\sin\varphi^{t+1}-30c_{\text{proj}}\times\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\geq(1+\frac{1}{30})\left(\sin\varphi^{t}-30c_{\text{proj}}\times\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right)

Since the population EM update only depends on the sub-optimality angle
\varphi^{t}
, the above inequality for
t>0
also holds for
t=0
without loss of generality.
Therefore, by the induction, we have shown that after running the Easy EM for at most
T_{1}\leq\lceil\frac{\ln(C_{1}/(10c_{\text{proj}}))+\frac{1}{2}\max(0,\ln(d/\log\frac{1}{\delta}))}{\ln(1+1/30)}\rceil_{+}\asymp\log\frac{d}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}
iterations,
we have

\varphi^{T_{1}}\geq\sin\varphi^{T_{1}}\geq C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
with some universal constant
C_{1}>0
.

Stage 2: run Standard EM from
\varphi^{T_{1}}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
to
\phi^{T_{1}+T_{2}}\lesssim 1

Given that
\varphi^{T_{1}}\geq C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
, for some positive constant
C_{1}>0
, here we let
C_{1}=20c_{\text{stat}}
,
we continue to run the standard EM with the initial sub-optimality angle
\varphi^{T_{1}}\geq 20c_{\text{stat}}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}>c_{\text{stat}}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
.

For the ease of analysis, we introduce the following notations:
r:=\frac{\pi}{2}c_{\text{stat}}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}<\frac{1}{20}
and
s:=\frac{r}{\sqrt{1-r^{2}}}<(1+\frac{1}{100})r
, namely
r=\frac{s}{\sqrt{1+s^{2}}}
.
Then by the previous establised Lemma for single iteration of pertubation and using the fact that
\|\theta^{t+1}-\bar{\theta}^{t+1}\|\leq c_{\text{stat}}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
and
\|\bar{\theta}^{t+1}\|\geq\frac{2}{\pi}
, we have shown that

\varphi^{t+1}\geq\bar{\varphi}^{t+1}-\arcsin\left(\frac{\|\theta^{t+1}-\bar{\theta}^{t+1}\|}{\|\bar{\theta}^{t+1}\|}\right)\geq\bar{\varphi}^{t+1}-\arcsin\left(\frac{c_{\text{stat}}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}}{2/\pi}\right)=\bar{\varphi}^{t+1}-\arcsin r

Applying the linear growth of
\tan\varphi
in population level in the proven Proposition, we have
\tan\bar{\varphi}^{t+1}\geq 2\tan\varphi^{t}
. and noting that
\tan(x-y)=\frac{\tan x-\tan y}{1+\tan x\tan y},\tan(\arcsin r)=s
,

\displaystyle\tan\varphi^{t+1}\geq\tan(\bar{\varphi}^{t+1}-\arcsin r)\geq\frac{2\tan\varphi^{t}-s}{1+2\tan\varphi^{t}\cdot s}=(1+\frac{1}{10})\tan\varphi^{t}-\frac{s}{1+2s\cdot\tan\varphi^{t}}+\frac{\tan\varphi^{t}(2-1.1-1.1s\tan\varphi^{t})}{1+2s\cdot\tan\varphi^{t}}

In particular, when
\varphi^{t}\leq 1
, we have
2-1.1-2.2s\tan\varphi^{t}>0
for
s<(1+\frac{1}{100})r<(1+\frac{1}{100})\frac{1}{20}
, therefore,

\displaystyle\tan\varphi^{t+1}\geq(1+\frac{1}{10})\tan\varphi^{t}-s\geq(1+\frac{1}{10})\tan\varphi^{t}-(1+\frac{1}{100})r\geq(1+\frac{1}{10})\tan\varphi^{t}-\frac{16}{10}c_{\text{stat}}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}

By the initial assumption
\varphi^{T_{1}}\geq 20c_{\text{stat}}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}>c_{\text{stat}}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
,
as long as
\varphi^{t}\leq 1
, we have

\tan\varphi^{t+1}-16c_{\text{stat}}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\geq(1+\frac{1}{10})\left(\tan\varphi^{t}-16c_{\text{stat}}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right)>0

Therefore, by the induction, we have shown that after running the standard EM for at most
T_{2}\leq\left\lceil\frac{\ln(\frac{\tan 1}{4c_{\text{stat}}})+\frac{1}{2}\min(\ln\frac{n}{d},\ln\frac{n}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}})}{\ln(1+1/10)}\right\rceil_{+}\asymp\log\frac{n}{d}\wedge\log\frac{n}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}
iterations,
we have

\varphi^{T_{1}+T_{2}}\geq 1
, and therefore
\phi^{T_{1}+T_{2}}=\pi-2\varphi^{T_{1}+T_{2}}<1.2
.

Stage 3: run Standard EM from
\phi^{T_{1}+T_{2}}\lesssim 1
to
\phi^{T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}

Given that
\phi^{T_{1}+T_{2}}<1.2<1.4
, we continue to run the standard EM with quadratic convergence rate.
Recall the established inequality
\varphi^{t+1}\geq\bar{\varphi}^{t+1}-\arcsin r
, then by the relation
\phi^{t+1}=\pi-2\varphi^{t+1},\bar{\phi}^{t+1}=\pi-2\bar{\varphi}^{t+1}

and noting that
\arcsin r\leq\frac{\pi}{2}r
.

\phi^{t+1}\leq\bar{\phi}^{t+1}+2\arcsin r\leq\bar{\phi}^{t+1}+\pi r

Applying Proposition for the quadratic convergence rate of
\phi^{t}
in population level, namely

\bar{\phi}^{t+1}/\pi\leq[\phi^{t}/\pi]^{2}
when
\phi^{t}\leq 1.4
.

\frac{\phi^{t+1}}{\pi}\leq\left[\frac{\phi^{t}}{\pi}\right]^{2}+r

In one iteration,
\phi^{T_{1}+T_{2}+1}/\pi\leq(1.2/\pi)^{2}+r<(1.2/\pi)^{2}+\frac{1}{20}<\frac{1}{5}
,
then we have
\phi^{t}<\frac{\pi}{5}<\frac{\pi}{4}
in the following iterations.

\left[\frac{\phi^{t}}{\pi}-2r\right]\leq\left[\frac{\phi^{t}}{\pi}\right]^{2}-r=\left[\frac{\phi^{t}}{\pi}-2r\right]^{2}-4r^{2}-\frac{4r}{\pi}\left(\frac{\pi}{4}-\phi^{t}\right)<\left[\frac{\phi^{t}}{\pi}-2r\right]^{2}

Therefore, by the above inequality, we have shown that after running the standard EM for at most
T_{3}\leq 1+\left\lceil\frac{\ln\left(\frac{\ln(1/r)}{\ln 5}\right)}{\ln 2}\right\rceil_{+}=1+\left\lceil\frac{\ln[\frac{1}{2}\min(\ln(n/d),\ln(n/\log\frac{1}{\delta}))]-\ln(\frac{\pi}{2}c_{\text{stat}})-\ln\ln 5}{\ln 2}\right\rceil_{+}\asymp\log[\log\frac{n}{d}\wedge\log\frac{n}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}]
iterations,
we have
\phi^{T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}}\leq 3r=\frac{3\pi}{2}c_{\text{stat}}\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
.

Stage 4: run Standard EM from
\phi^{T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
to
\phi^{T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4}}\lesssim\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}

By the previous establised Proposition of statistical accuracy, we have the following inequality:

\phi^{t+1}\leq\pi\sin\frac{\phi^{t+1}}{2}\leq\pi\frac{\|\theta^{t+1}-\textnormal{sgn}(\rho^{t+1})\theta^{\ast}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\lesssim[\phi^{t}]^{2}\vee[\phi^{t}]^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\vee\phi^{t}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}

With the assumption that
n\gtrsim d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}
and the fact that
\phi^{T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\lesssim 1
, then for
t\geq T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+1
, we have

\phi^{t}\leq\phi^{T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+1}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\phi^{T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}}\lesssim\frac{d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n},\quad\phi^{t}\lesssim[\phi^{t}]^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\vee\phi^{t}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}

Under the assumption that
n\gtrsim d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}
, if (i)
n\lesssim d^{2}/\log\frac{1}{\delta}
(therefore,
d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}\asymp d
must hold), then only when
\phi^{t}\gtrsim\log\frac{1}{\delta}/d
:

\phi^{t+1}\lesssim[\phi^{t}]^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\vee\phi^{t}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\lesssim[\phi^{t}]^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\implies\phi^{[T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+1]+\Theta\left(\left\lceil\ln\left(\ln\frac{n}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}/\left(2\ln\frac{n}{d}\right)\right)/\ln\frac{3}{2}\right\rceil\right)}\lesssim\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{d}

then, for
t\geq T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+1+\Theta\left(\left\lceil\ln\left(\ln\frac{n}{\ln\frac{1}{\delta}}/\left(2\ln\frac{n}{d}\right)\right)/\ln\frac{3}{2}\right\rceil\right)
, we have

\phi^{t+1}\lesssim\phi^{t}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\implies\phi^{[T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+1]+\Theta\left(\left\lceil\ln\left(\ln\frac{n}{\ln\frac{1}{\delta}}/\left(2\ln\frac{n}{d}\right)\right)/\ln\frac{3}{2}\right\rceil\right)+\Theta\left(\left\lceil 2[\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}-\ln\frac{d}{\ln\frac{1}{\delta}}]/\ln\frac{n}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right\rceil\right)}\leq\varepsilon

Otherwise, if (ii)
n\gtrsim d^{2}/\log\frac{1}{\delta}
, then for
t\geq T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+1
, we have

\phi^{t+1}\lesssim\phi^{t}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\implies\phi^{[T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+1]+\Theta\left(\left\lceil 2[\ln\frac{1}{\varepsilon}-\ln\frac{n}{\ln\frac{1}{\delta}}\wedge\ln\frac{n}{d}]/\ln\frac{n}{\ln\frac{1}{\delta}}\right\rceil\right)}\leq\varepsilon

Combining the above two case (i) and (ii), we have shown that after running the standard EM for at most
T_{4}\lesssim\log[\log\frac{n}{\ln\frac{1}{\delta}}/\log\frac{n}{d}]\vee[\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}/\log\frac{n}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}]
iterations,
we have
\phi^{T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4}}\leq\varepsilon
.

Summary: number of iterations
Therefore, after running Easy EM for at most
T_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{d}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)
iterations,
and then running Standard EM for at most
T^{\prime}:=T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4}=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{n}{d}\wedge\log\frac{n}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}+\log[\log\frac{n}{d}\wedge\log\frac{n}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}]+\log[\log\frac{n}{\ln\frac{1}{\delta}}/\log\frac{n}{d}]\vee[\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}/\log\frac{n}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}]\right)=\mathcal{O}\left([\log\frac{n}{d}\wedge\log\frac{n}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}]\vee\log[\log\frac{n}{\ln\frac{1}{\delta}}/\log\frac{n}{d}]\vee[\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}/\log\frac{n}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}]\right)
iterations,
we have
\phi^{T}=\phi^{T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4}}\leq\varepsilon
with probability at least
1-T\delta
, where
T:=T_{1}+T^{\prime}=T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4}
.
∎

Theorem C.9

(Theorem
22
: Finite-Sample Level Convergence)
.

In the noiseless setting, suppose any initial mixing weights
\pi^{0}
and any initial regression parameters
\theta^{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
ensuring that
\varphi^{0}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}
. Given a positive number
\varepsilon\lesssim\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}
, we
run finite-sample Easy EM for at most
T_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{d}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)

iterations followed by the finite-sample standard EM for at most
T^{\prime}=\mathcal{O}\left([\log\frac{n}{d}\wedge\log\frac{n}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}]\vee\log[\log\frac{n}{\ln\frac{1}{\delta}}/\log\frac{n}{d}]\vee[\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}/\log\frac{n}{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}]\right)

iterations with
n\gtrsim d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}
samples, then

\displaystyle\frac{\|\theta^{T+1}-\mathrm{sgn}(\rho^{T+1})\theta^{\ast}\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}=\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right),\quad\left\|\pi^{T+1}-\bar{\pi}^{\ast}\right\|_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon\left\|\frac{1}{2}-\pi^{\ast}\right\|_{1}\vee\left[\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}/n}{\log\left(1+\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}/n}{p(\varepsilon,\pi^{\ast})}\right)}\wedge\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right]\right),

with probability at least
1-T\delta
, where
T:=T_{1}+T^{\prime},\varphi^{0}:=\frac{\pi}{2}-\arccos\left|\frac{\langle\theta^{0},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta^{0}\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\right|,\rho^{T+1}:=\frac{\langle\theta^{T+1},\theta^{\ast}\rangle}{\|\theta^{T+1}\|\cdot\|\theta^{\ast}\|},\bar{\pi}^{\ast}:=\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}+\operatorname{sgn}(\rho^{T+1})(\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2})
,
and
p(\varepsilon,\pi^{\ast}):=\varepsilon\left\|\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}+\min(\pi^{\ast}(1),\pi^{\ast}(2))
.

Proof.

By Proposition for convergence of angle, we have shown that
\phi^{T}\leq\varepsilon\lesssim\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}
after
T
iterations with
n\gtrsim d\vee\log\frac{1}{\delta}
samples.
Then, by using Proposition for statistical accuracy of EM updates, and
noting that
p^{T}:=\frac{\phi^{T}}{2\pi}\|\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\|_{1}+\min(\pi^{\ast}(1),\pi^{\ast}(2))\leq\varepsilon\|\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\|_{1}+\min(\pi^{\ast}(1),\pi^{\ast}(2))\equiv p(\varepsilon,\pi^{\ast})
:

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|\theta^{T+1}-\mathrm{sgn}(\rho^{T+1})\theta^{\ast}\right\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|M_{n}(\theta^{T},\nu^{T})-\textnormal{sgn}(\rho^{T})\theta^{\ast}\right\|}{\|\theta^{\ast}\|}\lesssim[\phi^{T}]^{2}\vee[\phi^{T}]^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\vee\phi^{T}\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\lesssim\varepsilon\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}

\displaystyle\left\|\pi^{T+1}-\bar{\pi}^{\ast}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|N_{n}(\theta^{T},\nu^{t})-\textnormal{sgn}(\rho^{T})\tanh\nu^{\ast}\right|\lesssim\phi^{T}\left\|\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}\vee\left[\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}/n}{\log\left(1+\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}/n}{p^{T}}\right)}\wedge\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\varepsilon\left\|\pi^{\ast}-\frac{\mathds{1}}{2}\right\|_{1}\vee\left[\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}/n}{\log\left(1+\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}/n}{p(\varepsilon,\pi^{\ast})}\right)}\wedge\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right]

∎

Appendix D
Derivations for EM Update Rules

The detailed derivations for the EM update rules are adapted from pages 20-25, Appendix B of
[
1
]
,
and included here in this Appendix Chapter for completeness.

D-A

Negative Log-Likelihood, Surrogate Function, and Q Function

Lemma D.1
.

The negative expected log-likelihood
f(\theta,\pi):=-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}[\log p(s\mid\theta,\pi)]
for the mixture model of
s:=(x,y),z\in[M]

with the mixing weights
\pi^{\ast}\in\mathbb{R}^{M}
and regression parameters
\theta
is as follows.

\displaystyle-f(\theta,\pi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\operatorname{KL}_{s}[p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})||p(s\mid\theta,\pi)]-\mathcal{H}_{s}[p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}[\ln p(s\mid\theta,\pi)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\mathbb{E}_{z\sim q_{s}(z)}\ln p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)+\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\mathcal{H}_{z}[q_{s}(z)]+\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\operatorname{KL}_{z}[q_{s}(z)||p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)]

where
\operatorname{KL}_{s},\mathcal{H}_{s}
are KL divengence and Shannon’s
entropy wrt.
s=(x,y)
;

\operatorname{KL}_{z},\mathcal{H}_{z},\operatorname{softmax}_{z}
are KL divengence, Shannon’s
entropy and softmax wrt.
z\in\mathcal{Z}=[M]
;

\{q_{s}(z)\mid s\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}=\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}\}
is a family of distributions wrt.
z\in\mathcal{Z}=[M]
,
namely
\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}q_{s}(z)=1
.

Proof.

Note that
p(s\mid\theta,\pi)=\frac{p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)}{p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)}
, we obtain the following expression.

\displaystyle-f(\theta,\pi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\operatorname{KL}_{s}[p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})||p(s\mid\theta,\pi)]-\mathcal{H}_{s}[p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}[\log p(s\mid\theta,\pi)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\left[\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}q_{s}(z)\log p(s\mid\theta,\pi)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\left[\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}q_{s}(z)\ln\left(\frac{p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)}{q_{s}(z)}\cdot\frac{q_{s}(z)}{p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\mathbb{E}_{z\sim q_{s}(z)}\ln p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)+\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\mathcal{H}_{z}[q_{s}(z)]+\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\operatorname{KL}_{z}[q_{s}(z)||p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)]

∎

Lemma D.2
.

The surrogate function
g^{t}
of
f(\theta,\pi):=-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}[\ln p(s\mid\theta,\pi)]
at
(t-1)
-th iteration
(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})
be expressed as follows.

\displaystyle-g^{t}(\theta,\pi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Bigg\{\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\mathbb{E}_{z\sim q_{s}(z)}\ln p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)+\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\mathcal{H}_{z}[q_{s}(z)]\Bigg\}_{q_{s}(z)=p(z\mid s;\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}

that is
g^{t}(\theta,\pi)\geq f(\theta,\pi)
, and

g^{t}(\theta,\pi)\mid_{(\theta,\pi)=(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}=f(\theta,\pi)\mid_{(\theta,\pi)=(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}
,

\nabla_{\theta}g^{t}(\theta,\pi)\mid_{(\theta,\pi)=(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}=\nabla_{\theta}f(\theta,\pi)\mid_{(\theta,\pi)=(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}
;

\nabla_{\pi}g^{t}(\theta,\pi)\mid_{(\theta,\pi)=(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}=\nabla_{\pi}f(\theta,\pi)\mid_{(\theta,\pi)=(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}
;

where
\operatorname{KL}_{s},\mathcal{H}_{s}
are KL divengence and Shannon’s
entropy wrt.
s=(x,y)
;

\operatorname{KL}_{z},\mathcal{H}_{z},\operatorname{softmax}_{z}
are KL divengence, Shannon’s
entropy and softmax wrt.
z\in\mathcal{Z}=[M]

Proof.

Let
r^{t}:=g^{t}-f
, note that
r^{t}=\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\operatorname{KL}_{z}[q_{s}(z)||p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)]_{q_{s}(z)=p(z\mid s;\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}\geq 0
, and

\displaystyle r^{t}(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\operatorname{KL}_{z}[q_{s}(z)||p(z\mid s;\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})]_{q_{s}(z)=p(z\mid s;\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}=0

\displaystyle\left[\frac{\mathrm{d}q_{s}(z)\ln\frac{q_{s}(z)}{p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)}}{\mathrm{d}p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)}\right]_{(\theta,\pi)=(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{q_{s}(z)}{p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)}_{q_{s}(z)=p(z\mid s;\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1}),(\theta,\pi)=(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}=-1

Hence, the gradients of
r^{t}
wrt.
(\theta,\pi)
at
(t-1)
-th iteration
(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})
are all zeros by the chain rule.

\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta}\operatorname{KL}_{z}[q_{s}(z)||p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)]_{q_{s}(z)=p(z\mid s;\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1}),(\theta,\pi)=(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}=-\left[\nabla_{\theta}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)\right]_{(\theta,\pi)=(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}=\vec{0}

\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta}r^{t}(\theta,\pi)_{(\theta,\pi)=(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}=\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\nabla_{\theta}\operatorname{KL}_{z}[q_{s}(z)||p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)]_{q_{s}(z)=p(z\mid s;\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1}),(\theta,\pi)=(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}=\vec{0}

The same proof can be applied to
\nabla_{\pi}r^{t}(\theta,\pi)_{(\theta,\pi)=(\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}=\vec{0}
.
∎

Lemma D.3
.

Assuming
(z;\pi)\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\theta
, and
\pi\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}s\mid z
, and
x\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(z;\theta,\pi)
; then

\displaystyle Q(\theta,\pi\mid\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\left[\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\mathbb{E}_{z\sim q_{s}(z)}\ln p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)\right]_{q_{s}(z)\leftarrow p(z\mid s;\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{z\sim q_{s}(z)}\ln p(y\mid x,z;\theta)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}\ln p(x)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}_{z}\left[\pi^{t}(z)||\pi(z)\right]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{z}\left[\pi^{t}(z)\right]

\displaystyle p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{softmax}_{z}(\ln\pi(z)+\ln p(y\mid x,z;\theta))

where
\operatorname{KL}_{s},\mathcal{H}_{s}
are KL divengence and Shannon’s
entropy wrt.
s=(x,y)
;

\operatorname{KL}_{z},\mathcal{H}_{z},\operatorname{softmax}_{z}
are KL divengence, Shannon’s
entropy and softmax wrt.
z\in\mathcal{Z}=[M]
;

q_{s}(z)\leftarrow p\left(z\mid s;\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1}\right)
and
\pi^{t}=\{\pi(z)\}_{z\in\mathcal{Z}},\pi^{t}(z):=\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}q_{s}(z)
.

Proof.

\bullet

(z;\pi)\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\theta
are independent:

therefore
p(z\mid\theta,\pi)=p(z\mid\pi)=\pi(z)

\bullet

\pi\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(s;\theta)\mid z
are conditional independent given
z
:

then
p(\pi\mid z)=p(\pi\mid z;\theta)=p(\pi\mid z,s;\theta)
,
it implies
\frac{p(s,z;\theta,\pi)}{p(z;\theta,\pi)}=\frac{p(s,z;\theta)}{p(z;\theta)}
;

hence
p(s\mid z;\theta,\pi)=p(s\mid z;\theta)
,
p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)=p(s\mid z;\theta,\pi)p(z\mid\theta,\pi)=p(s\mid z;\theta)\pi(z)

\bullet

x\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(z;\theta,\pi)
are independent:

then
p(x\mid z;\theta)=p(x)

hence
p(s\mid z;\theta)=p(y\mid x,z;\theta)\cdot p(x\mid z;\theta)=p(y\mid x,z;\theta)\cdot p(x)

therefore
p(s\mid\theta,\pi)=\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)=p(x)\cdot\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\pi(z)p(y\mid x,z;\theta)

and
p(z\mid s;\theta,\pi)=\frac{p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)}{p(s\mid\theta,\pi)}=\frac{\pi(z)\cdot p(y\mid x,z;\theta)}{\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}}\pi(z^{\prime})\cdot p(y\mid x,z^{\prime};\theta)}=\operatorname{softmax}_{z}(\log\pi(z)+\log p(y\mid x,z;\theta))

With the above assumptions, we obtain that
p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)=p(y\mid x,z;\theta)\cdot p(x)\cdot\pi(z)
, further prove this Lemma.
∎

D-B

Negative Log-Likelihood, Surrogate Function for General MLR and 2MLR

Lemma D.4
.

For MLR
y=\langle x,\theta_{z}^{\ast}\rangle+\varepsilon,z\in\mathcal{Z}=[M]
,
\theta:=\{\theta_{z}\}_{z\in\mathcal{Z}},\pi:=\{\pi(z)\}_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}
,
with assumptions:
(z;\pi)\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\theta
, and
\varepsilon\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(x,z;\theta,\pi)
, and
x\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(z;\theta,\pi)
and
\varepsilon\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})

\displaystyle f(\theta,\pi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{s}\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}\log\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\exp\left[-\frac{\left\|y-\left\langle\theta_{z},x\right\rangle\right\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}+\log\pi(z)\right]-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}\log p(x)-c

\displaystyle g^{t}(\theta,\pi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(2\sigma^{2}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{z\sim q_{x}(z)}\left\|y-\left\langle\theta_{z},x\right\rangle\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}_{z}\left[\pi^{t}(z)\|\pi(z)\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{z}\left[\pi^{t}(z)\right]-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}\log p(x)-c

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}\mathcal{H}_{z}\left[q_{s}(z)\right]

where
c=-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(2\pi\sigma^{2}\right)
and
q_{s}(z)\leftarrow p\left(z\mid s;\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1}\right)=\operatorname{softmax}_{z}\left(-\frac{\left\|y-\left\langle\theta_{z}^{t-1},x\right\rangle\right\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}+\log\pi^{t-1}(z)\right)
,
and
\pi^{t}=\{\pi(z)\}_{z\in\mathcal{Z}},\pi^{t}(z):=\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}q_{s}(z)
.

Proof.

Since
(z;\pi)\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\theta
, and
\varepsilon\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(x,z;\theta,\pi)
, and
x\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(z;\theta,\pi)
, then implies
\pi\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}s\mid z

because of
p(\pi\mid z,s)=p(\pi\mid z,x,y)=p(\pi\mid z,x,\varepsilon)=p(\pi\mid z)=\pi(z)
. Hence, we can apply the previous Lemma.

Furthermore,
p(y,x,z;\theta)=p(\varepsilon,x,z;\theta)\left|\frac{\partial\varepsilon}{\partial y}\right|=p(\varepsilon)\cdot p(x,z;\theta)

p(y\mid x,z;\theta)=p(\bm{\varepsilon})=(2\pi\sigma^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\varepsilon\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)=(2\pi\sigma^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|y-\langle x,\theta_{z}\rangle\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)=\mathcal{N}(\langle x,\theta_{z}\rangle,\sigma^{2})

Hence, we obtain
\log p(y\mid x,z;\theta)=-\frac{\|y-\langle x,\theta_{z}\rangle\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}+c
, where
c=-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(2\pi\sigma^{2}\right)
.

Subsequently, note that
g^{t}(\theta,\pi)=Q(\theta,\pi\mid\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1})-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}\mathcal{H}_{z}\left[q_{s}(z)\right]
,
we prove the expression for
g^{t}
by substituting
\log p(y\mid x,z;\theta)
in the previous Lemma.

As shown in the proof of the previous Lemma,
p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)=p(y\mid x,z;\theta)\cdot p(x)\cdot\pi(z)
.

Hence
p(s\mid\theta,\pi)=\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}p(s,z\mid\theta,\pi)=p(x)\cdot\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}p(y\mid x,z;\theta)\cdot\pi(z)
,
we prove the expression for
f
by substituting
\log p(y\mid x,z;\theta)
in the previous Lemma.
∎

Theorem D.5
.

For 2MLR,
y=(-1)^{z+1}\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle+\varepsilon,z\in\mathcal{Z}=\{1,2\}
,
\theta_{1}=\theta,\theta_{2}=-\theta,\pi:=\{\pi(z)\}_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}
,
with assumptions:
(z;\pi)\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\theta
, and
\varepsilon\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(x,z;\theta,\pi)
, and
x\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(z;\theta,\pi)
and
\varepsilon\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
.

Then the negative expected log-likelihood
f(\theta,\pi):=-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}[\ln p(s\mid\theta,\pi)]
and the surrogate function
g^{t}(\theta,\pi)
can be expressed as follows.

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\langle\theta,\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}xx^{\top}\cdot\theta\rangle+\ln\cosh\nu-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\ln\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}y^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\ln p(x)-c

\displaystyle g^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\langle\theta,\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}xx^{\top}\cdot\theta\rangle+\log\cosh\nu-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)\leftarrow(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla_{\theta}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)\leftarrow(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})},\theta-\theta^{t-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla_{\nu}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)\leftarrow(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})},\nu-\nu^{t-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}y^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log p(x)-c

where
\nu:=\frac{\log\pi(1)-\log\pi(2)}{2}
,
c=-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(2\pi\sigma^{2}\right)
.

Proof.

Note that
\operatorname{sigmoid}(2t)+\operatorname{sigmoid}(-2t)=1
and

\operatorname{sigmoid}(2t)-\operatorname{sigmoid}(-2t)=\tanh(t)
for
\forall t
,

q_{s}(z)\leftarrow p\left(z\mid s;\theta^{t-1},\pi^{t-1}\right)=\operatorname{softmax}_{z}(-\frac{\left\|y-\left\langle\theta_{z}^{t-1},x\right\rangle\right\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}+\log\pi^{t-1}(z))=\operatorname{sigmoid}(2(-1)^{z+1}\left[\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right])
,

\nu^{t-1}:=\frac{\log\pi^{t-1}(1)-\log\pi^{t-1}(2)}{2}
.

\displaystyle\left(2\sigma^{2}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{z\sim q_{x}(z)}\left\|y-\left\langle\theta_{z},x\right\rangle\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\mathbb{E}_{z\sim q_{s}(z)}\frac{\|(-1)^{z+1}y-\langle x,\theta\rangle\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\frac{y^{2}+\langle x,\theta\rangle^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\langle\theta,\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}xx^{\top}\cdot\theta\rangle-\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)\frac{yx}{\sigma^{2}},\theta\right\rangle+(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}y^{2}

Consider the other terms for
g^{t}
, note
\pi^{t}(z):=\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}q_{s}(z)
, and

\pi(z)=\operatorname{sigmoid}(2(-1)^{z+1}\nu)
.

Note that
\log 2+\log\cosh\nu=-[\frac{\log\pi(1)+\log\pi(2)}{2}]
and
\operatorname{sigmoid}(2t)-\operatorname{sigmoid}(-2t)=\tanh(t)
for
\forall t
.

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}_{z}\left[\pi^{t}(z)\|\pi(z)\right]+\mathcal{H}_{z}\left[\pi^{t}(z)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p\left(s\mid\theta^{*},\pi^{*}\right)}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}q_{s}(z)\log\pi(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log 2+\log\cosh\nu-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)\cdot\nu

To sum up, we obtain the following.

\displaystyle g^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\langle\theta,\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}xx^{\top}\cdot\theta\rangle-\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)\frac{yx}{\sigma^{2}},\theta\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\log 2+\log\cosh\nu-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)\cdot\nu

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\mathcal{H}_{z}[q_{s}(z)]

\displaystyle+(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}y^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log p(x)-c

For the negative expectation of log-likelihood
f
, we show the following.

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\sum_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\exp\left[-\frac{\|y-\langle\theta_{z},x\rangle\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}+\log\pi(z)\right]-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log p(x)-c

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}[y^{2}+\langle x,\theta\rangle^{2}]-\frac{\log\pi(1)+\log\pi(2)}{2}

\displaystyle-\log 2-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log p(x)-c

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\langle\theta,\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}xx^{\top}\cdot\theta\rangle+\log\cosh\nu-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)

\displaystyle+(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}y^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log p(x)-c

Note
g^{t}=f
at
(\theta,\nu)=(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})
, by comparing the expressions
f,g^{t}
,
and use
\frac{\mathrm{d}\log\cosh(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}=\tanh(t)
.

\displaystyle g^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\langle\theta,\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}xx^{\top}\cdot\theta\rangle+\log\cosh\nu-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)\leftarrow(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla_{\theta}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)\leftarrow(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})},\theta-\theta^{t-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla_{\nu}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)\leftarrow(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})},\nu-\nu^{t-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}y^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\log p(x)-c

∎

Theorem D.6
.

For 2MLR,
y=(-1)^{z+1}\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle+\varepsilon,z\in\mathcal{Z}=\{1,2\}
,
\theta_{1}=\theta,\theta_{2}=-\theta,\pi:=\{\pi(z)\}_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}
,
with assumptions:
(z;\pi)\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\theta
, and
\varepsilon\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(x,z;\theta,\pi)
, and
x\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(z;\theta,\pi)
and
\varepsilon\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
.

Then the negative Maximum Likelihood Estimat (MLE)
f_{n}(\theta,\pi):=-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[\log p(s_{i}\mid\theta,\pi)]
and the surrogate function
g_{n}^{t}(\theta,\pi)
for the dataset
\mathcal{S}:=\{s_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}=\{(x_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
of
n
i.i.d. samples can be expressed as follows.

\displaystyle f_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\langle\theta,\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}x_{i}^{\top}\cdot\theta\rangle+\log\cosh\nu-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y_{i}\langle x_{i},\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}y_{i}^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log p(x_{i})-c

\displaystyle g_{n}^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\langle\theta,\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}x_{i}^{\top}\cdot\theta\rangle+\log\cosh\nu-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y_{i}\langle x_{i},\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)\leftarrow(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla_{\theta}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y_{i}\langle x_{i},\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)\leftarrow(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})},\theta-\theta^{t-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left\langle\nabla_{\nu}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log\cosh\left(\frac{y_{i}\langle x_{i},\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu\right)\mid_{(\theta,\nu)\leftarrow(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})},\nu-\nu^{t-1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(2\sigma^{2})^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}y_{i}^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log p(x_{i})-c

where
\nu:=\frac{\log\pi(1)-\log\pi(2)}{2}
,
c=-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(2\pi\sigma^{2}\right)
.

Proof.

This is proved by susbstituting
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n},s_{i}:=(x_{i},y_{i})
for
\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})},s:=(x,y)
in the previous Theorem.
∎

D-C

Derivations of EM Update Rules for 2MLR

Theorem D.7
.

(Derivation of Population EM Update Rules: Eq. (
6
), (
7
))
For 2MLR,
y=(-1)^{z+1}\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle+\varepsilon,z\in\mathcal{Z}=\{1,2\}
,
\theta_{1}=\theta,\theta_{2}=-\theta,\pi:=\{\pi(z)\}_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}
,
with assumptions:
(z;\pi)\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\theta
, and
\varepsilon\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(x,z;\theta,\pi)
, and
x\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(z;\theta,\pi)
and
\varepsilon\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}),x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
.

The EM update rules
M(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1}),N(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})
for
\theta,\tanh(\nu)
at the population level, namely the minimizer of the surrogate
g^{t}
/ the maximizer of
Q
, are the following.

\displaystyle M(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)yx

\displaystyle N(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)

Proof.

Take the gradients of
g^{t}
wrt.
\theta,\nu
, we obtain the following.

\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta}g^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\sigma^{2})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}xx^{\top}\cdot\theta-(\sigma^{2})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)yx

\displaystyle\nabla_{\nu}g^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tanh\nu-\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)

Furthermore, the Hessian of
g^{t}
wrt.
\theta,\nu
are positive-definite, we show that the solution to
\nabla_{\theta}g^{t}=0,\nabla_{\nu}g^{t}=0
must be the minimizer of
g^{t}
.
Since the Hessian of
g^{t}
at the solution is positive-definite,
\nabla_{\theta}(\nabla_{\nu}g^{t})=\vec{0}
and

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}_{\theta}g^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\sigma^{2})^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}xx^{\top}

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}_{\nu}g^{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cosh^{-2}\nu

Note that
\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})}xx^{\top}=I_{d}
for
x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
, we derive the expressions for EM update rules.
∎

Theorem D.8
.

(Derivation of Finite-Sample EM Update Rules: Eq. (
8
))
For 2MLR,
y=(-1)^{z+1}\langle x,\theta^{\ast}\rangle+\varepsilon,z\in\mathcal{Z}=\{1,2\}
,
\theta_{1}=\theta,\theta_{2}=-\theta,\pi:=\{\pi(z)\}_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}
,
with assumptions:
(z;\pi)\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}\theta
, and
\varepsilon\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(x,z;\theta,\pi)
, and
x\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}(z;\theta,\pi)
and
\varepsilon\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}),x\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
.

The EM update rules
M_{n}(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1}),N_{n}(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})
for
\theta,\tanh(\nu)
at the finite-sample level, namely the minimizer of the surrogate
g_{n}^{t}
, are the following.

\displaystyle M_{n}(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}x_{i}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)yx

\displaystyle N_{n}(\theta^{t-1},\nu^{t-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tanh\left(\frac{y\langle x,\theta^{t-1}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}+\nu^{t-1}\right)

Proof.

This is proved by susbstituting
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n},s_{i}:=(x_{i},y_{i})
for
\mathbb{E}_{s\sim p(s\mid\theta^{\ast},\pi^{\ast})},s:=(x,y)
in the previous Theorem, but note that
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}x_{i}^{\top}\not\equiv I_{d}
.
∎

Appendix E
Helper Lemmas Used in the Proofs of Results

E-A

Asymptotic Analysis of Integrals

Lemma E.1
.

Suppose
0\leq a<\frac{\pi}{2}
and
0<b\lesssim\cos^{2}a
, then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{b^{2}|\sin x+\sin a|\mathrm{d}x}{(b+|\sin x+\sin a|)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{b^{2}}{\cos a}\log\left(\frac{\cos^{2}a}{b}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{b^{2}\operatorname{sgn}(\sin(x+a))|\cos x+\cos a|\mathrm{d}x}{(b+|\sin x+\sin a|)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\tan a\cdot\frac{b^{2}}{\cos a}\log\left(\frac{\cos^{2}a}{b}\right)\right)

Proof.

Consider the two roots of
\sin x+\sin a=0
:

r_{1}=\pi+a,r_{2}=2\pi-a
,
split integrals into two regions, select
\delta=\cos a
:

Near roots

\displaystyle E=E_{1}\cup E_{2},E_{i}:=\{(r_{i}+u)\operatorname{mod}2\pi\mid|u|<\delta\},\forall i\in\{1,2\}

Away from roots

\displaystyle\bar{E}=\bar{E}_{1}\cup\bar{E}_{2},\bar{E}_{1}=\{x\mid r_{1}+\delta\leq x\leq r_{2}-\delta\},\bar{E}_{2}=\left\{x\mid 0\leq x\leq r_{1}-\delta\text{ or }r_{2}+\delta\leq x\leq 2\pi\right\}

(i) Consider the region
E
near the roots:

Note
|\cos r_{i}|=\cos a
and by Taylor’s theorem, apply
|u|<\delta
and
\delta=\cos a
:

\frac{1}{2}\cos a\cdot|u|\leq\cos a\cdot|u|-\frac{u^{2}}{2}\leq|\sin(r_{i}+u)+\sin a|\leq\cos a\cdot|u|+\frac{u^{2}}{2}\leq\frac{3}{2}\cos a\cdot|u|,\forall i\in\{1,2\}

For the sign
\operatorname{sgn}(\sin(x+a))
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{sgn}(\sin(r_{1}+u+a))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{sgn}(\sin(\pi+a+u+a))=-\operatorname{sgn}(\sin(2a+u))

For the sign
-\operatorname{sgn}(\sin x+\sin a)
, note
-\frac{\pi}{2}<-1\leq a-\cos a=a-\delta\leq a+u\leq a+\delta=a+\cos a\leq\frac{\pi}{2}
for
|u|<\delta
,

-\operatorname{sgn}(\sin(r_{1}+u)+\sin a)=-\operatorname{sgn}(\sin(\pi+a+u)+\sin a)=\operatorname{sgn}(\sin(a+u)-\sin a)=\operatorname{sgn}(u)

-\operatorname{sgn}(\sin(r_{2}+u)+\sin a)=-\operatorname{sgn}(\sin(2\pi-a+u)+\sin a)=\operatorname{sgn}(\sin(a-u)-\sin a)=-\operatorname{sgn}(u)

(ii) Consider the region
\bar{E}
away from the roots:

By convexity of
\sin(t)
on
[\pi,2\pi]
, note
r_{1}=\pi+a,\delta=\cos a
,
\left|\cos\left(r_{1}+\frac{\delta}{2}\right)\right|=\cos\left(a+\frac{\cos a}{2}\right)\geq\frac{1}{2}\cos a
, for
\forall x\in\bar{E}
,

|\sin x+\sin a|=|\sin x-\sin r_{1}|\geq\sin r_{1}-\sin(r_{1}+\delta)\geq\delta\times\left|\cos\left(r_{1}+\frac{\delta}{2}\right)\right|\geq\cos a\times\cos\left(a+\frac{\cos a}{2}\right)\geq\frac{1}{2}\cos^{2}a

For the measure
|\bar{E}_{1}|
of the set
\bar{E}_{1}=\{x\mid r_{1}+\delta\leq x\leq r_{2}-\delta\}
, note that
\cos a\leq\frac{\pi}{2}-a\leq\frac{\pi}{2}\cos a
,

0\leq|\bar{E}_{1}|=(r_{2}-r_{1})-2\delta=(\pi-2a)-2\cos a\leq(\pi-2)\cos a

Regarding the differences of values of
\sin
, letting
r(a):=\tfrac{\pi}{2}-a
for brevity, then by Taylor’s theorem

\displaystyle\sin(a+r(a)t)-\sin a

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cos a\cdot r(a)t-\frac{\sin\theta}{2}r^{2}(a)t^{2}\quad t\in[0,1],\theta\in[a,a+r(a)]=\left[a,\frac{\pi}{2}\right]

\displaystyle\sin a-\sin(a-r(a)t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cos a\cdot r(a)t+\frac{\sin\theta^{\prime}}{2}r^{2}(a)t^{2}\quad t\in[0,1],\theta^{\prime}\in[a-r(a),a]=\left[2a-\frac{\pi}{2},a\right]

By
0\leq t^{2}\leq t\leq 1
,
0\leq\sin a\leq\sin\theta\leq 1,\cos a\leq r(a)\leq\frac{\pi}{2}\cos a
and
1-2\cos^{2}a=\sin\left(2a-\frac{\pi}{2}\right)\leq\sin\theta^{\prime}\leq\sin a
, note
\sin
is
concave on
\left[a,\frac{\pi}{2}\right]
, and
1+\frac{1-2\cos^{2}a}{2}\cdot\frac{r(a)}{\cos a}t\geq 1-\frac{\pi}{4}
, then

\displaystyle\frac{\cos^{2}a}{2}t=\left(\frac{1-\sin^{2}a}{2}\right)t\leq(1-\sin a)t\leq

\displaystyle\sin(a+r(a)t)-\sin a

\displaystyle\leq\cos a\cdot r(a)t

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)\cos a\cdot r(a)t\leq\left(1+\frac{1-2\cos^{2}a}{2}\cdot\frac{r(a)}{\cos a}t\right)\cos a\cdot r(a)t\leq

\displaystyle\sin a-\sin(a-r(a)t)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\cos a\cdot r(a)+\frac{r^{2}(a)}{2}\right)t

By applying
\cos a\leq r(a)\leq\frac{\pi}{2}\cos a
again, thus

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}\cos a\cdot r(a)t\leq

\displaystyle\sin(a+r(a)t)-\sin a

\displaystyle\leq\cos a\cdot r(a)t

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)\cos a\cdot r(a)t\leq

\displaystyle\sin a-\sin(a-r(a)t)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{\pi}{4}\right)\cos a\cdot r(a)t

Namely, for
\forall u\in[0,r(a)]=\left[0,\tfrac{\pi}{2}-a\right]
,

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)\cos a\cdot u\leq\frac{1}{\pi}\cos a\cdot u\leq

\displaystyle\sin(a+u)-\sin a

\displaystyle\leq\cos a\cdot u\leq\left(1+\frac{\pi}{4}\right)\cos a\cdot u

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)\cos a\cdot u\leq

\displaystyle\sin a-\sin(a-u)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{\pi}{4}\right)\cos a\cdot u

(1)
For the integral
\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{b^{2}|\sin x+\sin a|\mathrm{d}x}{(b+|\sin x+\sin a|)^{2}}

In the region
E=E_{1}\cup E_{2}
near the roots, by using
\frac{1}{2}\cos a\cdot|u|\leq|\sin(r_{i}+u)+\sin a|\leq\frac{3}{2}\cos a\cdot|u|
for
|u|<\delta
,

b^{2}\int_{|u|<\delta}\frac{\frac{1}{2}\cos a|u|\mathrm{d}u}{\left(b+\frac{3}{2}\cos a|u|\right)^{2}}\leq\int_{E_{i}}\frac{b^{2}|\sin x+\sin a|\mathrm{d}x}{(b+|\sin x+\sin a|)^{2}}\leq b^{2}\int_{|u|<\delta}\frac{\frac{3}{2}\cos a|u|\mathrm{d}u}{\left(b+\frac{1}{2}\cos a|u|\right)^{2}}\quad\forall i\in\{1,2\}

Let
t=\frac{u}{\delta}=\frac{u}{\cos a}
, and note that
\int_{E}=\int_{E_{1}}+\int_{E_{2}}
, then
