2\times\frac{\frac{1}{2}}{\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)^{2}}\times\frac{b^{2}}{\cos a}\int^{1}_{0}\frac{t\mathrm{d}t}{\left(\frac{\frac{2}{3}b}{\cos^{2}a}+t\right)^{2}}\leq\int_{E}\frac{b^{2}|\sin x+\sin a|\mathrm{d}x}{(b+|\sin x+\sin a|)^{2}}\leq 2\times\frac{\frac{3}{2}}{\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}}\times\frac{b^{2}}{\cos a}\int^{1}_{0}\frac{t\mathrm{d}t}{\left(\frac{2b}{\cos^{2}a}+t\right)^{2}}

Note that
\int_{0}^{1}\frac{t\mathrm{d}t}{(C+t)^{2}}=\ln(1+C^{-1})-\frac{1}{1+C}=\Theta(\log C^{-1})
for
0<C\lesssim 1
, and under the
assumption
0<b\lesssim\cos^{2}a
,

\int_{E}\frac{b^{2}|\sin x+\sin a|\mathrm{d}x}{(b+|\sin x+\sin a|)^{2}}=\Theta\left(\frac{b^{2}}{\cos a}\log\left(\frac{\cos^{2}a}{b}\right)\right)

In the region
\bar{E}=\bar{E}_{1}\cup\bar{E}_{2}
away from the roots, note
that
0\leq|\bar{E}_{1}|\leq(\pi-2)\cos a
,
|\sin x+\sin a|\geq\frac{\cos^{2}a}{2},\forall x\in\bar{E}
,

0\leq\int_{\bar{E}_{1}}\frac{b^{2}|\sin x+\sin a|\mathrm{d}x}{(b+|\sin x+\sin a|)^{2}}\leq|\bar{E}_{1}|\times\sup_{x\in\bar{E}_{1}}\frac{b^{2}}{|\sin x+\sin a|}\leq(\pi-2)\cos a\times\frac{2b^{2}}{\cos^{2}a}=\Theta\left(\frac{b^{2}}{\cos a}\right)

Under the assumption
0<b\lesssim\cos^{2}a
, then
2|\sin x+\sin a|\geq\cos^{2}a\gtrsim b,\forall x\in\bar{E}
, and note
\sin x+\sin a>0,\forall x\in\bar{E}_{2}
,

\frac{1}{9}\cdot b^{2}\times\int_{-a+\delta}^{\pi+a-\delta}\frac{\mathrm{d}x}{\sin x+\sin a}\leq\int_{\bar{E}_{2}}\frac{b^{2}|\sin x+\sin a|\mathrm{d}x}{(b+|\sin x+\sin a|)^{2}}\leq b^{2}\times\int_{-a+\delta}^{\pi+a-\delta}\frac{\mathrm{d}x}{\sin x+\sin a}

Note that
\int_{-a+\delta}^{\pi+a-\delta}\frac{\mathrm{d}x}{\sin x+\sin a}=\frac{2}{\cos a}\ln\left(\frac{\cos\left(a-\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}{\sin\left(\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}\right)=\Theta\left(\frac{1}{\cos a}\right)
, since
\frac{\cos\left(a-\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}{\sin\left(\frac{\delta}{2}\right)}\in[1.8,3]
for
\delta=\cos a,0\leq a<\frac{\pi}{2}
,

\int_{\bar{E}_{2}}\frac{b^{2}|\sin x+\sin a|\mathrm{d}x}{(b+|\sin x+\sin a|)^{2}}=\Theta\left(\frac{b^{2}}{\cos a}\right)

Note that
\int_{\bar{E}}=\int_{\bar{E}_{1}}+\int_{\bar{E}_{2}}
, then

\int_{\bar{E}}\frac{b^{2}|\sin x+\sin a|\mathrm{d}x}{(b+|\sin x+\sin a|)^{2}}=\Theta\left(\frac{b^{2}}{\cos a}\right)

Combine these two regions
E
and
\bar{E}
,
[0,2\pi]=E\cup\bar{E}

\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{b^{2}|\sin x+\sin a|\mathrm{d}x}{(b+|\sin x+\sin a|)^{2}}=\Theta\left(\frac{b^{2}}{\cos a}\log\left(\frac{\cos^{2}a}{b}\right)\right)+\Theta\left(\frac{b^{2}}{\cos a}\right)=\Theta\left(\frac{b^{2}}{\cos a}\log\left(\frac{\cos^{2}a}{b}\right)\right)

(2)
For the integral
\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{b^{2}\operatorname{sgn}(\sin(x+a))|\cos x+\cos a|\mathrm{d}x}{(b+|\sin x+\sin a|)^{2}}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{b^{2}\operatorname{sgn}(\sin(x+a))|\cos x+\cos a|\mathrm{d}x}{(b+|\sin x+\sin a|)^{2}}=\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{b^{2}(-1)^{\bm{1}_{0\leq u\leq 2a}}(-\cos(a+u)+\cos a)\mathrm{d}x}{(b+|-\sin(a+u)+\sin a|)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{-\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}-a}\frac{b^{2}(-\cos(a+u)+\cos a)\mathrm{d}u}{(b+\sin(a+u)-\sin a)^{2}}+\int_{-\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-a\right)}^{0}\frac{b^{2}(-\cos(a+u)+\cos a)\mathrm{d}u}{(b-\sin(a+u)+\sin a)^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\{-\int_{\frac{\pi}{2}-a}^{2\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-a\right)}\frac{b^{2}(-\cos(a+u)+\cos a)\mathrm{d}u}{(b+\sin(a+u)-\sin a)^{2}}+\int_{2\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-a\right)}^{3\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-a\right)}\frac{b^{2}(-\cos(a+u)+\cos a)\mathrm{d}u}{(b-\sin(a+u)+\sin a)^{2}}\right\}+\int_{3\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-a\right)}^{\frac{3\pi}{2}+a}\frac{b^{2}(-\cos(a+u)+\cos a)\mathrm{d}u}{(b-\sin(a+u)+\sin a)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2b^{2}\cos a\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}-a}\left[-\frac{1}{(b+\sin(a+u)-\sin a)^{2}}+\frac{1}{(b-\sin(a-u)+\sin a)^{2}}\right]\mathrm{d}u+2b^{2}\cos a\int_{0}^{2a}\frac{\mathrm{d}u}{(b+\cos u+\sin a)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2b^{2}\cos a\times 2\sin a\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}-a}\frac{4\sin^{2}\frac{u}{2}(b+\cos a\sin u)}{(b+\sin(a+u)-\sin a)^{2}(b-\sin(a-u)+\sin a)^{2}}\mathrm{d}u+2b^{2}\cos a\int_{0}^{2a}\frac{\mathrm{d}u}{(b+\cos u+\sin a)^{2}}

Note that
\max\{\sin(a+u)-\sin a,\sin a-\sin(a-u)\}\leq\left(1+\frac{\pi}{4}\right)\cos a\cdot u
, and
\left(1-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)\cos a\cdot u\leq\max\{\sin(a+u)-\sin a,\sin a-\sin(a-u)\}
, for
r(a)=\frac{\pi}{2}-a
, we have
\cos a\leq r(a)\leq\frac{\pi}{2}\cos a
,
\frac{2}{\pi}u\leq\sin u\leq u,\frac{8}{\pi^{2}}u^{2}\leq 4\sin^{2}\frac{u}{2}\leq u^{2}
, then we can
establish such upper/lower bounds for the integral:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}-a}\frac{4\sin^{2}\frac{u}{2}(b+\cos a\sin u)}{(b+\sin(a+u)-\sin a)^{2}(b-\sin(a-u)+\sin a)^{2}}\mathrm{d}u\leq\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}-a}\frac{(b+\cos a\cdot u)u^{2}\mathrm{d}u}{\left(b+\left(1-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)\cos a\cdot u\right)^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\tfrac{\pi}{4}}\int^{r(a)}_{0}\frac{u^{2}\mathrm{d}u}{\left(b+\left(1-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)\cos a\cdot u\right)^{3}}=\frac{1}{1-\frac{\pi}{4}}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{t^{2}\mathrm{d}t}{\left(\frac{b}{r(a)}+\left(1-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)\cos a\cdot t\right)^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left(1-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)^{4}}\times\frac{1}{\cos^{3}a}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{t^{2}\mathrm{d}t}{\left(\frac{b/\cos^{2}a}{\left(1-\frac{\pi}{4}\right)\cdot\frac{\pi}{2}}+t\right)^{3}}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}-a}\frac{4\sin^{2}\frac{u}{2}(b+\cos a\sin u)}{(b+\sin(a+u)-\sin a)^{2}(b-\sin(a-u)+\sin a)^{2}}\mathrm{d}u\geq\frac{8}{\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}-a}\frac{\left(b+\frac{2}{\pi}\cos a\cdot u\right)u^{2}\mathrm{d}u}{\left(b+\left(1+\frac{\pi}{4}\right)\cos a\cdot u\right)^{4}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{16}{\pi^{3}\left(1+\frac{\pi}{4}\right)}\int^{r(a)}_{0}\frac{u^{2}\mathrm{d}u}{\left(b+\left(1+\frac{\pi}{4}\right)\cos a\cdot u\right)^{3}}=\frac{16}{\pi^{3}\left(1+\frac{\pi}{4}\right)}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{t^{2}\mathrm{d}t}{\left(\frac{b}{r(a)}+\left(1+\frac{\pi}{4}\right)\cos a\cdot t\right)^{3}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{16}{\pi^{3}\left(1+\frac{\pi}{4}\right)^{4}}\times\frac{1}{\cos^{3}a}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{t^{2}\mathrm{d}t}{\left(\frac{b/\cos^{2}a}{1+\frac{\pi}{4}}+t\right)^{3}}

Note
\int_{0}^{1}\frac{t^{2}\mathrm{d}t}{(C+t)^{3}}=\ln(1+C^{-1})-\frac{3+2C}{2(1+C)^{2}}=\Theta(\log C^{-1})
for
0<C\lesssim 1
, and under the
assumption
0<b\lesssim\cos^{2}a
,

2b^{2}\cos a\times 2\sin a\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}-a}\frac{4\sin^{2}\frac{u}{2}(b+\cos a\sin u)}{(b+\sin(a+u)-\sin a)^{2}(b-\sin(a-u)+\sin a)^{2}}\mathrm{d}u=\Theta\left(\tan a\cdot\frac{b^{2}}{\cos a}\log\left(\frac{\cos^{2}a}{b}\right)\right)

For the other integral, note that

\displaystyle\cos u-\cos 2a

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\min\left\{\frac{1-\cos 2a}{2a},\sin 2a\right\}(2a-u)=\min\left\{\frac{\sin a}{a},2\cos a\right\}\sin a\cdot(2a-u)\geq\cos a\cdot\sin a\cdot(2a-u)

\displaystyle\cos u+\sin a

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1+2\sin a}{1+\sin a}\cos^{2}a+2a\sin a\times\cos a\cdot\left(1-\frac{u}{2a}\right)\geq\cos^{2}a+2\sin^{2}a\times\cos a\cdot\left(1-\frac{u}{2a}\right)

By susbstitution
t=1-\frac{u}{2a}\in[0,1]
, and
a\leq\frac{\pi}{2}\sin a
for
a\in\left[0,\tfrac{\pi}{2}\right]

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2a}\frac{\mathrm{d}u}{(b+\cos u+\sin a)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2a\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{(\cos^{2}a+2\sin^{2}a\times\cos a\cdot t)^{2}}=\frac{2a}{\cos^{4}a}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{\left(1+\frac{2\sin^{2}a}{\cos a}t\right)^{2}}=\frac{2a}{\cos^{4}a}\cdot\frac{1}{1+\frac{2\sin^{2}a}{\cos a}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2a}{\cos^{3}a}\cdot\frac{1}{1+(1-\cos a)(1+2\cos a)}\leq\frac{2a}{\cos^{3}a}\leq\pi\times\tan a\cdot\frac{1}{\cos^{2}a}

Thus, we have

2b^{2}\cos a\int_{0}^{2a}\frac{\mathrm{d}u}{(b+\cos u+\sin a)^{2}}=\mathcal{O}\left(\tan a\cdot\frac{b^{2}}{\cos a}\right)

Combine these two integrals

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{b^{2}\operatorname{sgn}(\sin(x+a))|\cos x+\cos a|\mathrm{d}x}{(b+|\sin x+\sin a|)^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Theta\left(\tan a\cdot\frac{b^{2}}{\cos a}\log\left(\frac{\cos^{2}a}{b}\right)\right)-\mathcal{O}\left(\tan a\cdot\frac{b^{2}}{\cos a}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\tan a\cdot\frac{b^{2}}{\cos a}\log\left(\frac{\cos^{2}a}{b}\right)\right)

∎

E-B

Identities and Inequalities for Useful Expectations

Lemma E.2
.

Suppose standard Gaussian variables
g,g^{\prime}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
have the correlation coefficient
\mathbb{E}[gg^{\prime}]=\sin\varphi
with
\varphi\in[-\frac{\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}]
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\textnormal{sgn}(gg^{\prime})]=\frac{2}{\pi}\varphi,\quad\mathbb{E}[|gg^{\prime}|]=\frac{2}{\pi}[\varphi\sin\varphi+\cos\varphi],\quad\mathbb{E}[g^{2}\textnormal{sgn}(gg^{\prime})]=\frac{2}{\pi}[\varphi+\sin\varphi\cos\varphi].

Proof.

Let’s prove the first identity (Grothendieck’s identity) at the beginning.
Since we can express
g,g^{\prime}
in terms of two independent variables
R,U

with
R\sim r\exp(-\frac{r^{2}}{2})\mathbb{I}_{r\geq 0}
(standard Rayleigh distribution with
\mathbb{E}[R^{2}]=2
)
and
U\sim\mathrm{Unif}[0,4\pi)
,

g=R\cos((U-\varphi)/2),\quad g^{\prime}=R\sin((U+\varphi)/2)

Noting that
\textnormal{sgn}(gg^{\prime})=\textnormal{sgn}(\sin(U)+\sin\varphi)
for
R\neq 0
, we have

\mathbb{E}[\textnormal{sgn}(gg^{\prime})]=\mathbb{E}[\textnormal{sgn}(\sin(U)+\sin\varphi)]=\frac{(+1)\cdot(2\pi+4\varphi)+(-1)\cdot(2\pi-4\varphi)}{4\pi}=\frac{2}{\pi}\varphi

We start the proof of the second identity by applying Price’s theorem (see
[
63
]
),

\frac{1}{\cos\varphi}\cdot\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{E}[|gg^{\prime}|]}{\mathrm{d}\varphi}=\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{E}[|gg^{\prime}|]}{\mathrm{d}\sin\varphi}=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}|gg^{\prime}|}{\partial g\partial g^{\prime}}\right]=\mathbb{E}[\textnormal{sgn}(gg^{\prime})]=\frac{2}{\pi}\varphi

Note that when
\varphi=-\frac{\pi}{2}
,
\mathbb{E}[|gg^{\prime}|]_{\varphi=-\frac{\pi}{2}}=\mathbb{E}[g^{2}]=1
, we have

\mathbb{E}[|gg^{\prime}|]=\mathbb{E}[|gg^{\prime}|]_{\varphi=-\frac{\pi}{2}}+\frac{2}{\pi}\int^{\varphi}_{-\frac{\pi}{2}}\varphi^{\prime}\cos\varphi^{\prime}\mathrm{d}\varphi^{\prime}=\frac{2}{\pi}[\varphi\sin\varphi+\cos\varphi]

To prove the third identity, we start by expressing
g^{\prime}=\sin\varphi\cdot g+\cos\varphi\cdot h
for some
h\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
with
h\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}g
,
and applying Stein’s lemma (see Lemma 2.1 of
[
62
]
),
and noting that
\frac{\partial}{\partial g}\left\{|g|\textnormal{sgn}(\sin\varphi\cdot g+\cos\varphi\cdot h)\right\}=\textnormal{sgn}(gg^{\prime})+2\sin\varphi\cdot|g|\delta(g^{\prime})
,

\mathbb{E}[g^{2}\textnormal{sgn}(gg^{\prime})]=\mathbb{E}_{h}\mathbb{E}_{g}[g\cdot|g|\textnormal{sgn}(\sin\varphi\cdot g+\cos\varphi\cdot h)]=\mathbb{E}_{h}\mathbb{E}_{g}\left[\frac{\partial|g|\textnormal{sgn}(\sin\varphi\cdot g+\cos\varphi\cdot h)}{\partial g}\right]=\mathbb{E}[\textnormal{sgn}(gg^{\prime})]+2\sin\varphi\mathbb{E}[|g|\delta(g^{\prime})]

where the former term is the first identity
\mathbb{E}[\textnormal{sgn}(gg^{\prime})]=\frac{2}{\pi}\varphi
, and the latter term can be evaluated by the symmetricity of
g,g^{\prime}
,

\mathbb{E}[|g|\delta(g^{\prime})]=\mathbb{E}[|g^{\prime}|\delta(g)]=\mathbb{E}_{h}\mathbb{E}_{g}[|\sin\varphi\cdot g+\cos\varphi\cdot h|\delta(g)]=\mathbb{E}_{h}\mathbb{E}_{g}[|\cos\varphi\cdot h|\delta(g)]=\cos\varphi\mathbb{E}_{h}[|h|]\mathbb{E}_{g}[\delta(g)]=\frac{1}{\pi}\cos\varphi

Thus, by combining these two terms, we obtain the third identity, and the proof is complete.
∎

Lemma E.3
.

Suppose a random variable
X
has a density
X\sim f_{X}(x)=K_{0}(|x|)/\pi
involving modified Bessel function
K_{0}
of the second kind of order
0
, then:

\mathbb{E}[|X|]=\frac{2}{\pi},\quad\mathbb{E}[X^{2n}]=[(2n-1)!!]^{2}=[(2n-1)\times\cdots\times 3\times 1]^{2}\quad\forall n\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}

Proof.

Since the product
X=gg^{\prime}
of two independent standard Gaussian variables
g,g^{\prime}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1),\mathbb{E}[gg^{\prime}]=0
has a density
X\sim f_{X}(x)=K_{0}(|x|)/\pi
involving modified Bessel function
K_{0}
of the second kind of order
0

(see page 50, Section 4.4 Bessel Function Distributions, Chapter 12 Continuous Distributions (General) of
[
49
]
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|X|]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|gg^{\prime}|]=\mathbb{E}[|g|]\mathbb{E}[|g^{\prime}|]=\mathbb{E}[|g|]^{2}=\frac{2}{\pi}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X^{2n}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(gg^{\prime})^{2n}]=\mathbb{E}[g^{2n}]\mathbb{E}[g^{\prime 2n}]=\mathbb{E}[g^{2n}]^{2}=[(2n-1)!!]^{2}

due to the well-known results that
\mathbb{E}[g^{2n}]=(2n-1)!!,\mathbb{E}[|g|]=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}
for
g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)

(see equations (13.11), (13.14) on page 89 ang page 91, Section 3 moments and other properties, Chapter 13 Normal Distributions of
[
49
]
).
∎

Lemma E.4
.

Suppose
A>0
and a random variable
X
has a density
X\sim f_{X}(x)=K_{0}(|x|)/\pi
involving modified Bessel function
K_{0}
of the second kind of order
0
, then:

\frac{\sqrt{12A^{2}+1}-1}{6A}<\mathbb{E}[\tanh(AX)X]<\frac{\sqrt{4A^{2}+1}-1}{2A}

Proof.

Let
f(A):=\mathbb{E}[\tanh(AX)X]
, and
\underline{B}(A):=\frac{\sqrt{12A^{2}+1}-1}{6A},\overline{B}(A):=\frac{\sqrt{4A^{2}+1}-1}{2A}
.
For
A>0
, we have:

0<f(A)<\mathbb{E}[|X|]=\frac{2}{\pi},\quad 0<\underline{B}(A)<\lim_{A\to\infty}\underline{B}(A)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}},\quad 0<\overline{B}(A)<\lim_{A\to\infty}\overline{B}(A)=1

And
f(A),\underline{B}(A),\overline{B}(A)
are all increasing and concave with respect to
A>0
, due to the fact that:

f^{\prime}(A)=\mathbb{E}[(1-\tanh^{2}(AX))X^{2}]>0,f^{\prime\prime}(A)=-2\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\tanh(AX)X^{3}}{\cosh^{2}(AX)}\right]<0;\quad\underline{B}^{\prime}(A)>0,\underline{B}^{\prime\prime}(A)<0;\overline{B}^{\prime}(A)>0,\overline{B}^{\prime\prime}(A)<0

By Taylor expansions of
\tanh(t)=t-\frac{t^{3}}{3}+\frac{2t^{5}}{15}+\cdots
and
\underline{B}(A),\overline{B}(A)
and using
\mathbb{E}[X^{2n}]=[(2n-1)!!]^{2}
in Lemma
E.3
:

\displaystyle A-3A^{3}<f(A)<A-3A^{3}+30A^{5},\quad A-3A^{3}<\underline{B}(A)<A-3A^{3}+18A^{5},\quad A-A^{3}<\overline{B}(A)<A-A^{3}+2A^{5}

When
0<A\leq 1/4
is small enough, we have shown:

f(A)<A-3A^{3}+30A^{5}=A-A^{3}-2A^{3}(1-15A^{2})<A-A^{3}<\overline{B}(A)

Similarly, we show
\underline{B}(A)<f(A)
when
A
is sufficiently small by comparing the series approximations of
f(A),\underline{B}(A)
at
A\to 0_{+}
,
and we extent it to the range of
0<A\leq 1/4
by seprating
\underline{B}(A)
and
f(A)
with piecewise linear functions
based on the numerical evaluations of
f(A),f^{\prime}(A),\underline{B}(A),\underline{B}^{\prime}(A)
at finite points and noting
f(A),\underline{B}(A)
are concave.

Let’s show the inequalities
\underline{B}(A)<f(A)
and
f(A)<\overline{B}(A)
for
1/4\leq A\leq 5/4
by numerical evaluations at some points
A_{1}=\frac{1}{4},A_{2}=\frac{1}{3},A_{3}=\frac{1}{2},A_{4}=\frac{3}{4},A_{5}=\frac{5}{4}
.

\displaystyle\underline{B}(A)\leq\underline{B}(A_{i})+\underline{B}^{\prime}(A_{i})(A-A_{i})<f(A_{i})+\frac{f(A_{i+1})-f(A_{i})}{A_{i+1}-A_{i}}(A-A_{i})\leq f(A)

\displaystyle f(A)\leq f(A_{i})+f^{\prime}(A_{i})(A-A_{i})<\overline{B}(A_{i})+\frac{\overline{B}(A_{i+1})-\overline{B}(A_{i})}{A_{i+1}-A_{i}}(A-A_{i})\leq\overline{B}(A)\quad A\in[A_{i},A_{i+1}],i\in\{1,2,3,4\}

The above inequalities hold due to that
f(A),\underline{B}(A),\overline{B}(A)
are concave and the numerical evaluations show

\underline{B}(A_{i})<f(A_{i})<\overline{B}(A_{i}),\underline{B}(A_{i})+\underline{B}^{\prime}(A_{i})(A_{i+1}-A_{i})<f(A_{i+1}),f(A_{i})+f^{\prime}(A_{i})(A_{i+1}-A_{i})<\overline{B}(A_{i+1})
for
i\in\{1,2,3,4\}
.
Therefore, we have shown
\underline{B}(A)<f(A)<\overline{B}(A)
for
0<A\leq 5/4
by combining these two cases.
For the case of
A>5/4
, we have shown:

\displaystyle\underline{B}(A)<\underline{B}(2.33)\approx 0.5102<0.5111\approx f(5/4)<f(A)\quad\forall A\in(5/4,2.33)

\displaystyle\underline{B}(A)<\lim_{A\to\infty}\underline{B}(A)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\approx 0.5774<0.5776\approx f(2.33)\leq f(A)\quad\forall A\in[2.33,\infty)

\displaystyle f(A)\leq\lim_{A\to\infty}f(A)=\mathbb{E}[|X|]=\frac{2}{\pi}\approx 0.6366<0.6770\approx\overline{B}(5/4)<\overline{B}(A)\quad\forall A\in(5/4,\infty)

by the fact that
f(A),\underline{B}(A),\overline{B}(A)
are increasing and
\mathbb{E}[|X|]=\frac{2}{\pi}
in Lemma
E.3
, then the proof is complete.
∎

E-C

Limit Behavior and Non-Increasing Property of Some Helper Functions

Lemma E.5
.

Suppose these three conditions for function
F(A,k):\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}
hold:

1.

F(A,k)
is continuous in
k
for any
A>0
, its unique root
k=k^{\ast}(A)
of
F(A,k)=0
is a interior point of the compact interval
I:=[a,b],0<a<b<\infty
.

2.

F(A,k)
converges uniformly to
F_{A_{0}}(k):=\lim_{A\to A_{0}}F(A,k)
on compact interval
I=[a,b]
as
A\to A_{0}
, where
A_{0}
is a positive number or
0_{+},\infty
.

3.

The unique root
k=k_{A_{0}}^{\ast}
of
F_{A_{0}}(k)=0
is a interior point of the compact interval
I=[a,b]
;
the derivative
\frac{\mathrm{d}F_{A_{0}}(k)}{\mathrm{d}k}\mid_{k=k_{A_{0}}^{\ast}}\neq 0
, and
\frac{\mathrm{d}F_{A_{0}}(k)}{\mathrm{d}k}
is continuous in
k
on
I=[a,b]
.

Then, we have:

\lim_{A\to A_{0}}k^{\ast}(A)=k_{A_{0}}^{\ast}.

Proof.

To prove that
\lim_{A\to A_{0}}k^{\ast}(A)=k_{A_{0}}^{\ast}
, we must show that for any
\varepsilon>0
,
if
A_{0}
is finite (
A_{0}
is a positive number or
0_{+}
), there exists a
\delta>0
such that
|k^{\ast}(A)-k_{A_{0}}^{\ast}|<\varepsilon
for all
A>0
with
|A-A_{0}|<\delta
;
and if
A_{0}=\infty
, there exists an
N>0
such that
|k^{\ast}(A)-k_{A_{0}}^{\ast}|<\varepsilon
for all
A>N
.

By the condition 3, for the root
k=k_{A_{0}}^{\ast}\in(a,b)
of
F_{A_{0}}(k)=0
, there exists a
0<\delta_{A_{0}}<\min(\varepsilon,k_{A_{0}}^{\ast}-a,b-k_{A_{0}}^{\ast})
such that

F_{A_{0}}(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}+\Delta k)\cdot F_{A_{0}}(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}-\Delta k)=(F_{A_{0}}(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}+\Delta k)-F_{A_{0}}(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}))(F_{A_{0}}(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}-\Delta k)-F_{A_{0}}(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}))<0

for any
\Delta k\in(0,\delta_{A_{0}})
. Let’s select
\Delta k=\delta_{A_{0}}/2
, then we have:

F_{A_{0}}\left(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}+\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2}\right)\cdot F_{A_{0}}\left(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}-\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2}\right)<0.

Without loss of generality, we may assume
F_{A_{0}}\left(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}+\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2}\right)>0,F_{A_{0}}\left(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}-\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2}\right)<0
, and select:

m_{A_{0}}:=\min\left(\left|F_{A_{0}}\left(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}+\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2}\right)\right|,\left|F_{A_{0}}\left(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}-\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2}\right)\right|\right)>0

By the condition 2, since
k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}+\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2},k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}-\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2}\in[a,b]=I
,
there exists a
\delta>0
for all
A>0
with
|A-A_{0}|<\delta
if
A_{0}
is finite; or there exists an
N>0
for all
A>N
if
A_{0}=\infty
such that:

\max\left(\left|F(A,k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}+\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2})-F_{A_{0}}(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}+\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2})\right|,\left|F(A,k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}-\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2})-F_{A_{0}}(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}-\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2})\right|\right)\leq\sup_{k\in I}|F_{A_{0}}(k)-F_{A_{0}}(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}})|<\frac{m_{A_{0}}}{2}.

Therefore, the signs of function values will not change as long as
A
is close to
A_{0}
.

\displaystyle F(A,k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}+\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle F_{A_{0}}(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}+\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2})-\left|F(A,k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}+\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2})-F_{A_{0}}(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}+\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2})\right|>m_{A_{0}}-\frac{m_{A_{0}}}{2}=\frac{m_{A_{0}}}{2}>0

\displaystyle F(A,k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}-\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle F_{A_{0}}(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}-\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2})+\left|F(A,k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}-\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2})-F_{A_{0}}(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}-\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2})\right|<-m_{A_{0}}+\frac{m_{A_{0}}}{2}=-\frac{m_{A_{0}}}{2}<0

By condition 1, the application of the intermediate value theorem, under the condition that
F(A,k)
is continuous in
k
for any
A>0
and the uniqueness of the root
k=k^{\ast}(A)
of
F(A,k)=0
, ensures that:

k^{\ast}(A)\in\left(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}-\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2},k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}+\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2}\right)\subset(a,b)\subset I

Then, we have:

|k^{\ast}(A)-k_{A_{0}}^{\ast}|<\max\left(\left|(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}-\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2})-k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}\right|,\left|(k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}+\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2})-k^{\ast}_{A_{0}}\right|\right)=\frac{\delta_{A_{0}}}{2}<\min(\varepsilon,k_{A_{0}}^{\ast}-a,b-k_{A_{0}}^{\ast})/2<\varepsilon

for all
A>0
with
|A-A_{0}|<\delta
if
A_{0}
is finite (
A_{0}
is a positive number or
0_{+}
); or for all
A>N
if
A_{0}=\infty
.
Therefore, we have shown that
\lim_{A\to A_{0}}k^{\ast}(A)=k_{A_{0}}^{\ast}
, and the proof is complete.
∎

Lemma E.6
.

Suppose
s\in[0,1]
and
\delta\geq 0
, then the function
H(s,\delta)
is non-decreasing with respect to
s\in[0,1]
given fixed
\delta\geq 0
, and
H(s,\delta)
is defined as:

H(s,\delta)=\left(s\arcsin\frac{s}{\sqrt{1+\delta}}+\sqrt{1-s^{2}+\delta}\right)^{2}+\frac{(1+\delta)(1-s^{2})}{1-s^{2}+\delta}

Proof.

When
\delta=0
, we have
H(s,0)=(s\arcsin s+\sqrt{1-s^{2}})^{2}
, which is non-decreasing with respect to
s\in[0,1]
,
due to the fact that
\partial_{s}H(s,0)=2(s\arcsin s+\sqrt{1-s^{2}})\cdot\arcsin s>0,\forall s\in(0,1)
.
When
\delta>0
, we introduce following notations to show
\partial_{s}H(s,\delta)>0,\forall s\in(0,1)
:

r:=1-s^{2},y:=\arcsin\frac{s}{\sqrt{1+\delta}},q:=\sqrt{1-s^{2}+\delta},K(s):=sy+q

Then we have
H(s,\delta)=K(s)^{2}+\frac{(1+\delta)r}{r+\delta}
and

y^{\prime}(s)=1/q,q^{\prime}=-s/q,K^{\prime}(s)=y\implies\partial_{s}H(s,\delta)=2K(s)K^{\prime}(s)+\partial_{s}\left(\frac{(1+\delta)r}{r+\delta}\right)=2\left[K(s)y-\frac{s(1+\delta)\delta}{q^{4}}\right]

By susbstitution
s=\sqrt{1+\delta}\sin y,q=\sqrt{1+\delta}\cos y,K(s)=\sqrt{1+\delta}(y\sin y+\cos y)
for some
y\in(0,1)
, we have

K(s)y-\frac{s(1+\delta)\delta}{q^{4}}=\frac{\sin y}{\sqrt{1+\delta}\cos^{4}y}\Phi(y)\geq\frac{\sin y}{\sqrt{1+\delta}\cos^{4}y}\Phi(0_{+})>0

where the last inequality is due to the fact that
\Phi(y)
is non-decreasing with respect to
y\in(0,\frac{\pi}{2})
given fixed
\delta
, and
\Phi(0_{+})=1
, and
\Phi(s)
is defined as:

\Phi(y)=(1+\delta)y(y+\cot y)\cos^{4}y-\delta\implies\partial_{y}\Phi(y)=\frac{(1+\delta)\cos^{3}y(1+4y\sin y)}{2\sin^{2}y}\cos(2y)(\tanh(2y)-2y)\geq 0

Therefore,
\partial_{s}H(s,\delta)>0,\forall s\in(0,1)
and
H(s,\delta)
is non-decreasing with respect to
s\in[0,1]
given fixed
\delta\geq 0
.
∎

E-D

Probability Concentration Inequality for Bernoulli Random Variables

Lemma E.7
.

Let
q:=\max(p,1-p)
for
V_{i}\overset{\operatorname{iid}}{\sim}\operatorname{Bern}(p),\forall i\in[n]:=\{1,2,\cdots,n\}
, then

for
t\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(V_{i}-\mathbb{E}[V_{i}])\right|\geq t\right)\leq 2\exp(-2nt^{2})

for
t\in[\mathrm{e}(1-q),q]\neq\varnothing

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(V_{i}-\mathbb{E}[V_{i}])\right|\geq t\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-n\left\{\frac{t}{q}\left[\ln\frac{t}{(1-q)}-1\right]+\frac{t^{2}}{2q^{2}}\right\}\right)

for
t\in(q,\infty)
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(V_{i}-\mathbb{E}[V_{i}])\right|\geq t\right)=0
.
Moreover, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(V_{i}-\mathbb{E}[V_{i}])\right|\lesssim\min\left(\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}/n}{\log\left(1+\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}/n}{1-q}\right)},\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{n}}\right)

Proof.

Let’s denote
q:=\max(p,1-p),V^{\prime}:=V_{i}-\mathbb{E}[V_{i}],i\in[n]
, thus
\mathbb{E}\left[{V^{\prime}}^{2}\right]=\operatorname{Var}[V_{i}]=p(1-p)=q(1-q),|V^{\prime}|\leq q
.With Chenorff bound and let
\psi(\lambda):=\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda(V_{i}-\mathbb{E}[V_{i}]))]=-\lambda p+\ln(1+p(\exp(\lambda)-1))\leq\frac{\lambda^{2}}{8}

\displaystyle\log\mathbb{P}[\sum_{i\in[n]}(V_{i}-\mathbb{E}[V_{i}])\geq nt]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\inf_{\lambda>0}\left\{\log\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\sum_{i\in[n]}(V_{i}-\mathbb{E}[V_{i}])\right)\right]-\lambda nt\right\}\leq\inf_{\lambda>0}\left\{n\left[\frac{\lambda^{2}}{8}-\lambda t\right]\right\}=-2nt^{2}

Hence, one side of the first probability inequality is proved, the other side is proved by replacing
V_{i}
with
1-V_{i}
.

Let’s focus on next concentration inequality. We begin with bounding
\psi(\lambda)
, note that
2\sinh(x)>\exp(x)-x-1,\forall x>0
.

\displaystyle\psi(\lambda)=1+\sum_{k\geq 2}\frac{\mathbb{E}[{V^{\prime}}^{k}]}{k!}\lambda^{k}\leq 1+\sum_{k\geq 2}\frac{\mathbb{E}[{V^{\prime}}^{2}]\cdot q^{k-2}}{k!}\lambda^{k}\leq 1+\frac{(1-q)}{q}\{\exp(q\lambda)-q\lambda-1\}\leq 1+2\frac{(1-q)}{q}\sinh(q\lambda)

Let
\mu:=2\frac{(1-q)}{q}\sinh(q\lambda)
, then
\lambda=\frac{1}{q}\operatorname{arcsinh}\left(\frac{q}{2(1-q)}\mu\right)
,
and
\mu^{\prime}:=\frac{q}{(1-q)}\mu
,
\gamma:=\frac{(1-q)}{q}\in(0,1],\tau:=\frac{t}{(1-q)}
.

\displaystyle\log\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(V_{i}-\mathbb{E}[V_{i}])\geq t\right)\leq n\inf_{\lambda>0}[\ln\psi(\lambda)-\lambda t]\leq n\inf_{\lambda>0}\left\{\ln\left[1+2\frac{(1-q)}{q}\sinh(q\lambda)\right]-\lambda t\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\gamma\inf_{\mu^{\prime}>0}\left\{\frac{1}{\gamma}\ln(1+\gamma\mu^{\prime})-\tau\operatorname{arcsinh}\left(\frac{\mu^{\prime}}{2}\right)\right\}\leq n\gamma\inf_{\mu^{\prime}>0}\left\{\frac{1}{\gamma}\ln(1+\gamma\mu^{\prime})-\tau\ln(\mu^{\prime})\right\}

If
t>q
, then
\tau:=\frac{t}{(1-q)}\in[\frac{1}{\gamma},\infty)
,

n\gamma\inf_{\mu^{\prime}>0}\left\{\frac{1}{\gamma}\ln(1+\gamma\mu^{\prime})-\tau\ln(\mu^{\prime})\right\}=-\infty
.
If
t\in[\mathrm{e}(1-q),q]
,
\tau:=\frac{t}{(1-q)}\in[\mathrm{e},\frac{1}{\gamma}]
.

\displaystyle n\gamma\inf_{\mu^{\prime}>0}\left\{\frac{1}{\gamma}\ln(1+\gamma\mu^{\prime})-\tau\log(\mu^{\prime})\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\gamma\left\{\frac{1}{\gamma}\ln(1+\gamma\mu^{\prime})-\tau\ln(\mu^{\prime})\right\}_{\mu^{\prime}=\frac{\tau}{1-\gamma\tau}}=n\gamma\left\{-\tau\ln\tau+\frac{1}{\gamma}[-(1-\gamma\tau)\ln(1-\gamma\tau)]\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle n\gamma\left\{-\tau\ln\tau+\frac{\gamma\tau}{\gamma}\left(1-\frac{\gamma\tau}{2}\right)\right\}\leq n\gamma\left\{-\tau[\ln\tau-1]-\frac{\gamma}{2}\tau^{2}\right\}=n\left\{-\frac{t}{q}\left[\ln\frac{t}{(1-q)}-1\right]-\frac{t^{2}}{2q^{2}}\right\}

Therefore, one side of the other probability inequality is proved, similarly, the other side is proved by replacing
V_{i}
with
1-V_{i}
.
Also, we show the probability is 0 when
t\geq q
.

By the first concentration inequality, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(V_{i}-\mathbb{E}[V_{i}])\right|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}}

By the second concentration inequality and letting
t=2\frac{\ln(2/\delta)}{n}/\ln(1+\frac{\ln(2/\delta)}{n\times\mathrm{e}(1-q)})\geq 2\mathrm{e}(1-q)
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}(V_{i}-\mathbb{E}[V_{i}])\right|\leq 2\frac{\ln(2/\delta)}{n}/\ln\left(1+\frac{\ln(2/\delta)}{n\times\mathrm{e}(1-q)}\right)\asymp\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}/n}{\log\left(1+\frac{\log\frac{1}{\delta}/n}{1-q}\right)}

Since the failure probability is less than
\delta
, by using
2x/\sqrt{1+x}>\ln(1+x)
for
x=\frac{\ln(2/\delta)}{n\times\mathrm{e}(1-q)}>0
,

2\exp\left(-nt\log\frac{t}{\mathrm{e}(1-q)}\right)_{t=2\frac{\ln(2/\delta)}{n}/\ln(1+\frac{\ln(2/\delta)}{n\times\mathrm{e}(1-q)})}<2\exp\left(-n\frac{\ln(2/\delta)}{n}\right)=\delta

The proof is completed by combining the bounds for
t\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and
t\in[\mathrm{e}(1-q),q]
.
∎

His research interests include machine learning, statistical learning, generative diffusion models,
and optimization for distributed and decentralized algorithms.

Abolfazl Hashemi

and the M.S.E. and Ph.D. degrees in electrical and computer engineering from the University of Texas at Austin, USA, in 2016 and 2020, respectively.

Since 2021, he has been an Assistant Professor with the Elmore Family School of Electrical and Computer Engineering, Purdue University.
His research interests include large-scale optimization. He was the 2019 Schmidt Science Fellows Award nominee from UT Austin, the recipient of the Iranian National Elite Foundation Fellowship, and the Best Student Paper Award finalist at the 2018 American Control Conference.
